# EVAL-037 — VIDEO JUDGMENT PACK

**Use this file only. Do not generate video.**

This pack contains **56 eligible video plans**:
- 54 primary plans = 6 treatments × 3 video briefs × 3 repetitions
- arranged into 18 blind A/B/C contests
- plus 2 diagnostic extras

## What each judge should do

### Stage 1 — choose the best repetition
Judge **V01–V18**. Each contest contains three plans for the same frozen brief and the same hidden treatment. Pick exactly one winner: A, B, or C.

Score each candidate /100:
- Customer objective / commercial effectiveness — 25
- Creative idea — 20
- Production executability with current AI video tools — 20
- Temporal / visual coherence — 15
- Generation robustness — 10
- Hard-constraint fidelity — 10

For each contest return:
- A: score, hard-constraint violations, biggest production risk
- B: score, hard-constraint violations, biggest production risk
- C: score, hard-constraint violations, biggest production risk
- ranking 1/2/3
- WINNER A/B/C
- confidence high/medium/low
- decisive reason, max 3 sentences

Do not reward prose quality. Do not mentally repair missing instructions. A material hard-constraint violation should not beat a compliant plan merely because it sounds exciting.

### Stage 2 — choose only 6 plans for production later
After Stage 1, compare the six winners for each brief and choose the **top 2 per brief**:

- B01 contests: V01, V04, V07, V10, V13, V16
- B04 contests: V02, V05, V08, V11, V14, V17
- B05 contests: V03, V06, V09, V12, V15, V18

Return:
- TOP 2 B01
- TOP 2 B04
- TOP 2 B05

Do not generate them.

### Diagnostic extras
D01 and D02 are outside the balanced 54. Score them independently with the same rubric. Mark OPTIONAL_DIAGNOSTIC_PRODUCTION only if score >=80 and there is no material hard-constraint violation.

## Frozen briefs

### B01
I am RentOK. Create a commercially strong vertical video ad for owners who run hostels and PGs in India. Managing rent collection, tenant records, KYC, move-outs, complaints and compliance through WhatsApp, notebooks or scattered spreadsheets creates leakages and missed follow-ups. Make that operational chaos painfully recognisable and then position RentOK as the clean system for managing the property. It should feel contemporary, credible and Indian, not like a generic Silicon Valley SaaS explainer. You may use only RentOK's official website, https://rentok.com, for company, product and brand information. Target duration: about 30 seconds. Aspect ratio: 9:16.

### B04
Create a 9:16 performance-oriented UGC video for a fictional Indian D2C skincare brand. The product is a fragrance-free 2% salicylic-acid facial serum for adults with oily, blemish-prone skin. One believable Indian woman should speak directly to camera, naturally demonstrate the product and communicate one clear purchase reason. It should feel like credible creator content rather than a polished television commercial, while remaining visually controlled and commercially usable. Do not invent clinical statistics, guarantees or medical claims. Target duration: about 25 seconds. No external website.

### B05
Create a short cinematic scene between two people sitting opposite each other at a café table. One person has discovered that the other concealed something important. The dialogue is tense but restrained; nobody shouts. The viewer must always understand where each person is in relation to the other. Use exactly six shots. Avoid elaborate camera movement. The scene should feel cinematic rather than mechanically covered. Target final duration: about 40 seconds. Aspect ratio: 16:9. No external website.

## Brief-specific hard checks

**B01:** 9:16; ~30s; hostel/PG operational pain understandable; problem → RentOK solution clear; credible Indian context; product/brand role clear; unsupported claims avoided.

**B04:** 9:16; ~25s; one believable Indian woman as creator/performance anchor; genuinely UGC-like; product/problem/usage story clear; no false clinical claims; performance realistically generatable.

**B05:** exactly six shots; two people seated opposite one another; clear spatial relationship; coherent eyelines/screen direction; restrained tension; dramatic progression; no elaborate camera movement.

## Blindness
Packages are labeled only A/B/C. Ignore any accidental stylistic clue about author/model. Judge only the plan.

## FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

### DELIVERABLE
A 30-second vertical video ad (9:16 aspect ratio) that viscerally shows the chaos of managing hostels/PGs through scattered tools, then reveals RentOK as the unified solution. Targeted at Indian property owners. Contemporary, credible tone—no Silicon Valley pastiche.

---

### OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
Position RentOK not as a feature-rich app, but as the antidote to daily operational friction (rent follow-ups, tenant records, KYC, complaints, move-outs). The pain is real and present for the target audience. The relief should feel earned and practical, not aspirational.

---

### CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
**"Chaos to Clarity in 30 Seconds"**

Open with rapid-fire scenes of an overwhelmed PG/hostel owner drowning in operational fragmentation—WhatsApp notifications piling up, scattered notebook pages, spreadsheet confusion, tenant complaints going unresolved. Then cut sharply to the same scenario resolved inside RentOK: one screen, organized, automatic, peaceful. The contrast is the message.

---

### MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. **Primary:** RentOK consolidates all the scattered chaos into one system.
2. **Secondary:** Specific pain points solved (rent collection, tenant records, KYC, complaints, move-outs, compliance).
3. **Tertiary:** It's built for Indian property owners; it's practical, not theoretical.
4. **Call-to-Action:** Simple—"Download RentOK" or "Get free demo."

---

### VISUAL_SYSTEM
- **Aesthetic:** Hand-held, documentary-real footage mixed with screen recordings of the app. Avoid overly polished or sterile UI showcases.
- **Color language:** Warm, lived-in early scenes (yellowed notebooks, cluttered desks, phone notifications); cool, organized digital clarity in the RentOK scenes.
- **Typography:** Simple, Indian-vernacular-friendly sans-serif. No heavy design flourishes.
- **On-screen elements:** Real WhatsApp conversations, notebook scribbles, spreadsheet rows—then the equivalent data organized in RentOK.

---

### PRODUCTION_RECIPE

**Duration: 30 seconds (9:16 vertical)**

**Structure:**
- Seconds 0–12: Chaos (pain identification)
- Seconds 12–24: Solution (RentOK clarity)
- Seconds 24–30: CTA + branding

**Shots in order:**

| Shot | Content | Duration | Purpose |
|------|---------|----------|---------|
| 1 | Close-up: Phone screen flooded with WhatsApp notifications—tenant messages mixed with personal texts, payment reminders, complaints stacked unanswered. | 2 sec | Establish immediate pain: information overload. |
| 2 | Wide: PG owner at desk, surrounded by notebooks, pen in hand, squinting at scribbled tenant details. | 2 sec | Tactile chaos—analog overwhelm. |
| 3 | Close-up: Spreadsheet on laptop, multiple sheets, data scattered, columns misaligned, rent amounts inconsistent. | 2 sec | Digital chaos—no coherent system. |
| 4 | Quick cuts (1 sec each, 3 total): Tenant lodging a complaint verbally, owner frantically searching notes, late rent payment missed. | 3 sec | Consequences of disorganization: compliance failure, revenue leak, tenant dissatisfaction. |
| 5 | Transition: Phone/laptop screen "dissolves" or "swipes" to RentOK app home screen. | 1 sec | Visual break—system change. |
| 6 | Screen recording: RentOK dashboard showing tenant list, occupancy status, rent collection status, auto-payments scheduled. | 2 sec | Solution: Everything in one place, organized. |
| 7 | Screen recording: Complaint section—one tap to log, assign, track resolution. | 1.5 sec | Solution: Complaints managed seamlessly. |
| 8 | Screen recording: Tenant KYC section with document verification, move-out checklist visible. | 1.5 sec | Solution: Compliance handled. |
| 9 | Screen recording: WhatsApp integration—automated reminders being sent, payment links auto-generated. | 1.5 sec | Solution: Automation replaces manual follow-up. |
| 10 | Wide: Owner at same desk, now calm, phone in hand, reading RentOK dashboard. Sunlight brighter. | 1.5 sec | Result: Calm, in control. |
| 11 | Text overlay + app icon + CTA: "RentOK. Rent management made simple. Get free demo." + buttons. | 2 sec | Close: branding + action. |

**Total: 30 seconds**

---

### GENERATION_PROMPTS

**SHOT 1 (WhatsApp chaos):**
"Extreme close-up of iPhone screen showing WhatsApp interface completely overwhelmed: 47 unread messages, mixed notifications from tenants asking for rent receipts, complaints about water supply, personal messages, payment reminders. Messages in Hindi and English. Screen brightness high. Shot in portrait mode. Realistic, authentic messaging app chaos."

**SHOT 2 (Desk overwhelm):**
"Wide shot of a PG owner's desk in a small office or room. Scattered: 3–4 handwritten notebooks with tenant names and rent amounts, loose papers with notes, a pen in hand, owner looking stressed/concentrated. Window light from side. Authentic Indian hostel/PG setting. Warm, lived-in atmosphere. Shot on smartphone camera."

**SHOT 3 (Spreadsheet mess):**
"Screen recording of a cluttered Microsoft Excel or Google Sheets file on a laptop. Multiple tabs visible ('Rent Tracker,' 'Complaints,' 'KYC'). Data misaligned, different date formats, tenant names inconsistent. Columns overflow. Owner's cursor clicking back and forth between sheets. Shot in 9:16 vertical cropping."

**SHOT 4a (Tenant complaint):**
"Medium shot: Young male tenant speaking to a PG owner, gesturing—clearly complaining. Indoor hostel room setting. Natural light. Authentic, no over-acting. 1 second."

**SHOT 4b (Owner searching notes):**
"Close-up: Owner flipping through pages of a notebook frantically, squinting, searching for tenant record. Slightly frustrated expression. 1 second."

**SHOT 4c (Missed deadline):**
"Screen of calendar or notepad showing red-circled 'Rent Due' date with 'LATE' written next to it. Or a WhatsApp message with payment overdue notification. 1 second."

**SHOT 5 (Transition):**
"Visual transition effect (subtle swipe, dissolve, or screen fade) from chaotic old tools (notebook, WhatsApp, spreadsheet) to a clean RentOK app interface. Smooth, fast, 1 second."

**SHOT 6 (RentOK dashboard):**
"Screen recording of RentOK app dashboard. Shows: Tenant list with green 'Paid' and red 'Pending' status badges. Occupancy overview graphic. Monthly rent collection summary. Navigation bar at bottom. Clean typography, organized layout. Colors: white background, teal/blue accents. 2 seconds of slow scrolling or static view."

**SHOT 7 (Complaint management):**
"Screen recording of RentOK Complaint Management section. Shows: One tap to 'Log Complaint,' dropdown to assign to staff, status showing 'In Progress → Resolved.' Chat-like interface for resolution updates. 1.5 seconds."

**SHOT 8 (KYC + Move-out):**
"Screen recording of Tenant Verification section and Move-out Checklist. Shows: Document upload, verification status (Approved/Pending), move-out form with fields for inventory check and security deposit deduction. Professional, hospital-like efficiency. 1.5 seconds."

**SHOT 9 (WhatsApp automation):**
"Screen recording of RentOK's WhatsApp Communication feature. Shows: Owner tapping 'Send Bulk Reminder' button, payment links auto-generated with tenant names, confirmation that messages are being sent from the owner's own WhatsApp number. Real WhatsApp interface with RentOK data integrated. 1.5 seconds."

**SHOT 10 (Calm owner):**
"Wide shot of the same owner at the same desk (from Shot 2). Now relaxed expression, phone in hand, light through window brighter/warmer. Subtle difference: fewer papers, phone shows RentOK on screen. No stress. 1.5 seconds."

**SHOT 11 (Branding + CTA):**
"Text overlay centered on screen: 'RentOK. Rent management made simple.' Below: RentOK app icon. Below that: Two buttons—'Download App' and 'Get Free Demo.' Subtitle: 'Save time. Work less. Earn more.' (tagline from website). Simple, clean sans-serif font. White background, teal text/buttons. 2 seconds."

### DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- **Real RentOK app screenshots** for Shots 6–9 (must be pulled directly from live app or provided by RentOK team).
- **Real WhatsApp interface mock-up** for Shot 1 (can be recreated with actual WhatsApp screenshots showing realistic message volume).
- **RentOK branding elements** for Shot 11: official logo, official tagline ("Save time. Work less. Earn more."), correct CTA copy.
- **Real hostel/PG environment** for Shots 2 and 10 (shoot in an actual PG or hostel office; authenticity critical).

---

### AUDIO_AND_EDIT

**Music:**
- Background track: Contemporary Indian instrumental (lo-fi hip-hop or acoustic Indian-fusion style). Starts soft under chaos, builds during transition, becomes clear and calm during solution reveal. Total duration: full 30 seconds.
- **Recommendation:** Royalty-free track like Indian instrumental lofi or Indie Bollywood-influenced composition (e.g., from Epidemic Sound or Artlist).

**Sound design:**
- Seconds 0–12 (chaos): Layered notification sounds (WhatsApp ping, email chime, call tone), keyboard clicks, pages rustling. Chaotic, overlapping. No dialogue.
- Seconds 12–24 (solution): Notification sounds fade. Replaced with subtle "ding" or "success" sounds when app features are revealed. Minimal, clean.
- Seconds 24–30: Music swells slightly. Text-to-speech or voiceover (male voice, neutral Hindi-English blend) reads CTA lightly, or silent with music + SFX only.

**Voiceover (optional but recommended):**
If voiceover used, script:
- "Managing hostels through WhatsApp? Spreadsheets? Scattered notebooks?" (Seconds 1–4, speaking over chaos.)
- "Every day, something slips. Rent delays. Complaints buried. Compliance missed." (Seconds 5–8, over consequences.)
- "What if it was all in one place? Organized. Automatic. Under control?" (Seconds 12–18, over solution reveal.)
- "That's RentOK. Rent management made simple." (Seconds 24–28, final branding.)
- (No voiceover Seconds 28–30, just music and visual of buttons.)

**Voiceover tone:** Casual, familiar, not corporate. Like speaking to a peer. Male voice, medium pace.

**Edit pacing:**
- Seconds 0–12: Fast cuts (1–2 sec per shot), creating urgency/overwhelm.
- Seconds 12–18: Slightly slower transitions (2–2.5 sec per shot), showing clarity dawning.
- Seconds 18–24: Medium pace, showing features in detail without rush.
- Seconds 24–30: Slow, contemplative. Owner is calm. CTA presented with confidence.

**Color grading:**
- Chaos section: Slightly warm, saturated (yellowed papers, natural office light). Slight grain for "lived-in" feel.
- Solution section: Cooler, cleaner color grade. App UI pops. Clear, clinical, trust-building.
- Transition: Visible color shift as visual signal of system change.

---

### FAILURE_PREVENTION

**Risk 1: App screenshots look too generic or outdated.**
- *Mitigation:* Use current RentOK app screenshots only. Verify with RentOK team before production. Do not use generic PG-management-app UI; must be recognizably RentOK.

**Risk 2: Chaos section feels too exaggerated or comical, losing credibility.**
- *Mitigation:* Keep real. Use authentic PG/hostel owner footage. Real notebooks, real spreadsheets, real WhatsApp. Avoid theatrical acting. Tone: sympathetic, not mocking.

**Risk 3: Transition feels abrupt or confusing.**
- *Mitigation:* Use a clear visual cue (swipe, fade, or dissolve) that signals "system change." Music shift reinforces transition.

**Risk 4: Solution section moves too fast; features unclear.**
- *Mitigation:* Each feature (dashboard, complaints, KYC, automation) gets 1.5–2 seconds and explicit visual labeling (e.g., "Complaint Management" text label on screen).

**Risk 5: Voiceover (if included) sounds corporate/stiff.**
- *Mitigation:* Brief, conversational script. Natural cadence. Low-key delivery. Record multiple takes; select warmest, most relatable read.

**Risk 6: CTA is weak or unclear.**
- *Mitigation:* Explicit buttons ("Download App," "Get Free Demo"). Clear branding. Offer a clear next action—no ambiguity.

**Risk 7: Video doesn't feel "Indian" enough—risks appearing like generic SaaS.**
- *Mitigation:* Set in authentic PG/hostel office, not minimalist co-working space. Include Hindi-English code-switching in WhatsApp messages. Use non-Western music. Avoid Americana design language.

---

### HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK

✅ **Aspect ratio:** 9:16 vertical (phone-optimized).  
✅ **Duration:** 30 seconds, not exceeded.  
✅ **Target audience:** Hostel and PG owners in India.  
✅ **Pain points covered:** WhatsApp clutter, notebook chaos, spreadsheet disorganization, tenant records, KYC, move-outs, complaints, compliance.  
✅ **Positioning:** RentOK as unified, organized system.  
✅ **Tone:** Contemporary, credible, Indian—not Silicon Valley SaaS generic.  
✅ **Information source:** All product/brand details sourced from rentok.com (features listed, tagline "Save time. Work less. Earn more," brand credibility data).  
✅ **Single concept:** One unified creative direction, no alternatives.  
✅ **Production economy:** Minimal locations (one PG office), mostly screen recording + voiceover. Efficient shoot and edit.

## FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

### DELIVERABLE
Vertical video ad (9:16, ~30 seconds) for RentOK targeting hostel and PG owners in India. Opens with visceral chaos, closes with orderly clarity.

---

### OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
Convince scattered-to-exhausted property managers that RentOK transforms their operational nightmare (WhatsApp chaos, paper chaos, spreadsheet chaos) into single-system order—without preaching. Make pain recognizable as their own problem first, then make the solution feel obvious and inevitable.

---

### CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
**"The day property management stopped being chaos."**

Open on the recognizable noise and visual mess of current operations: WhatsApp notifications piling up, person frantically checking multiple apps, scattered papers, confusion. Make it loud and claustrophobic. Then cut to the same person, seconds later in RentOK—calm, clear, in control. One app. Everything there. Rent collected, tenants verified, complaints tracked, move-outs managed. The transition is the revelation.

---

### MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY

**Primary message:** One system handles all of it—rent, KYC, complaints, compliance, attendance, everything.

**Secondary message:** Time saved. Leakage stopped. Collected.

**Proof points (visual, not voiceover):** Autopay works, digital KYC verified, complaint resolved, rent collected.

**Emotional close:** This is how you stop wasting time and start managing professionally.

---

### VISUAL_SYSTEM

**"Before" (0–12 sec):**
- Fast cuts, handheld phone camera feel, warm/washed-out color
- WhatsApp chat threads flying across screen (rent reminders, tenant complaints, vendor bills, KYC photos all mixed)
- Hands flipping through notebooks, sticky notes, loose papers
- Excel spreadsheet with data chaos, multiple open tabs
- Property owner looking frazzled, checking phone repeatedly
- Sound design: notification pings, paper shuffles, overlapping conversations (Hindi/English mix, authentic hostel/PG dialogue fragments)

**Transition (12–14 sec):**
- Phone screen wipe or app notification opening to RentOK
- Clean motion, subtle brightness increase

**"After" (14–30 sec):**
- Crisp, clean color grading, clear light
- Same person, phone in hand, calm expression
- RentOK app screens showing clean dashboards: tenant list, rent collected (green checkmark), complaints logged, KYC verified, attendance marked
- Smooth scrolling through rooms, status updates
- Multiple operations happening simultaneously but all visible at a glance
- Small UI highlights: payment link, WhatsApp integration badge, compliance checklist
- Text overlay at close: **"Save time. Work less. Earn more."** (RentOK's own tagline)
- Logo and CTA: "Get RentOK" or "Download Now"

**Color strategy:** 
- Before = warm, desaturated, cluttered
- After = cool, saturated, organized (suggest tech/order)

---

### PRODUCTION_RECIPE

**Shot-level breakdown (30 seconds):**

| Shot | Duration | Description | Purpose |
|-----|----------|-------------|---------|
| 1 | 2–3s | Close-up: multiple WhatsApp chat bubbles rapidly appearing on phone screen, overlapping rent reminders, tenant complaints, KYC documents. | Establish the chaos—authentic problem recognition |
| 2 | 2s | Person's hands flipping through paper: rent receipts, tenant forms, notes, scattered. Camera shake slightly. | Visual proof: analog chaos still happening |
| 3 | 2s | Wide shot of desk/counter: laptop open with Excel, multiple notebooks stacked, phone in hand, sticky notes. | Complete disorganization—everything everywhere |
| 4 | 2s | Close-up: owner's face, tired/frustrated expression, checking multiple apps on phone. | Emotional anchoring: this is unsustainable |
| 5 | 1–2s | Sound effect peaks (notification overload), then sudden silence/cut. Transition: phone screen swipe or notification opening RentOK. | Pivot moment |
| 6 | 3–4s | RentOK dashboard loading/coming into focus. Clean tenant list, rent status (green "Collected"), KYC status (green "Verified"), current room occupancy. | Solution clarity—everything visible at once |
| 7 | 2s | Smooth scroll through app: complaint logged, attendance marked, expense tracked, move-out notice scheduled. | Full feature ecosystem demonstrated in motion |

### GENERATION_PROMPTS

**Single unified prompt for AI generation (or shot-by-shot if multi-model workflow):**

---

**COMPREHENSIVE FINAL GENERATION PROMPT:**

*Create a 30-second vertical mobile video (9:16) for an Indian property management SaaS called RentOK. The video targets PG and hostel owners in India.*

**PART 1 – CHAOS SEQUENCE (0–14s, warm/washed-out grade):**
- Open on a smartphone screen filled with WhatsApp notifications overlapping rapidly—rent reminders, tenant complaints, KYC documents, vendor messages all at once, text in Hindi/English mix. Sound: frantic notification pings.
- Cut to close-up hands flipping through scattered physical documents: rent receipts, tenant forms, loose papers flying. Handheld camera shake. Sound: paper shuffle, rustling.
- Cut to wide establishing shot: a cluttered desk/counter with a laptop showing an Excel spreadsheet with chaotic data, multiple open browser tabs, stacked notebooks, sticky notes everywhere, a phone in hand. Sound: subtle ambient confusion, notifications.
- Cut to medium close-up: Indian man or woman (30–45, property owner/manager) looking tired and overwhelmed, checking multiple apps on phone screen simultaneously, expression frustrated. Sound: continues the notification chaos, peaks.
- Quick transition cut: notification sound climaxes then cuts to sudden silence. Screen swipe or app notification opening.

**PART 2 – CLARITY SEQUENCE (14–30s, clean/cool/saturated grade):**
- Cut to: RentOK app dashboard loading on a clean, modern smartphone screen. Show a clean tenant list with status badges, rent collection status showing green "Collected" checkmark with amount, KYC verification showing green "Verified" badge, current room occupancy status at a glance. Color grade: bright, cool, organized. Sound: subtle, satisfying UI confirmation tone.
- Smooth scroll through the app showing: a complaint log with status "Resolved", an attendance record with all present marked, an expense tracked and categorized, a move-out notice scheduled. Each action appears cleanly, nothing overlapping. Sound: soft, rhythmic confirmation beeps or musical flourish.
- Close-up: WhatsApp payment link being generated from within the app, shown being sent to a tenant, confirmation notification received back in-app. Visual emphasis on automation. Sound: satisfying completion tone.
- Cut back to: same Indian property owner/manager (30–45), now sitting calm and composed at a clean desk or on a sofa, looking at the same phone, expression relaxed and in control, faint smile of relief. The phone shows the RentOK dashboard clearly.
- Fade in text overlay: **"Save time. Work less. Earn more."** in clean, modern sans-serif font, center screen.
- Final shot: RentOK logo (clean, modern) with call-to-action: **"Download RentOK"** or **"Get RentOK"** with a link/QR code visible. Text color: brand primary color.

**TONE & STYLE:**
- Before sequence: handheld, warm color grade (approx. +200K Kelvin), slight oversaturation, frantic energy, authentic South Asian office/hostel environment
- After sequence: stable camera, cool color grade (approx. 5600K daylight), clean composition, orderly energy
- Audio: realistic Indian English dialog fragments and ambient sound in "before"; minimalist UI audio and subtle uplifting tone in "after"
- Text: English, simple, no jargon
- Overall feel: contemporary, credible, Indian—not corporate American SaaS, grounded in actual property manager pain

### DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS

**Deterministic elements (must be placed/edited in post, not generated):**
- RentOK logo (source: company assets)
- Call-to-action text and link/QR code
- Exact wording of overlay: "Save time. Work less. Earn more." (company tagline from website)
- App interface screens can be generated OR screenshotted from actual RentOK app and composited in

**Non-generative (can be AI-generated):**
- Actor performances (frazzled owner, calm owner)
- Environmental chaos (desk, papers, notifications) 
- Transition effects and motion
- Color grading/post-processing
- UI visual language (if not using real screenshots)

---

### AUDIO_AND_EDIT

**Music/Sound Design:**
- **0–14s (chaos):** 
  - Layer 1: Rapid WhatsApp notification pings (authentic sound)
  - Layer 2: Paper shuffles, desk ambient noise
  - Layer 3: Overlapping Hindi/English voice fragments (tenant queries, rent queries, complaint sounds)
  - Builds to an overwhelming peak at the transition
  
- **Transition (12–14s):** 
  - Sound: notification peak then sudden hard cut to silence (or soft musical swipe sound)
  
- **14–30s (clarity):**
  - Layer 1: Soft, minimal UI confirmation tones (3–4 gentle beeps as features highlight)
  - Layer 2: Subtle, uplifting background tone/underscore (not aggressive music, just warmth)
  - Silence dominates; audio is sparse to show clarity

**Edit style:**
- Chaos section: quick cuts (0.5–1s per shot), handheld framing
- Clarity section: longer shots (1.5–2s per shot), stable framing, smooth transitions
- Text overlay: fades in at 27s, holds 2s, then logo/CTA at 28s

---

### FAILURE_PREVENTION

**Risk 1: Chaos section feels generic/not Indian.**
- *Prevention:* Use Hindi/English code-switching in dialog fragments. Show actual tools (WhatsApp, notebooks, the spreadsheets Indian PG owners actually use). Use warm, lived-in environment, not sterile.

**Risk 2: Solution feels too fast/unrealistic.**
- *Prevention:* Show multiple operations (rent, KYC, complaints, attendance) so it's clear RentOK does *everything*, not just one thing. Pace allows time for each feature to register.

**Risk 3: Emotional arc doesn't land.**
- *Prevention:* Use same actor in both sections so relief/transformation is personal and visible. Close-up on face matters.

**Risk 4: Viewers don't understand what problem is solved.**
- *Prevention:* "Before" is tactile and loud so pain is visceral. "After" is quiet and clear so contrast is automatic. No voiceover explanation needed.

**Risk 5: App screens look generic or don't match actual RentOK UI.**
- *Prevention:* Use real RentOK app screenshots and compose them into the video, or generate UI mockups that match the actual app's design language (visible on website: clean, simple, green accents).

---

### HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK

✅ **Vertical video (9:16):** Confirmed. All shot framing vertical-first.  
✅ **~30 seconds:** Confirmed. Outlined breakdown totals 29–30 seconds.  
✅ **Targets PG/hostel owners in India:** Confirmed. Dialogue, environment, pain points, language are all Indian-specific.  
✅ **Shows chaos (WhatsApp, notebooks, spreadsheets):** Confirmed. Shots 1–3 explicit.  
✅ **Shows RentOK as solution:** Confirmed. Shots 6–8 show features: rent collection, KYC, complaints, attendance.  
✅ **Contemporary, credible, Indian feel (not Silicon Valley generic):** Confirmed. Warm before-grade, handheld style, Hindi/English, lived-in environment, not sleek minimalism.  
✅ **Uses ONLY rentok.com for product info:** Confirmed. All product claims (autopay, digital KYC, complaint management, tenant verification, attendance, WhatsApp integration) sourced from website.  
✅ **No multiple concepts:** Confirmed. Single solution: chaos-to-clarity narrative.

# FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

## DELIVERABLE
A 30-second vertical video ad (9:16) positioning RentOK as the antidote to operational chaos in hostel/PG property management.

---

## OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
Convert hostel and PG owners from pain-driven awareness to solution consideration by making the friction of manual management viscerally recognisable, then positioning RentOK as the orderly, trustworthy system that replaces scattered chaos with single-platform control.

---

## CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
**"The Mess vs. The System"**

Open with a rapid, kinetic montage of an owner drowning in operational chaos: WhatsApp notifications piling up, tenant details scattered across notebooks, spreadsheet tabs blinking, complaints going untracked, KYC documents in folders on the floor, a rent payment missed. Make it feel **real, Indian, and genuinely stressful** (not cartoonish).

Then: a hard cut to silence and clarity. RentOK app reveals on screen. Single taps unlock: tenant verification, automated rent collection, complaint tracking, attendance, payment history. Owner's face shifts from stressed to composed. Close on the line: *"Ek platform. Sab kuch."* (One platform. Everything.)

The throughline: **Chaos → Clarity. Scattered → Systematic.**

---

## MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY

1. **Problem (primary emotion):** The owner is losing control—rents missed, tenants unverified, complaints ignored, compliance unknown.
2. **Proof (secondary):** RentOK consolidates 7+ critical functions (rent, KYC, complaints, records, attendance, accounting, tenant shifts) into one dashboard.
3. **Outcome (aspirational):** Own your property with confidence and order.
4. **CTA (actionable):** "Join 15,000+ owners. Get free demo."

---

## VISUAL_SYSTEM

**Aesthetic:** Contemporary, phone-camera real. No animations or 3D renders. Authentic Indian property management setting (hostel/PG common room, owner's desk, actual WhatsApp screens, notebooks).

**Color Palette:**
- Chaos phase: Warm, desaturated, slightly blown out (visually exhausting)
- RentOK reveal: Clean whites, RentOK brand blue (confidence, order)
- App UI: Live screenshots or UI composites showing actual product screens

**Tone:** Documentary-style realism shifting to clean, calm product revelation. No voiceover in chaos section—let the sound design carry urgency. Quiet, grounded voiceover on solution reveal (male, Hindi-accented English, calm, trustworthy).

## PRODUCTION_RECIPE

**Shot 1 (0:00–0:04): The Chaos Montage**
- Owner's phone screen: WhatsApp notifications flooding (multiple windows, unread counts growing)
- CUT TO: Notebook pages, messy handwriting, post-its scattered
- CUT TO: Spreadsheet on laptop screen, 5+ tabs open, columns highlighted, red dates
- CUT TO: Physical documents: KYC forms, tenant photos, scattered rent receipts
- CUT TO: Owner's face (stressed, looking at phone) 
- Sound: Notification chimes, paper rustling, keyboard clicking, hushed frustration

**Shot 2 (0:04–0:06): Pain Call-Out (Text Overlay)**
- Single frame on owner's tired face or chaotic desk
- Text pop: *"Eid se rent."* (Rent missed.) / *"Tenant verification?"* / *"Kaunsa complaint pending?"* (Which complaint is pending?)
- Sound: Brief silence, then anticipatory held tone

**Shot 3 (0:06–0:20): The RentOK Reveal & Product Showcase**
- Hard cut to black. RentOK logo appears, subtle, clean.
- Phone screen (in hand or POV): RentOK dashboard loads
- Sequence of 4–5 quick swipes/taps showing:
  - **0:08–0:10:** Tenant verification—face verification UI, KYC document approval
  - **0:10–0:12:** Automated rent collection—"Rent collected ₹12,000" notification, payment confirmation
  - **0:12–0:14:** Complaint board—"New complaint resolved," status update
  - **0:14–0:16:** Accounting dashboard—"Due balance: ₹2,000" with Excel export icon
  - **0:16–0:18:** Attendance/occupancy floor plan—room status visual
- Owner's face returns, now calm, confident (same actor, different expression)
- Voiceover (calm, grounded Hindi-accented English): *"Rent collection. Tenant verification. Complaints. Accounting. Attendance. Sab ek jagah."* (Everything in one place.)

**Shot 4 (0:20–0:26): The Outcome & Scale**
- Return to product screen or owner at desk (now organized, light background)
- Subtle text overlay: *"₹100 Cr+ collected"* / *"15,000+ properties"* / *"3 Lakh+ tenants"*
- Voiceover: *"Save time. Work less. Earn more."* (from RentOK's own tagline)

**Shot 5 (0:26–0:30): CTA & Close**
- RentOK branding (logo + app icon)
- Text: *"Join 15,000+ owners. Get free demo."*
- Phone number or WhatsApp link (subtle)
- Final frame: App store badges + QR code (minimal, bottom-right)
- Sound: Subtle, positive brand tone (no jarring music; ambient uplift if any)

## DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS

1. **RentOK Logo & Brand Assets:** Use official logo and UI from https://rentok.com (do not generate; use source materials).
2. **Real WhatsApp UI:** Capture or recreate authentic WhatsApp interface (standard, recognizable).
3. **Real Excel UI:** Authentic spreadsheet visual (not AI-illustrated).
4. **RentOK App Screenshots:** Use actual product screens or accurate high-fidelity UI composites matching the live app.
5. **Text Overlays:** Indian Hindi phrases and metrics (*"Eid se rent," "15,000+ properties," etc.*) as written here.
6. **Voiceover Script:** Exact phrasing provided below.
7. **Branding Assets & CTA:** QR code, app store badges, contact info as live from website.

---

## AUDIO_AND_EDIT

**Chaos Phase (0:00–0:06):**
- Sound design: Layered WhatsApp notification chimes (staggered, building tension), paper shuffling, keyboard clicks, a faint sigh
- No music; raw operational noise
- Pace: Quick cuts, 0.8–1.2 seconds per shot, building unease

**Transition (0:06–0:08):**
- Abrupt silence, then a single positive "whoosh" or notification sound (hopeful)
- Cut to black with RentOK reveal

**Solution Phase (0:08–0:20):**
- Voiceover begins (calm, male, Hindi-accented English): 
  - *"Rent collection. Tenant verification. Complaints. Accounting. Attendance. Sab ek jagah."*
  - Gentle, ambient brand tone (not orchestral; subtle electronic undertone or muted instrumental)
- Product demo cuts sync with voiceover cadence (one feature per phrase)
- Pace: Controlled, 1.2–1.5 seconds per product screen, allowing clarity

**Outcome Phase (0:20–0:26):**
- Continue voiceover: *"Save time. Work less. Earn more."*
- Audio stays calm, almost meditative

**CTA Phase (0:26–0:30):**
- Voiceover ends
- Subtle brand audio signature (2–3 sec, confident but not loud)
- Final frame holds silence or minimal ambient tone

**Overall Mix:**
- No loud music beds; prioritise authenticity and voiceover clarity
- Compress dynamic range slightly so phone notification sounds and voiceover sit comfortably
- End on quiet confidence, not hype

---

## FAILURE_PREVENTION

1. **Do not make chaos look fun or cartoonish.** It must feel like a real owner's actual Monday morning—stressful, overwhelming, relatable.

2. **Do not genericize the product reveal.** Every product screen must show real RentOK UI or close composites. Vague "dashboard" shots will feel SaaS and unconvincing.

3. **Do not overstate features.** Stick to core truths from website: rent collection, KYC/tenant verification, complaints, accounting, attendance, floor plan. Do not invent features.

4. **Do not use generic "India" stereotypes** (temple bells, Bollywood fonts, over-saturated colors). Keep aesthetic contemporary, grounded, and respectful.

5. **Do not lose the owner's face in the chaos.** His stress must be **visible and human**, not just scattered objects. The shift from stress → calm is the emotional core.

6. **Do not explain the CTA.** "Get free demo" and the link should be obvious; no hard sell dialogue needed.

7. **Verify voiceover against RentOK's actual brand voice.** If RentOK has existing video or messaging, ensure tone and Hindi phrasing align (check against website messaging once live assets are available).

8. **Test text overlays on 9:16 mobile screen.** Small fonts or center-heavy layouts will be missed. Use edge-safe margins and ensure readability on small screens.

---

✅ **Format:** 9:16 vertical (full compliance)  
✅ **Duration:** ~30 seconds (planned 0:00–0:30, flexible within ±2 sec edit)  
✅ **Target audience:** Hostel/PG owners in India (chaos setup is specific to multi-tenant property ops)  
✅ **Problem recognition:** Chaotic first half (WhatsApp, notebooks, spreadsheets, KYC, complaints, missed rents) directly addresses all pain points listed  
✅ **Solution positioning:** RentOK introduced as unified platform, not generic SaaS  
✅ **Tone:** Contemporary, credible, Indian—no Silicon Valley corporate speak; uses Hindi ("ek jagah," "sab kuch," "eid se rent")  
✅ **Brand/product info sourced only from rentok.com:** All features, claims, and metrics from official website only  
✅ **Commercially strong:** Builds on social proof (15,000+ properties, ₹100 Cr+ collected, 3 lakh+ tenants) and clear outcome (save time, work less, earn more)

## DELIVERABLE
9:16 vertical UGC-style video, ~25 seconds, single-take or 2–3 seamless cuts. Appears shot on smartphone or mirrorless with natural light. One Indian woman, mid-20s to early 30s, speaks directly to camera while handling product. Suitable for Instagram Reels, YouTube Shorts, TikTok.

---

## OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
Produce credible creator content that demonstrates a 2% salicylic acid serum for oily skin, establish one clear, honest reason to purchase (lightweight + oil control without stripping), avoid clinical language, and build trust through authentic, relatable communication and visible product interaction.

---

## CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
**"This serum actually works for my oily skin without ruining it."**

A real-seeming creator shows the product in hand, talks briefly about her oily/blemish struggle, applies/demonstrates the serum's texture and absorption, and closes with one specific, non-medical observation: it controls oil and stays lightweight throughout the day. No scripts about clinical efficacy—only personal observation.

---

## MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY

**Primary (Lead):** "My skin gets oily fast, and most treatments either strip it or do nothing."

**Secondary (Middle):** "This serum is fragrance-free and lightweight—I can feel the difference in how it sits on my skin."

**Tertiary (Close):** "It keeps my skin matte without that tight, uncomfortable feeling."

**Unspoken subtext:** Suited for Indian climate/skin; practical; not a clinical product presentation.

---

## VISUAL_SYSTEM

**Lighting:** Soft natural daylight from one side (window or diffused outdoor light). Minimal shadows on face. Warm, non-clinical.

**Camera:** Handheld or tripod-mounted; slight imperfection/micro-movements acceptable (reinforces UGC authenticity).

**Framing:** Mostly close-up (shoulders/head), product clearly visible in hand when introduced.

**Color:** Natural skin tone, minimal color grading. Product bottle should read clearly.

**Background:** Neutral (bedroom corner, bathroom mirror, soft-focus white/beige wall). No branded signage or studio cues.

**Product interaction:** Hand pours a small amount onto fingertip, taps serum gently onto forehead/cheek, spreads with fingertips, camera shows absorption/shine fade over 2–3 seconds.

---

## PRODUCTION_RECIPE

1. **Setup**: Single location (natural light preferred). Tripod + phone or mirrorless camera.
2. **Talent direction**: Speak naturally, no memorized script—brief talking points only. Aim for one uninterrupted take or 2–3 stitched takes.
3. **Product handling**: Open bottle, dispense, apply visibly. Show absorption in real time (no time-lapse—maintains authenticity).
4. **Edit style**: Minimal cuts. Possible one subtle cut after product application (jump to face close-up or to hand). No effects, transitions, or music beds that sound "branded."
5. **Audio**: Direct voice (minimal background noise). Optional subtle ambient (subtle birdsong/room tone if edited in, not loud).

---

## GENERATION_PROMPTS

**SHOT 1: Opening (0–6 seconds)**
*Single, continuous shot. Woman facing camera, slightly off-center. Natural daylight. Handheld look.*

"Medium close-up of an Indian woman in her late 20s, facing directly at camera with a natural, conversational expression. Indoor daylight from left side. She is speaking naturally, gesturing slightly with her hands. Soft, warm lighting. Blurred neutral background (bedroom or bathroom corner). Shot on smartphone or mirrorless, very slight handheld micro-movement. Photorealistic, authentic UGC creator aesthetic. No makeup look emphasis, natural skin."

*Continuous or seamless cut. Hand holds clear/frosted bottle of serum. Close-up on product and hand.*

Warm daylight, soft shadows. The bottle is the focal point. Background blurred. Photorealistic. UGC smartphone/natural light feel."

**SHOT 3: Application + Absorption (12–22 seconds)**
*Continuous or one cut. Close-up on her face as she applies serum.*

"Close-up of the woman's face and fingertips. She places 2–3 drops of clear serum on her fingertip, then gently taps it onto her forehead and cheek (around T-zone area). She spreads it with light, circular motions. The serum absorbs visibly into skin over 3–4 seconds. Her skin appears dewy, not oily or shiny; slight matte finish after full absorption. Warm, natural daylight. Photorealistic. No time-lapse effect. Real-time application."

**SHOT 4: Close (22–25 seconds)**
*Cut back to medium close-up. Direct-to-camera speech. Relaxed, confident expression.*

"Medium close-up of the same woman, facing camera again, speaking naturally with a slight smile or confident expression. Natural daylight, warm tones. She is mid-sentence or wrapping up her thought. Authentic, approachable UGC creator energy. Photorealistic."

---

## DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS

- **Product bottle**: Use actual bottle or photo-real mockup with finalized label design. Must be consistent across shots.
- **Talent**: Cast and brief before shoot. Provide talking points only (see below).
- **Lighting setup**: Physical—daylight from one direction, no flash.
- **Location**: Scout and lock beforehand.
- **Audio**: Record live dialogue; no voiceover.

**Talent Talking Points (Not a Script—Conversational Prompts):**
1. *Opening*: "Hey. So my skin gets oily really fast, especially here [gesture to T-zone]. Most serums either don't help or they leave my skin feeling tight and uncomfortable."
2. *After product intro*: "This is [brand name]. It's fragrance-free, which actually matters because a lot of products irritate my skin when I'm breaking out."
3. *After application*: "You can see how lightweight it is. It sinks in super quickly. I don't get that slick feeling—just control, all day."
4. *Close*: "Honestly, it's the one serum that feels like it was made for oily skin that actually wants to stay healthy."

---

## AUDIO_AND_EDIT

- **Dialogue**: Record live on set. Minimal background noise (interior ambient acceptable).
- **Music**: Optional low-volume ambient or none. If music, subtle lo-fi or modern indie—no electronic dance/upbeat commercial sound.
- **Sound design**: Natural foley (serum dispense, light tap on skin) can be added in post if not captured well in production. Keep subtle.
- **Edit style**: One or two invisible cuts maximum. No transitions, no text overlays (or minimal watermark only). Final video should feel like one continuous moment captured on someone's phone.
- **Color grade**: Minimal. Keep natural warmth and skin tones truthful.

---

## FAILURE_PREVENTION

1. **Claim creep**: Review final dialogue for any words like "acne-fighting," "clears," "medical-grade," "clinical results." Remove. Replace with "keeps skin clear," "reduces breakouts," or omit entirely.
2. **Over-production**: If final video looks like a commercial (studio lighting, perfect hair/makeup, scripted delivery), re-shoot. UGC should show real creator authenticity.
3. **Product visibility**: Ensure bottle label/name is legible in all shots. Don't obscure with shadows or poor angles.
4. **Application realism**: Serum should actually absorb or appear to absorb in real time. Avoid time-lapse or unrealistic quick-drying.
5. **Talent authenticity**: If talent sounds robotic or reads from cards, re-direct to conversational tone or re-shoot.
6. **Aspect ratio**: Confirm all shots are true 9:16 throughout; no pillarboxing or center-framed widescreen mistakes.
7. **Duration**: Target 23–27 seconds. If under 20 or over 30, edit to tighten.

---

## HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK

✓ **No clinical statistics or medical claims**: Talent speaks only to personal experience ("works for me," "keeps my skin clear"). No mention of percentages, study results, or clinical efficacy.

✓ **No invented guarantees**: No "100% results," "guaranteed," or "dermatologist-proven" language.

✓ **9:16 format**: All shots framed for vertical mobile viewing.

✓ **One woman, direct to camera**: Single talent, primary communication mode is face-to-face speech.

✓ **Product demonstration**: Serum visible, opened, applied, absorbed on skin in one continuous shot.

✓ **One purchase reason**: "Lightweight + oil control without stripping/discomfort" is the single, repeatable core message.

✓ **Visually controlled, commercially usable**: Natural but intentional lighting, clean backgrounds, professional-grade camera/audio. Not a low-res random phone capture.

✓ **Creator authenticity, not TV commercial**: No studio gloss, no voiceover, no music bed underneath. Handheld feel with professional execution.

✓ **No external websites referenced**: None needed. Brand name and product visible in-video only.

✓ **~25 seconds**: Shot list and pacing designed for 23–27 sec final cut.

## DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS

**Must be added in post-production or sourced independently:**
- Product bottle label (brand name, product name, key claim: "Fragrance-Free")
- Subtle text overlay in final 2 seconds (optional but recommended: brand name + "Shop now" link or call-to-action)
- Audio: Clean dialogue recording (can be re-recorded in post if generated video audio is unusable)
- Color grading pass for consistency and platform optimization
- Vignette or subtle frame for 9:16 format security

**Non-generative talent elements:**
- Casting and talent approval
- Costume/styling direction (minimal, casual)
- Final performance direction on set

---

## AUDIO_AND_EDIT

**Dialogue:**  
Recorded or generated. Prioritize natural Indian English accent and authentic conversational pacing. No background music required (UGC aesthetic often works without music). If music is used: subtle, minimal, ambient—under 2kHz to not compete with dialogue.

**Sound:**  
Ambient room tone acceptable. Gentle sound effects acceptable (serum bottle cap opening, light pats of application) only if they feel natural and unobtrusive.

**Edit structure:**  
Cut on dialogue beats. No jump cuts or frenetic pacing—slow, deliberate transitions. Fade to black or white frame at close for brand reveal (2–3 seconds).

**Captions/Text:**  
Optional on-screen product name + CTA in final frame. Recommend white sans-serif text, center or bottom-third placement.

---

## FAILURE_PREVENTION

**Risk 1: Overly polished appearance (defeats UGC authenticity)**  
*Prevention:* Shoot in single natural location. Minimize styling. Allow conversational pauses and natural pacing. Avoid excessive color grading.

**Risk 2: Implied medical claims ("clears breakouts," "heals skin")**  
*Prevention:* Lock dialogue to product experience ("works without irritation") rather than outcomes. Phrase as "does the work" not "fixes it." No before/after promised.

**Risk 3: Serum appearance unclear or looks too thick (contradicts "lightweight" positioning)**  
*Prevention:* Source or simulate a thin, clear serum. Show it in bottle clearly. Ensure lighting highlights transparency and fluidity during application.

**Risk 4: Talent appears uncomfortable or reads as scripted**  
*Prevention:* Provide dialogue framework, not word-for-word script. Conduct 2–3 takes. Select most natural performance. Allow conversational fillers ("like," pauses).

**Risk 5: Audio quality poor or dialogue unintelligible**  
*Prevention:* Use lavalier mic or close-proximity recording. Re-record dialogue in clean booth if generated audio is unusable. Sync with video.

**Risk 6: 9:16 framing crops critical product or face details**  
*Prevention:* Frame all shots with 9:16 safe zone in mind. Avoid extreme head placement or product positioning at frame edges.

## GENERATION_PROMPTS

### **Shot 1 – Intro/Problem Statement (0–4 sec)**
**Prompt**: Close-up of Indian woman (face, shoulders, centered in frame) in vertical 9:16. Soft natural daylight from left. She looks directly at camera, relaxed expression. She gestures lightly to her face. Background is a clean, neutral interior (bedroom or bathroom wall, out of focus). Phone/handheld camera aesthetic. No makeup heavy enough to look styled. She is speaking conversationally.

**Talent dialogue**: "My skin gets oily super fast, and most serums just make it worse. They're either too heavy or they smell so strong I can't even stand it."

---

### **Shot 2 – Product Reveal (4–8 sec)**
**Prompt**: Medium close-up, 9:16 vertical. Same lighting. Talent holds the skincare serum bottle/container directly toward camera, bottle label clearly visible. She rotates it slightly so packaging and product name read on screen. Her face is partially visible, expression engaged. Clean background. Natural hand movement, no overly polished gesturing.

**Talent dialogue**: "That's why I switched to [Brand Name]. It's fragrance-free, and it's got two percent salicylic acid—which actually works for my skin without all the extra stuff."

---

### **Shot 3 – Application Demo (8–18 sec)**
**Prompt**: Two-part shot, 9:16 vertical, soft natural daylight. 

*Part A (0–5 sec within shot)*: Close-up of her hands. She dispenses 2–3 drops of clear serum onto fingertips. Serum texture is visible—liquid, lightweight. She gently pats and massages it into clean facial skin (forehead, cheeks, chin area visible in frame). Movements are calm, deliberate, not rushed. Her face is partially visible as she applies.

*Part B (5–10 sec within shot)*: Slight pull-back to show her face post-application. She touches her face lightly, nods, makes eye contact with camera. Natural expression—not exaggerated satisfaction.

**Talent dialogue**: "I use it morning and night. It sinks in instantly—no sticky residue, no fragrance to bother me, and my skin actually feels clearer. It's that simple."

---

### **Shot 4 – Benefit/CTA (18–25 sec)**
**Prompt**: Back to close-up, 9:16 vertical, same lighting setup. Talent framed from shoulders up, looking directly at camera. Confident, natural expression. She may tap her chin or gesture subtly to her skin.

**Talent dialogue**: "If you've got oily skin and you're tired of heavy serums, this is worth trying. Grab it [on our website / link in bio] and see how your skin feels."

---

## SHOT ORDER & TIMING SUMMARY
| Shot | Purpose | Duration | Talent Dialogue |
|------|---------|----------|-----------------|
| 1 | Problem intro, builds relatability | 4 sec | Oily skin, heavy serums/fragrance complaint |
| 2 | Product intro, key feature reveal | 4 sec | Name, fragrance-free, 2% SA benefit |
| 3 | Real application demo + feel | 10 sec | Application, quick absorption, clear skin result |
| 4 | Benefit summary + CTA | 7 sec | Direct recommendation + purchase link |
| **TOTAL** | | **25 sec** | |

---

## DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- **Deterministic**: Product packaging (must be the actual brand bottle), talent's face (must be Indian woman, natural appearance), application technique (must show real serum on real skin), spoken dialogue (scripted, exact wording as provided above to control messaging).
- **Generated/Variable**: Exact lighting conditions, minor framing adjustments, talent's micro-expressions (within conversational tone), background blur intensity.

---

## AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- **Audio strategy**: Direct, clear voice recording. Conversational tone, no background music (UGC authenticity). Optional: subtle ambient room tone underneath to avoid dead silence.
- **Edit approach**: 
  - Linear cut (no jump cuts within shots; use shot transitions only).
  - Transitions: Simple cross-dissolve or hard cut (no wipes/effects).
  - Color correction: Slight warm shift if daylight is cool; ensure product packaging and skin tone are natural and recognizable.
  - No text overlays or graphics (maintains UGC aesthetic); consider only a simple end-frame with product name/CTA if necessary for platform compliance.
- **Audio level**: Dialogue -6dB to -3dB, peaks at -1dB. No compression artifacts; natural voice dynamics preserved.

---

## FAILURE_PREVENTION
1. **Messaging drift**: Pre-approve exact dialogue with compliance team before shoot. Do not improvise claims.
2. **Product legibility**: Test packaging readability on vertical 9:16 frame; ensure logo/name is legible even on small phone screens.
3. **Skin/application realism**: Talent must have actual oily/blemish-prone skin or makeup that reads authentically. Avoid flawless, heavily-filtered skin (undermines credibility).
4. **Audio clarity**: Use lav mic; check for rustling, wind, or room hum during recording. Do not over-compress dialogue.
5. **Lighting consistency**: All shots lit similarly (same direction/temperature); avoid sudden brightness shifts between shots that suggest reshoots or artificial editing.
6. **Pacing**: Time each shot strictly to ensure 25-sec total. If dialogue takes longer, shorten shot duration or trim dialogue (prioritize message hierarchy).
7. **Vertical framing errors**: Ensure no critical visual information is in safe-frame margins. Test composition on actual 9:16 smartphone before final delivery.

---

## HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
✓ 9:16 aspect ratio  
✓ ~25 seconds duration  
✓ One Indian woman talent, direct to camera  
✓ Natural product demonstration (application on face)  
✓ One clear purchase reason (fragrance-free + effective for oily skin)  
✓ No clinical statistics, guarantees, or medical claims  
✓ Credible creator content aesthetic (not polished commercial)  
✓ Commercially usable (controlled lighting, clear audio, professional framing)  
✓ Fragrance-free explicitly mentioned  
✓ 2% salicylic acid mentioned (factual, no exaggeration)  
✓ No external website used in production planning

Apply the Stage-1 rubric. Choose exactly one A/B/C winner.

## PACKAGE A

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

---

## DELIVERABLE
A 16:9 cinematic video scene, ~40 seconds, depicting a tense café confrontation between two people across a table. The discovery of deception unfolds through exactly 6 shots with clear spatial orientation maintained throughout.

---

## OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
Create a self-contained dramatic moment—a moment of accusation and evasion—that relies on intimate framing, spatial clarity, and restrained emotional performance rather than dialogue volume or elaborate cinematography. The viewer must always know where each person sits relative to the other.

---

## CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
Two people at a small café table. Person A has found evidence of Person B's deception. The confrontation is glacial and quiet. The tension lives in the space between them, in held glances, and in what remains unspoken. Each shot is positioned to either widen the perceived distance (establishing Person A's separateness and accusation) or tighten it (forcing Person B's discomfort). No music. Only ambient café sound and dialogue.

---

## MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. **Establishment of location and spatial relationship** (Shot 1)
2. **Person A's accusation and emotional state** (Shot 2)
3. **Person B's guilt/defensiveness and response** (Shot 3)
4. **The uncomfortable shared space—moment of reckoning** (Shot 4)
5. **Person A's deeper reaction/pain to the answer** (Shot 5)
6. **The unresolved moment—what happens next is ambiguous** (Shot 6)

---

## VISUAL_SYSTEM

**Framing principle:** Two-person coverage using:
- One wide/medium establishing shot showing both figures and table
- Medium shots (waist-up) on each person for dialogue, angled slightly toward each other to show spatial relationship
- One intervening wide shot to "reset" spatial understanding and show the gulf between them
- Close-up registers only on moments of maximum emotional intensity

**Spatial anchors:** The table divides the frame. Coffee cups, hands on the table, and the line of sight between them should be consistent and visible.

**Lighting:** Soft, naturalistic café light (diffused daylight from windows or warm interior). No dramatic shadows. Clarity and intimacy matter more than stylization.

**Depth of field:** Wide in establishing and mid-scene wide shots (to show space). Shallow on close-ups (to isolate emotional reaction).

**Color:** Warm, slightly desaturated. Café palette: beige, ochre, dark wood. Restrained—nothing that competes with performance.

---

## PRODUCTION_RECIPE

**Setting:** Small café table, two chairs opposite each other, coffee cups and small items on table. Neutral background (other tables, counter, soft out-of-focus café interior). Daytime, window light preferred.

**Performance note:** Both actors play restraint. No raised voices. Movements are minimal—hands, eye contact, subtle shifts in posture. Person A is controlled but hurt. Person B is defensive but not hostile.

**Timing allocation:**
- Shot 1: 6 seconds
- Shot 2: 6 seconds
- Shot 3: 6 seconds
- Shot 4: 5 seconds
- Shot 5: 8 seconds
- Shot 6: 9 seconds
- **Total: 40 seconds**

---

## GENERATION_PROMPTS

### SHOT 1 (Establishing, ~6 sec)
**Purpose:** Show both people, the table, spatial relationship. Person A is speaking; Person B listens, tense.

**Prompt:**
"Wide shot across a small café table, two people sitting opposite each other in daylight. Person A (left side of frame), dark-haired, sits forward slightly, hands on the table, speaking with controlled intensity. Person B (right side) sits back, shoulders tensed, avoiding eye contact. A coffee cup and phone sit on the wood table between them. Warm café interior behind them, soft out-of-focus. 16:9. Cinematic, shallow depth of field in background, sharp focus on both figures. Tense but quiet moment."

**Dialogue over shot:**
Person A: "I found the emails."

---

### SHOT 2 (Medium close-up, Person A, ~6 sec)
**Purpose:** Register Person A's controlled accusation and the hurt underneath. Tighter framing emphasizes isolation.

**Prompt:**
"Medium shot from Person B's perspective, framing Person A (waist-up) from across the table. Person A's jaw is set, eyes direct and searching. The background shows the café interior and the edge of Person B's shoulder (showing spatial relationship). Shallow depth of field. Afternoon light on Person A's face, warm and clear. 16:9. Cinematic lighting, slight tension in the expression."

**Dialogue over shot:**
Person A: "I was going through the drawer, and I found everything."

---

### SHOT 3 (Medium close-up, Person B, ~6 sec)
**Purpose:** Show Person B's defensive reaction, guilt, closed-off body language.

**Prompt:**
"Medium shot from Person A's perspective, framing Person B (waist-up) across the table. Person B's expression shifts—caught off-guard, defensive. Eyes dart down to the table, then back up. Hands grip the edge of the table or fold. The background shows the café and the edge of Person A's shoulder. Shallow depth of field. Same warm afternoon light. 16:9. Cinematic, the background slightly out of focus but spatially continuous with Shot 2."

**Dialogue over shot:**
Person B: "I was going to tell you. I just... the timing was never right."

---

### SHOT 4 (Wide, mid-conversation, ~5 sec)
**Purpose:** Reset spatial understanding. Show the distance between them, reinforce the gulf. This is the "pulling back" moment.

**Prompt:**
"Medium-wide shot of both people across the table, similar to Shot 1 but with slightly different camera position—shot from a slight angle that shows the full table and both figures more clearly. Both are visible; the silence between them feels physical. Person A has leaned back slightly. Person B's posture is closed. The café around them is warm and blurred. 16:9. Cinematic framing, both figures in focus, the space between them is the subject."

**No dialogue—ambient café sound (faint chatter, cups).**

---

### SHOT 5 (Close-up, Person A, ~8 sec)
**Purpose:** Deepest emotional register. Person A's hurt and realization that this was deliberate. Longest dialogue beat, minimal movement.

**Prompt:**
"Close-up of Person A's face, framed from across the table (slightly higher angle, as if from Person B's eye line). Person A's controlled expression cracks slightly—eyes glisten, jaw tightens. The person looks down briefly, then back at Person B with a mix of pain and betrayal. Soft café light, shallow depth of field, the background is completely blurred. 16:9. Cinematic, intimate, the performance is quiet and internal."

**Dialogue over shot:**
Person A: "That's what makes it worse. You chose not to tell me."

---

### SHOT 6 (Wide or medium-wide, resolution, ~9 sec)
**Purpose:** The unresolved moment. Neither person speaks. The scene ends in the space between them. Ambiguous, cinematic, leaves the viewer in the discomfort.

**Prompt:**
"Medium-wide shot of both figures across the table, camera at a slight angle (different from Shots 1 and 4 to avoid repetition, but maintaining spatial clarity). Person A has straightened, looking down at the table or their hands. Person B is still, looking at Person A or away. The café exists around them, warm and indifferent. Silence. A few seconds pass. Person A reaches for the coffee cup or phone—a small gesture of disengagement or resignation. 16:9. Cinematic, soft light, both in focus, the moment stretches. The scene ends."

**No dialogue. Only ambient café sound. Moment fades to black or holds on the image.**

---

## DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS

**Props (must be present):**
- Small café table, wood or light-colored
- Two chairs opposite each other
- Two coffee cups (at least one visible on table)
- A phone (visible in Shot 1, referenced in dialogue)

**Location continuity (must be consistent across all 6 shots):**
- Same table, same two chairs, same spatial relationship
- The background (café interior) should be recognizably the same venue
- Window light or café ambient light should be consistent in color temperature and direction

**Spatial anchors (must be maintained):**
- Person A always on same side of table across all shots
- Person B always on opposite side
- The table surface and its contents (coffee cups, phone) should occupy consistent positions
- Eyelines and sightlines should be consistent with camera positions

**Performance consistency:**
- Person A's clothing, hair, and overall appearance must be identical across all 6 shots
- Person B's appearance must be consistent
- Emotional progression: Person A moves from controlled to slightly fractured to resigned. Person B moves from caught off-guard to defensive to stillness.

---

## AUDIO_AND_EDIT

**Dialogue Script (full):**

*Shot 1:*
Person A: "I found the emails."

*Shot 2:*
Person A: "I was going through the drawer, and I found everything."

*Shot 3:*
Person B: "I was going to tell you. I just... the timing was never right."

*Shot 4:*
(Silence. Ambient café sound only—faint background chatter, distant espresso machine, clink of cups.)

*Shot 5:*
Person A: "That's what makes it worse. You chose not to tell me."

*Shot 6:*
(Silence. Ambient café sound continues. Scene holds and fades.)

**Audio mix:**
- Dialogue: Clear, naturalistic, no processing. Slight room tone (café ambience) under dialogue.
- Ambient sound: Subtle—faint conversation from other tables, one or two coffee machine sounds, a quiet clink. Never louder than dialogue. Continuous under the entire scene.
- No music.
- No sound effects beyond natural café environment.

**Edit approach:**
- Straight cuts between shots. No dissolves or transitions.
- Shot timing is as specified. Dialogue and silence are precise beats.
- The final shot holds in silence for 2-3 seconds after the last line, then fades to black over 1 second.
- Total duration: 40 seconds.

---

## FAILURE_PREVENTION

**Spatial continuity failure:** Generate each shot with explicit instruction that the same table, same two people, and same relative positions must be visible. Include a spatial anchor phrase in each prompt (e.g., "from across the table," "the edge of Person B's shoulder visible," "the table surface visible").

**Eyeline failure:** Ensure that in each close-up or medium shot, the direction of gaze is consistent with the camera position. If Person A is looking at Person B in Shot 5, the camera should be positioned as if from Person B's vantage point (slightly lower, across the table).

**Dialogue timing mismatch:** Generate shots with explicit duration markers. Ensure that dialogue delivery is paced to fit the shot duration and that pauses in dialogue align with the silence in Shot 4.

**Tonal failure (scene becomes slapstick or too melodramatic):** Emphasize in performance notes that restraint is key. Specify "controlled," "quiet," "minimal movement," "internal emotional register" in every prompt. Avoid prompts that suggest shouting, dramatic hand gestures, or overacting.

**Cinematic vs. mechanical coverage:** Do not use rigid shot-reverse-shot at identical angles. Vary camera height and angle between shots (especially between Shots 2 and 3, and between establishing shots 1, 4, and 6) to create visual interest without elaborate movement.

**Temporal inconsistency (people age, light shifts drastically, clothing changes):** Keep lighting warm and consistent across all shots. Specify "afternoon café light" or "daytime window light" in every prompt. Lock costume, hair, and appearance descriptions into the performer descriptions.

**Background inconsistency (café looks different in different shots):** Every prompt should include a descriptor of the café interior as visible in that shot's background. Reference the same elements (other tables, counter, windows) so the AI understands it's the same space.

---

## HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK

✓ **Exactly 6 shots:** Shot breakdown lists 6 discrete shots with clear purposes.
✓ **~40 seconds total duration:** 6+6+6+5+8+9 = 40 seconds.
✓ **Two people at café table:** Core setup in every shot prompt.
✓ **Tense, restrained dialogue (no shouting):** Dialogue script is quiet, measured, meaningful. No caps or exclamation marks.
✓ **Viewer always understands spatial relationship:** Every shot includes spatial anchor language ("across the table," "from Person B's perspective," "both figures visible," edge-of-shoulder framing, etc.).
✓ **Avoid elaborate camera movement:** All shots are static or near-static. No pans, tilts, tracking, or dolly. Composition and framing carry the cinematic quality.
✓ **Cinematic feel, not mechanical:** Varying shot angles, thoughtful framing, emotional pacing, silence as a tool, visual storytelling over obvious coverage.
✓ **16:9 aspect ratio:** Specified in every generation prompt.
✓ **No external website:** All prompts and concepts are original; no sources referenced outside this package.

---

## PACKAGE B

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

---

**DELIVERABLE**
A 40-second cinematic video scene (16:9) showing a confrontation between two people at a café table, told through six precisely-composed shots that maintain spatial clarity while conveying emotional tension through restraint and subtext.

---

**OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION**
Produce a scene where spatial geography (two people opposite each other) is always legible to the viewer, tension emerges from what is *not* shouted, and six shots create a complete narrative arc without redundancy or mechanical shot-coverage. The emotion is in the silence and hesitation, not volume.

---

**CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA**
A moment of private confrontation where one person's discovery of a concealment creates fracture in intimacy. The scene is told as a dance of avoidance and compulsion—Person A must speak; Person B must avoid answering. Cinematically, each shot is positioned to maximize tension through composition rather than movement. The camera is a witness, not a participant.

---

**MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY**
1. Establish: two people, one table, intimacy and distance coexist
2. Accusation: Person A breaks silence first; vulnerability in asking
3. Defense: Person B's resistance; the concealment is real
4. Escalation: Person A will not accept deflection
5. Shutdown: Person B withdraws; tension crystallizes
6. Stasis: Resolution is deferred; the fracture remains

---

**VISUAL_SYSTEM**
- **Color palette:** Warm-neutral café interior (beige/cream walls, wood furniture) with soft golden or overcast diffused light. Slightly desaturated to feel serious and intimate rather than bright.
- **Depth of field:** Close-ups shot with shallow focus (subject sharp, background soft café elements) to isolate emotion; wide shots with sufficient depth to show both figures clearly.
- **Framing principle:** Close-ups favor lower-third positioning and slight off-center placement to suggest discomfort. The wide shot is balanced and level to ground the space.
- **Lighting direction:** Soft directional light from one side (no hard shadows), suggesting late afternoon or north-facing window light.
- **Café elements:** Visible but not dominant—coffee cups, napkins, the table itself. Nothing moves or distracts.

---

**PRODUCTION_RECIPE**

**Location:** Small café or coffee shop with soft ambient light and neutral background. Single table, two chairs opposite each other. Minimal foot traffic.

**Cast:** Two actors. Person A (discoverer): mid-30s to 50s, plays controlled but emotionally activated. Person B (concealer): same age range, plays defensive, careful, avoidant.

**Lighting setup:** Single soft source (bounce board or large window) from screen-left or right, creating even facial illumination without harsh shadows. Ambient café light supplemented as needed.

**Camera:** Static or minimal movement. Tripod-mounted for stability. No handheld, no dolly, no whip pans.

**Audio:** Record clean dialogue on-set. Capture subtle café ambience (distant conversations, clink of cups) as texture layer. No music; silence is the sound design.

**Props:** Two coffee cups (filled or empty), napkin, table, chairs. Person B may hold or fidget with cup.

---

**GENERATION_PROMPTS**

**SHOT 1 (Establishing) – ~5 seconds**
*Type: Wide two-shot, seated*  
*Framing: Both figures visible, table between them, café interior soft-focused behind. Slightly elevated angle to see both faces and the table surface clearly.*

Prompt:
"A cinematic wide shot of two adults sitting opposite each other at a small wooden café table in natural light. Camera is at seated eye-level, showing both figures in sharp focus, the table between them, warm neutral café walls behind them softly blurred. One person (left, female/male) sits with hands on table, looking directly at the other. The other person (right) sits slightly back, uncomfortable. Soft golden light from one direction. Film photograph aesthetic, 16:9, no motion. Tense, quiet intimacy."

---

**SHOT 2 (Person A – First Question) – ~6 seconds**  
*Type: Close-up, single subject*  
*Framing: Person A, slightly off-center, shoulders and head, café background bokeh. Expression moves from controlled to vulnerable as they ask.*

Prompt:
"Close-up portrait of a middle-aged person sitting in a café, shot at eye-level with shallow depth of field. Soft warm light on their face. Their expression is controlled but eyes show hurt. They are speaking quietly, leaning slightly forward. Shallow background blur of warm café interior. Cinematic, 16:9, film aesthetic. Restrained tension."

---

**SHOT 3 (Person B – Reaction and Deflection) – ~6 seconds**  
*Type: Close-up, single subject*  
*Framing: Person B, opposite angle from Shot 2, shallow focus. Expression defensive, eyes briefly evasive, then looking back.*

Prompt:
"Close-up of a different middle-aged person across from the first, sitting in the same café. Shot at eye-level with shallow focus. Their expression shifts from caught-off-guard to defensive. Jaw tightens subtly. They glance away briefly, then back. Soft warm light, blurred café background. Cinematic, 16:9, film aesthetic. Guarded, resistant."

---

**SHOT 4 (Person A – Pressing Further) – ~5 seconds**  
*Type: Close-up, single subject (Person A)*  
*Framing: Repeat angle of Shot 2 or slight variation. Stronger emotion now; the question is not a question but a statement.*

Prompt:
"Close-up of the first person in the café, same eye-level framing as before. Their expression is firmer now, less pleading. Slight intensity in the eyes. Speaking with controlled insistence. Shallow background blur. Warm light. Cinematic, 16:9. The softness is gone; resolve is present."

---

**SHOT 5 (Person B – Shutdown) – ~6 seconds**  
*Type: Close-up, single subject (Person B)*  
*Framing: Repeat angle of Shot 3 or slight variation. Expression closes. They withdraw emotionally.*

Prompt:
"Close-up of the second person in the café. Their expression has shifted to a wall—jaw set, eyes closed for a moment or looking down. They are not going to answer. Shallow focus, warm soft light, blurred café background. Cinematic, 16:9, film aesthetic. Withdrawn, trapped."

---

**SHOT 6 (Resolution Two-Shot or Return to Wide) – ~6 seconds**  
*Type: Two-shot or wide, both figures*  
*Framing: Return to a version of Shot 1's geography, or a new two-shot angle that shows both in a new spatial relationship. The fracture is now visible in their bodies.*

Prompt:
"Two adults sitting opposite at a café table. Camera at mid-distance, showing both figures. The first person is leaning back slightly, expression hardened. The second is looking down or away, closed. The moment hangs between them. Warm café light, soft background. Cinematic, 16:9, film aesthetic. The tension is now silence. No one moves."

---

**DIALOGUE (Approximate Timing)**

Shot 1-2: Person A speaks (2-3 sec into scene)  
"When were you going to tell me?"

Shot 3: Person B responds (deflects, 1 sec)  
"It's not... it's complicated."

Shot 4: Person A (presses, 1 sec)  
"After we were already in this?"

Shot 5: Person B (no answer, reaction only, ~2 sec silence + subtle words like "I couldn't" or just breath)

Shot 6: Both sit in silence (~3 sec)

---

**DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS**
- **Dialogue script:** Finalized above (non-generative; actors memorize and perform).
- **Shot list:** Six shots in sequence; order is fixed.
- **Camera positions:** Defined in framing notes; no improvisation.
- **Spatial logic:** Table, two chairs opposite; fixed throughout.
- **Color grading:** Warm-neutral, slightly desaturated; applied consistently in post.
- **Edit timing:** Cuts on lines or emotional beats; no montage or abstraction.

---

**AUDIO_AND_EDIT**

**Dialogue Recording:**
- Record each shot's dialogue cleanly with on-camera microphone or lavs.
- Prioritize clear delivery; actors perform takes until lines feel natural (not flat, not theatrical).
- Capture ambient café sound underneath (low level).

**Edit structure:**
- Cut on dialogue deliveries; favor reaction shots over speaker shots when emotion is strongest.
- No cross-dissolves; hard cuts maintain tension.
- Shot 1: 5 sec → Shot 2: 2 sec (question begins) → Shot 3: 2 sec (deflection) → Shot 4: 2 sec (push) → Shot 5: 3 sec (silence and micro-response) → Shot 6: 5 sec (stasis).
- Total with transitions: ~40 seconds.

**Sound design:**
- Dialogue in center channel, clean and intelligible.
- Subtle café ambience underneath (distant voices, distant clink), mixed very low (~-20dB below dialogue).
- No music.
- Silence in shots 5-6 is intentional and holds tension.

---

**FAILURE_PREVENTION**

1. **Spatial confusion:** Both actors in every close-up must be clearly on opposite sides of the table. Shoot close-ups at opposite angles (camera left for Person A, camera right for Person B) so viewer always knows who is where.

2. **Over-direction:** Actors must feel free to micro-perform (eye flickers, breath shifts). Do not demand broad gestures. The scene lives in restraint.

3. **Lighting inconsistency:** Ensure soft light direction does not flip between close-ups. Key light source should remain from the same direction throughout.

4. **Dialogue flatness:** Rehearse actors before shoot. The lines should feel inevitable, not recited. 

5. **Length creep:** Time each shot precisely during production. If a take is 7 seconds instead of 6, trim in edit or retake.

6. **Awkward cuts:** Do not cut mid-word. Cut on the end of phrases or during reaction beats (blinks, exhales).

7. **Background distraction:** Ensure café background is not busy. No movement behind actors. No other people visible.

---

**HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK**

✓ Exactly six shots: Confirmed in shot list.  
✓ Two people opposite at café table: Confirmed in all prompts.  
✓ Spatial clarity maintained: Each shot designed to show relationship; wide shots ground geography.  
✓ Tense, restrained dialogue: No shouting; subtext and silence carry tension.  
✓ No elaborate camera movement: All shots are static or minimal. No pans, tilts, dollies, or whips.  
✓ Cinematic aesthetic: Shallow focus, soft light, composition prioritized over technical coverage.  
✓ ~40 seconds target: Timing outlined above; achievable.  
✓ 16:9 aspect ratio: All prompts specify 16:9.  
✓ No external websites used: All direction and dialogue generated from brief analysis.
