# CONTEST V10 — B01

Use frozen brief B01 from the top of the final file.

## PACKAGE A

RESEARCH_NEEDS:
- How to make chaotic "before" moments visually/audibly legible and painful within a very short runtime, and how to pace a fast product-turn ad so it reads clearly on a small vertical screen.
- How to keep the tone authentically Indian/small-business rather than generic Western SaaS, including what actually motivates Indian SME buyers (so message emphasis matches their psychology, not aspirational brand imagery).
- What RentOk itself claims about its product, features, proof points and brand voice, since only rentok.com may be used for company/product facts.

Findings used: Google's ABCD guidance (HOLD status — treated only as generic, non-authoritative craft reference for pacing/hook/contrast, not cited as proven fact) confirmed conventional wisdom about fast open, sustained hook, and audio-led attention on small screens — used only to sanity-check pacing, not as a governing rule. Bijapurkar's value-orientation model (ACCEPTED) indicates Indian SME/trade buyers are typically "cost-benefit optimizers" who are "very hard to seduce with imagery-oriented brand benefits" — this directly shaped the decision to keep messaging concrete/tangible (time, money, specific pain points) rather than aspirational SaaS language. rentok.com snapshot supplied all product, feature, proof-point and brand-voice facts used below.

---

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
One vertical video ad, 9:16, ~30 seconds, for hostel/PG owners in India. Single edited master file (MP4), captioned (on-screen text baked in + separate SRT for platform auto-caption), Hindi-English (Hinglish) VO with English text overlays.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
The customer wants recognition, not education: owners must see their own daily mess (WhatsApp threads, notebooks, spreadsheets) reflected back accurately enough to feel "that's literally me," then experience relief when one system replaces it. The explicit creative risk to avoid is looking like a polished, placeless B2B SaaS explainer (isometric icons, sterile actors, corporate voiceover). The ad must read as shot in India, for Indian PG/hostel owners, using real textures of that business (steel almirahs, handwritten ledgers, rooftop water tanks, Hinglish speech, cash, Aadhar cards) — credible first, "clean tech" second.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"One day, two systems." Follow one PG/hostel owner through the exact chaos named in the brief — rent chasing, tenant records, KYC paperwork, a move-out, a complaint, compliance — building to a visible breaking point, then a hard visual/audio snap-cut into the same day re-run through RentOk: every one of those five threads resolved in a tap. The structural device is direct 1:1 payoff — each chaos beat in Act 1 gets its exact mirror-resolution in Act 2, so the improvement is legible, not generic. RentOk's own site language ("Why rent like the 1990s when you're in 2026?") validates that this old-vs-new frame is on-brand, not invented.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Recognition (0–8s): "This is my exact daily mess" — rent follow-ups, tenant papers, KYC, complaints, notebooks/spreadsheets/WhatsApp scattered.
2. Tension peak (8–11s): the cost of the mess — exhaustion, missed follow-up, a specific dropped ball (an overdue rent, an unresolved complaint).
3. Turn (11–13s): one system replaces all of it — the single strongest branding beat.
4. Proof-by-feature (13–24s): rent collection automated, KYC digital, complaints + move-outs tracked — each stated as a concrete, tangible outcome (time/money/certainty), per Indian SME buyers' known preference for tangible cost-benefit claims over aspirational imagery.
5. Confidence + credibility proof (24–27s): calmer owner, plus real scale numbers from rentok.com (15,000+ properties, 4.9★/675 reviews).
6. CTA (27–30s): brand lockup + "Get Free Demo" + rentok.com.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Look: documentary-realist, handheld, natural available light (mixed tungsten/daylight Indian interiors), not studio-clean. Slightly desaturated, warm-neutral grade for chaos section; grade opens up (more contrast, cooler clarity, slightly brighter whites) once RentOk appears — a graded "before/after" rather than a set-dressed one.
- Location: a real-feeling PG/hostel — reception desk with steel cupboard, wall calendar, tube light, rooftop water tank visible from balcony, common corridor with room numbers.
- Character: one Indian owner (40s, everyday clothing — half-sleeve shirt, no corporate blazer), a couple of tenant background actors (20s, students/working professionals — realistic PG tenant demographic).
- UI inserts: real phone-in-hand inserts of the RentOk app screen (rent reminders, KYC verification tick, complaint ticket, floor-plan room status) — treated as clean deterministic graphic inserts, not asked of the generative model to "imagine" UI.
- Typography: bold sans, high-contrast white-on-dark stamps for chaos-phase words (RENT / RECORDS / KYC / COMPLAINTS / COMPLIANCE), switching to RentOk's own clean app-style type once the turn happens. All safe-zone respecting for 9:16 (captions and CTA within middle-safe 80%).

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
Shoot-and-composite hybrid: live-action (or live-action-style generative) narrative shots for the human/environment beats, deterministic screen-recorded/UI-graphic inserts for all app footage and all on-screen numerals/stats, motion-graphics text overlays laid in post (not generated in-shot). Grade shifts from warm/flat to punchy/contrast at the turn cut (shot 4). Sound design and VO mixed separately and married in edit.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
(Shot-level, in order; each is an executable prompt for the video generation step. Total ≈30s across 9 shots.)

Shot 1 — HOOK (0:00–0:03, 3s)
Purpose: instant recognition of the chaos.
Prompt: "Extreme close-up, handheld, slightly shaky: a middle-aged Indian man's hand flips through a worn, ink-stained notebook ledger full of crossed-out rent entries, while a smartphone next to it buzzes with a stack of overlapping WhatsApp notification pops (blurred generic chat bubbles, no real logos). Warm tungsten light, slightly desaturated grade, shallow depth of field. Ambient sound: overlapping muffled voices, notification pings, a ceiling fan. No dialogue yet."

Shot 2 — ESCALATION (0:03–0:08, 5s)
Purpose: pile on the five named pain points fast.
Prompt: "Fast-cut handheld montage, real PG/hostel interior: (a) owner walking a corridor with phone pressed to ear, saying in Hindi-accented English/Hinglish 'Bhaiya, rent kal tak bhej dena...'; (b) a tenant handing over a folded Aadhar card and cash at a reception desk; (c) a wall calendar with move-out dates scribbled and crossed out; (d) a laptop screen with a messy spreadsheet, red overdue cells; (e) another tenant saying off-camera 'Uncle, complaint diya tha, koi reply nahi aaya.' Documentary energy, quick cuts on action, natural available light, rising ambient noise of a busy PG."

Shot 3 — BREAKING POINT (0:08–0:11, 3s)
Purpose: emotional low point, sets up the turn.
Prompt: "Medium shot, static camera: the same owner sits down heavily at his desk, exhales, rubs his temple, notebook falls open on the desk revealing messy crossed-out ledger lines. Sound drops to near-silence except a slow ceiling fan and a distant phone ring. Text stamps on screen, bold white sans, one word per beat: RENT — RECORDS — KYC — COMPLAINTS — COMPLIANCE, each landing with a soft thud sound."

Shot 4 — THE TURN (0:11–0:13, 2s)
Purpose: hard tonal snap from chaos to clean system.
Prompt: "Whip-pan or match-cut transition: the owner's thumb taps his phone screen; hard cut to a crisp, bright close-up of the RentOk app home screen lighting up (clean UI, real app screenshot composited in post, not generated). Music cuts from tense low drone to a confident upbeat rhythm on this frame. A single clean UI chime sound on the cut."

Shot 5 — SOLUTION: RENT (0:13–0:16, 3s)
Purpose: resolve pain point 1 (rent collection).
Prompt: "Close-up of phone in hand: owner taps 'Send Bulk WhatsApp Reminder' inside the RentOk app; a list of tenant names shows payment status ticking from red 'Due' to green 'Paid' in quick succession. Bright, clean, high-key light — contrast with earlier warm chaos grade. Text overlay: 'Rent collected automatically.' Confident upbeat music continues."

Shot 6 — SOLUTION: KYC (0:16–0:19, 3s)
Prompt: "Close-up: owner holds phone camera over a tenant's ID card (details generically blurred/fictional, no real personal data), app shows a scanning animation resolving to a green 'Verified' checkmark with the tenant's photo and basic details populating a clean digital form. Text overlay: 'KYC done digitally.' Clean bright light, same high-key look as Shot 5."

Shot 7 — SOLUTION: COMPLAINTS & MOVE-OUT (0:19–0:23, 4s)
Prompt: "Two quick beats: (a) owner taps a complaint ticket on-screen marking it 'Resolved' with a green check; (b) a floor-plan screen shows a room card flipping from 'Under Notice' to 'Vacant' with a clean animated transition. Text overlay: 'Complaints and move-outs, tracked.' Same bright, orderly visual language as prior solution shots."

Shot 8 — RESOLUTION / CALM (0:23–0:27, 4s)
Purpose: emotional payoff, credibility.
Prompt: "Medium wide shot, golden-hour light, rooftop of the PG/hostel with water tanks and city skyline in soft background blur: the same owner now relaxed, leaning on the railing, glancing at his phone with a small satisfied smile, sipping chai from a steel tumbler; two tenants chat easily in the background, no tension. Text overlay: 'One app for it all.'"

Shot 9 — LOGO/CTA (0:27–0:30, 3s)
Purpose: brand lockup and action.
Prompt: "Clean static end card, RentOk logo centered on white/light background, tagline beneath: 'Save time. Work less. Earn more.' Below it, proof line: 'Trusted by 15,000+ properties · 4.9★ (675 reviews).' Call to action button-style text: 'Get Free Demo — rentok.com'. Simple upbeat musical resolve, UI chime echo fades out."

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- All RentOk app UI screens (Shots 4–7) and the end-card stats/logo (Shot 9): sourced from real product screenshots/brand assets and composited in post as deterministic motion graphics — not left to the generative model to invent, since generated UI text/logos are unreliable and any invented numbers or feature claims would misrepresent the real product.
- Proof numbers (15,000+ properties, 4.9★, 675 reviews, "Save time. Work less. Earn more.") — pulled verbatim from rentok.com; typeset deterministically, not generated as in-scene text.
- Any tenant ID/Aadhar card visuals must use entirely fictional placeholder data, generated or typeset deterministically, never real personal data patterns.
- On-screen Hindi/Hinglish text and captions: typeset in post for spelling accuracy, not generated in-shot (generative video frequently garbles Devanagari/Hinglish text).

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- VO/dialogue: Hinglish, colloquial, spoken by a native-sounding Indian voice actor matching the owner's on-screen register (not a corporate narrator). Sample lines: "Bhaiya, rent kal tak bhej dena..." (chaos) → post-turn line "Ab sab kuch ek jagah." → closing line "RentOk. Kaam kam, kamai zyada."
- Music: contemporary Indian-inflected track — tabla/percussion layered under a modern beat; tense/sparse under chaos (Shots 1–3), hard cut to upbeat confident version at the turn (Shot 4 onward).
- SFX: notification pings, overlapping voice murmur, page flips, phone ring in chaos section; single clean UI chime on the turn; ambient chai-cup clink and rooftop wind in resolution.
- Edit pace: fast cuts (0.5–1.5s) through chaos section to feel overwhelming; slightly longer, calmer holds (1.5–2s) in solution/resolution section to feel orderly — the cutting rhythm itself dramatizes "chaos vs system."
- Captions burned in for sound-off viewing (majority of vertical feed consumption), safe-zone respecting.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Risk: generative video renders illegible/garbled app UI or Devanagari text → mitigated by compositing real UI and typeset text in post (see Deterministic section).
- Risk: result reads as generic global SaaS ad → mitigated by specific Indian production detail (steel almirah, rooftop water tanks, chai tumbler, Hinglish dialogue, real PG visual clutter) and by avoiding corporate-actor styling, stock "diverse team in blazers" tropes, and isometric icon graphics.
- Risk: chaos section feels cartoonish/mocking rather than recognisable → mitigated by grounding every chaos beat in the customer's own named pain points (rent, records, KYC, move-outs, complaints, compliance) rather than invented generic mess.
- Risk: privacy/legal exposure from showing realistic ID documents → mitigated by mandating fictional placeholder data on any ID/KYC visual.
- Risk: pacing loses viewers before the turn on a sound-off vertical feed → mitigated by strong visual word-stamps (Shot 3) and burned-in captions throughout, so the hook and message survive without audio.
- Risk: overclaiming features not on the website (e.g., implying insurance/zero-deposit or legal services not requested by the brief) → mitigated by restricting feature claims strictly to the five pain points named in the brief and only the matching rentok.com-confirmed features (Autopay/reminders, Digital KYC/verification, Complaint Management, room status/move-out tracking).

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Vertical 9:16: yes, framing and safe zones specified throughout.
- ~30 seconds: yes, 9 shots timed to sum to 30s.
- Only rentok.com used for company/product/brand facts: yes — all feature names, stats, and tagline language sourced from the frozen snapshot; no other external brand claims used.
- Chaos made "painfully recognisable" then resolved by RentOk as clean system: yes — 1:1 mirrored structure (Shots 1–3 vs 5–7) built directly from the customer's five named pain points.
- Contemporary, credible, Indian, not generic Silicon Valley SaaS explainer: yes — documentary realism, Hinglish dialogue, specific Indian small-business visual texture, avoids stock-SaaS aesthetic; supported by Bijapurkar's finding that this buyer segment responds to tangible cost-benefit claims, not aspirational imagery, which shaped the concrete, feature-literal messaging over abstract "transformation" language.
- No model/provider recommended: confirmed, none specified.
- Single concept only: confirmed, one package delivered.

## PACKAGE B

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
One vertical 9:16 video ad, ~30 seconds, for RentOK, targeting hostel/PG (and adjacent co-living/flat) owners in India. Single cut, native-vertical for Reels/Shorts/Status placement, designed to work both with sound and captioned sound-off.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
The brief is a transformation ad, not a feature-tour ad: make the *lived, specific* chaos of running a PG/hostel on WhatsApp, notebooks and spreadsheets so recognisable that an owner watching feels seen and slightly exposed — then resolve that exact chaos, beat for beat, inside RentOK. "Contemporary, credible, Indian, not generic Silicon Valley SaaS" is read as an instruction about *texture*: real Tier‑2/3 PG geography, Hinglish speech, documentary imperfection in the "before" half, not stock-agency gloss, aspirational lighting, or an English voiceover reading generic value props.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"1990s mein chalayenge, ya 2026 mein?" — built directly from RentOK's own site line, "Why rent like 1990s when you are in 2026?" A real-looking PG/hostel owner, mid-scramble, talks and acts his way through the chaos of rent-chasing, notebooks and lost KYC papers, shot handheld/documentary. A single hard cut/snap at the midpoint flips the same owner, same rooftop, into the calm of RentOK's actual app doing the same jobs automatically. The pivot line is spoken, not just written, so it lands as a credible Indian small-business rebuke rather than a slogan card.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Recognition (0–15s): "This is literally what I do every month" — WhatsApp chasing, notebook ledger, KYC photocopies, unresolved complaint.
2. Reframe (15–18s): "There's a 2026 way to do this" — the website's own contrast line, spoken as a real thought, not a jingle.
3. Resolution (18–24s): RentOK doing the exact same four jobs — automatic rent/WhatsApp reminders, digital KYC verify/reject, complaint marked resolved, floor plan at a glance — shown as real product UI, not illustrated abstraction.
4. Proof (24–27s): hard numbers from RentOK's own site (15,000+ properties, 3 Lakh+ rooms, 2.3 Lakh+ tenants, ₹100 Cr+ collection) — specifics, not adjectives, to carry credibility with a cost‑conscious owner audience.
5. Action (27–30s): logo lockup + "Get free demo" + site tagline "Save time. Work less. Earn more."

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Before-half (0–15s): handheld phone-camera look, slightly wide/distorted angle, mixed practical light (tube light, courtyard daylight, one bare bulb at night if a night beat is used), real PG textures — steel almirah, cement stairwell, water tank, plastic chairs, a torn spiral notebook, a cracked-screen laptop. No colour grade polish; slight motion blur and handheld shake are intentional signal of authenticity, not a defect.
- Pivot (15–18s): a single hard cut (no dissolve) on the spoken line — same actor, same rooftop, camera now on a small stabiliser, slightly cleaner light (same source, tidied frame).
- After-half (18–24s): same location family, calmer camera, but the *screen content* is the star — real RentOK app UI in the owner's hand, thumb tapping, WhatsApp reminder actually sending, a document turning from "pending" to "verified" — high-contrast, legible even on a small muted phone screen.
- Proof + CTA (24–30s): flat brand card, RentOK typography/colours as used on rentok.com, numerals set large and bold (specifics-forward, no exclamation-heavy copy).
- Typography throughout: burned-in bilingual captions (Hindi Devanagari + English), bold sans, high contrast against footage, kept inside a safe zone clear of platform UI (like/comment column, caption bar).

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
- Cast: one male or female owner-type actor, 35–55, Tier‑2/3 city look and Hinglish delivery (not polished newsreader English) — this is the single most important casting decision for credibility.
- Locations: one real or dressed PG/hostel rooftop + corridor/stairwell, used for both halves to make the transformation legible as "same place, same person, different system."
- Shoot the "before" half on a phone/handheld rig for authentic imperfection; shoot the "after" half's human beats on a light stabiliser; capture the app UI beats as genuine screen recordings of the RentOK app/site (not AI-recreated interface), to keep every claim about the product literally true.
- Edit on a single hard cut at the pivot line; everything before and after uses quick cuts (0.8–1.5s per beat) to match how the audience actually scrolls Reels.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
(8 shots, ~30s total; shots 1–5 and 8 are generative/live-action production prompts, shot 6 is real product-screen capture, shot 7 is a motion-graphics card — see DETERMINISTIC section.)

SHOT 1 — HOOK (0:00–0:03, purpose: pattern-interrupt open, stop the scroll)
Prompt: "Vertical 9:16 handheld phone-camera footage, documentary realism, slight motion blur. An Indian PG/hostel owner, 40s, Hinglish-speaking, stands in a cramped hostel corridor holding his phone up close to his own face, exasperated, scrolling frantically through a wall of WhatsApp chat bubbles looking for a rent payment. Practical tube-light lighting, unpolished, real environment with steel cupboards and a notice board in the background. He mutters under his breath in Hindi-English, visibly stressed."

SHOT 2 — CHAOS BEAT 1 (0:03–0:07, purpose: WhatsApp leakage)
Prompt: "Vertical 9:16 handheld documentary shot, close on hands thumb-scrolling a phone through dozens of unread WhatsApp chats and voice notes from different tenants, timestamps visible, notification badges piling up. Cut to the owner's face, tired, saying half to himself 'Isko bola tha, usko nahi bola... yaad hi nahi hai.' Real Indian mid-size hostel setting, imperfect natural light."

SHOT 3 — CHAOS BEAT 2 (0:07–0:11, purpose: notebook/ledger leakage)
Prompt: "Vertical 9:16 handheld shot of a worn spiral notebook rent ledger on a plastic table, pages crossed out and re-written, a chai cup ring stain on one page, the owner flipping back several pages trying to find who paid this month, frustrated sigh, documentary lighting, real hostel office corner with a ceiling fan and old calendar on the wall."

SHOT 4 — CHAOS BEAT 3 (0:11–0:15, purpose: KYC/complaint leakage)
Prompt: "Vertical 9:16 handheld shot, owner rummaging through a loose stack of photocopied ID documents and a crumpled paper complaint note on a steel shelf, a tenant in the background waiting impatiently at the door for a resolved complaint that clearly hasn't moved, real PG stairwell/corridor setting, unglamorous practical lighting."

SHOT 5 — PIVOT (0:15–0:18, purpose: reframe, spoken tagline)
Prompt: "Vertical 9:16, same actor and same rooftop location as before, camera now steadier (light stabiliser), slightly cleaner framing and light. He looks directly at camera, half-smiling, and says in Hindi-English: '1990s mein chalayenge... ya 2026 mein?' Hard cut on the last word into the next shot."

SHOT 6 — SOLUTION (0:18–0:24, purpose: product resolution, real UI) — see DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS; not generated.

SHOT 7 — PROOF (0:24–0:27, purpose: credibility via specifics) — motion graphics card, not generated footage; see DETERMINISTIC section.

SHOT 8 — CTA (0:27–0:30, purpose: close, brand lockup, action)
Prompt: "Vertical 9:16 clean flat brand card, RentOK logo centred on a light background using RentOK's own brand colours, tagline 'Save time. Work less. Earn more.' beneath it, a bold 'Get Free Demo' button-style graphic, subtle upward-motion animation on the logo, no live-action content."

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- Shot 6 (app resolution beat) must be a genuine screen recording of the actual RentOK product/app captured live — showing an automatic WhatsApp rent reminder being sent, a KYC document flipping from pending to verified, a complaint marked resolved, and the floor-plan view — intercut with the owner's hand/thumb on his phone. Do not generate synthetic UI; the claims must match the real product exactly, since UI and feature claims are brand-sensitive.
- Shot 7 (proof card) is a designed motion-graphics overlay using the exact figures published on rentok.com: 15,000+ Properties, 3 Lakh+ Rooms, 2.3 Lakh+ Tenants, ₹100 Cr+ Collection. No invented or rounded-up statistic beyond what the site states.
- Logo, brand colours, typography and the two taglines ("Why rent like 1990s when you are in 2026?" and "Save time. Work less. Earn more.") are taken verbatim/near-verbatim from rentok.com and must be reproduced as on the site, not reinterpreted.
- All burned-in captions are a deterministic text/graphics layer added in edit, not part of the generated footage.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- VO/dialogue: Hinglish, delivered by the same on-camera actor, colloquial and slightly weary in the chaos half, wry and confident on the pivot line, absent (UI sound only) in the solution half except for one closing VO line under the CTA: "RentOk — sab kuch, ek jagah." 
- SFX: notification pings and paper-rustle/chai-stain sounds in the chaos half, a single sharp whoosh/snap on the hard cut, clean soft UI chimes (message-sent, checkmark) in the solution half.
- Music: a contemporary Indian instrumental/lo-fi-with-tabla-or-dhol-accent bed, low in the chaos half, lifting in energy at the pivot — deliberately not a generic corporate synth bed, to avoid the "Silicon Valley explainer" register.
- Captions: bilingual (Devanagari + English) burned in throughout for sound-off viewing, bold high-contrast type, positioned inside platform-safe margins.
- Edit pace: quick cuts (0.8–1.5s) through the chaos beats, one clean hard cut at the pivot, slightly slower holds (1.5–2s) in the solution half so the UI is actually readable.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Risk: chaos footage reads as low-production-value rather than intentional documentary style → mitigate with confident, consistent handheld language and real locations, not random shake; the imperfection must look chosen, not accidental.
- Risk: pain points tip into caricature and lose credibility with the actual owner audience → keep each vignette specific and mundane (a stained ledger page, a genuinely full WhatsApp thread) rather than exaggerated slapstick.
- Risk: casting an actor who reads as urban-elite/English-medium breaks the "credible Indian" requirement → cast and direct specifically for Tier‑2/3 Hinglish cadence.
- Risk: AI-generated or illustrated fake UI in the solution half misrepresents the product → solved by mandating real screen capture (see DETERMINISTIC section).
- Risk: captions unreadable at small size on mobile with mixed script → enforce bold, high-contrast type and safe-margin placement clear of platform overlays.
- Risk: overclaiming stats → use only the exact figures published on rentok.com.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Vertical 9:16: ✔ specified throughout.
- ~30 seconds: ✔ shot list sums to 30s.
- Audience: Indian hostel/PG owners: ✔ casting, setting, language.
- Chaos made painfully recognisable (WhatsApp, notebooks, spreadsheets, KYC, move-outs, complaints, compliance): ✔ WhatsApp, notebook ledger, KYC photocopies, unresolved complaint explicitly dramatized; compliance/move-outs folded into KYC/complaint beats given 30s runtime constraint.
- RentOK positioned as the clean system: ✔ pivot + solution half.
- Contemporary, credible, Indian, not generic SaaS: ✔ documentary texture, Hinglish, real locations, Indian music bed.
- Only rentok.com used for company/product/brand facts: ✔ all features, stats, taglines sourced from the site snapshot; no other source used for claims about RentOK.
- Single concept delivered: ✔ one package, no alternatives offered.

## PACKAGE C

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
One vertical video ad, 9:16, ~30 seconds (target 30s, acceptable 28–32s), 1080×1920, 25fps, master with burned-in captions (India social feeds are usually watched muted). Language: Hinglish VO + English/Hindi-script on-screen supers. Single finished cut — no alternate versions.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
RentOk needs a performance-style brand ad that does two jobs in 30 seconds: (1) make the *specific* daily operational chaos of running a PG/hostel — rent follow-up, KYC/verification, complaints, move-outs — instantly recognisable and slightly uncomfortable to an owner watching on their phone, and (2) resolve that chaos into RentOk as one calm system, using only features and proof points RentOk itself publishes (autopay + WhatsApp reminders, digital KYC/tenant verification, complaint management, tenant ledger/floor plan, 15,000+ properties / 100 Cr+ collection / 4.9 rating). The ad must read as Indian and lived-in — steel almirah, cot register, tube light, WhatsApp pings, cash counting — not a polished global SaaS explainer with abstract icons and stock actors.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"Notebook se App tak" (From the notebook to the app). The first half of the film is shot handheld, cluttered, slightly frantic — a real PG/hostel owner physically drowning in paper ledgers, loose KYC photocopies, WhatsApp notification pile-ups and hand-written complaint chits. At the exact moment of overwhelm, a single match-cut turns the messy notebook page into the RentOk app dashboard, and the film changes register completely: locked-off camera, clean light, one hand, one phone, everything resolved in taps. The chaos and the solution are built as three exact pairs (rent chasing → Autopay, paper KYC → Digital KYC, ignored complaints → Complaint Management), so every pain shown has its named fix shown, in the same order — nothing decorative, nothing invented beyond what RentOk's own site states.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Hook (recognition): "This is your day right now."
2. Three concrete pains, one at a time, each with its own super: Rent follow-up → KYC/verification → Complaints.
3. Turn: the pain becomes too much — visual and audio break.
4. Reveal: RentOk, one app, shown solving the *same three* pains in the *same order*.
5. Proof: trust numbers (15,000+ properties, 100 Cr+ collected, 4.9★).
6. CTA: "Get Free Demo" + brand lockup.
Only one message per beat; nothing is introduced in the solution half that wasn't set up as a problem half. Brand mark appears at the match-cut (roughly mid-film) and stays on-screen (as phone UI + logo) through to the end, rather than only flashing at the very end.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Chaos half: handheld, slightly wide lens, mixed practical light (tube light + window daylight), desaturated/slightly warm-yellow grade, fast but legible cuts, tight framing on hands/paper/phone.
- Turn: a beat of stillness — audio drops, single held frame — then a hard, deliberate match-cut (notebook page edge → app card edge) into the second half.
- Solution half: static/locked tripod shots, cooler-neutral clean grade, generous negative space, one phone in one steady hand, real app-UI screen inserts composited in, calm pacing.
- Location: a real-feeling PG/hostel — corridor with room-number boards, steel cupboard, cot, tin trunk, ceiling fan, tulsi plant or calendar on wall — never a generic "office."
- Talent: an Indian PG/hostel owner, 40s–50s, everyday clothing (not corporate formalwear), naturalistic and slightly harried performance in the chaos half, relieved/confident in the solution half. Performance should read as under-directed/real rather than polished-presenter delivery, since a slightly rough, unpolished performance reads as more credible for this owner-testimonial register than an ad-actor delivery (this is a directional, not certified, craft choice — see Knowledge section).
- On-screen supers: large, high-contrast, one idea per card, safe-margined for 9:16 UI overlays (avoid top/bottom 12% where platform UI sits).

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
1. Shoot/generate the live-action performance half (chaos + owner reaction) as continuous vertical coverage, handheld, in one practical location, 3–4 short setups.
2. Capture the RentOk product UI as real screen recordings from the live app/website (autopay reminder screen, digital KYC verify/reject screen, complaint ticket resolve screen, floor-plan view, dues report) — these are deterministic brand assets, not generated.
3. Composite the UI recordings into a phone-in-hand plate shot in the same clean solution-half lighting.
4. Build the match-cut transition (paper texture morphing into UI card) as a single dedicated transition asset.
5. Add motion-graphics end card (logo, tagline, stats, CTA button) as a deterministic template, not generative.
6. Grade chaos half warm/handheld-textured, solution half clean/neutral; mix VO, music and SFX per Audio section; export 1080×1920, burned captions.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
Shot 1 — HOOK (0:00–0:04, purpose: instant recognition of chaos)
"Vertical 9:16 handheld shot, tight framing, inside a modest Indian PG/hostel owner's small office corner — tube light, steel almirah, wall calendar. A 40s Indian man in home-casual kurta/shirt sits at a cluttered table stacked with handwritten rent ledgers, loose photocopies and a buzzing phone showing stacked WhatsApp notification pop-ups. He rubs his forehead, phone buzzes again. Fast, slightly shaky camera move-in. Warm practical light, slightly desaturated grade. No dialogue, ambient sound only."

Shot 2 — RENT CHAOS (0:04–0:09, purpose: pain #1)
"Vertical handheld close-ups: a hand crossing out and rewriting due dates in a paper rent register; a finger scrolling a long WhatsApp thread of unanswered 'Sir rent kab tak?' messages; cash notes being recounted on the table. Quick cuts, natural light through a window grille. On-screen super appears: 'Rent yaad rakhna, follow-up karna... har mahine.' Hinglish VO (male, tired, conversational): 'Rent kaun bhoola, kiska due hai — sab yaad rakhna padta hai.'"

Shot 3 — KYC CHAOS (0:09–0:13, purpose: pain #2)
"Vertical shot: a loose stack of photocopied ID proofs and a handwritten tenant register spilling out of a tin trunk; owner flips through pages looking for one tenant's police-verification form, can't find it, sighs. Handheld, tight framing. Super: 'KYC records kahin bhi, kabhi bhi.' VO: 'Naya tenant aaya — KYC dhoondo, verify karo, sambhalo.'"

Shot 4 — COMPLAINTS CHAOS (0:13–0:16, purpose: pain #3)
"Vertical shot: a wall with several hand-written complaint chits pinned and curling at the edges (leaking tap, no water, broken fan); phone shows an angry tenant WhatsApp voice-note icon and unread badge count climbing. Quick cut. Super: 'Complaints jama hote jaate hain.' VO: 'Complaint aati hai, kahin likh dete ho — aur bhool jaate ho.'"

Shot 5 — THE TURN (0:16–0:19, purpose: overwhelm → pivot)
"Vertical shot: owner exhales, phone rings again, he closes his eyes for one still beat — audio and music drop to near-silence. Then: extreme close-up on the notebook page edge; hard match-cut as the paper texture morphs into the edge of a clean phone-app card (same warm-to-cool colour transition). No VO across the silence; a single soft whoosh SFX carries the cut."

Shot 6a — SOLUTION: AUTOPAY (0:19–0:21.5, purpose: resolve pain #1)
"Vertical locked-off shot, clean neutral light, one hand holding a phone showing the real RentOk app: an automatic WhatsApp rent-reminder message sending itself with a payment link, then a 'Paid' tick appearing. Super: 'Rent apne aap collect.' VO (confident, warm): 'RentOk se rent apne aap aata hai — WhatsApp pe reminder, seedha payment link.'"

Shot 6b — SOLUTION: DIGITAL KYC (0:21.5–0:24, purpose: resolve pain #2)
"Same clean setup: phone screen shows RentOk's tenant verification flow — an ID document being reviewed with a single tap 'Verify' turning green. Super: 'KYC ek tap mein.' VO: 'Naye tenant ka KYC — digital, turant, sahi.'"

Shot 6c — SOLUTION: COMPLAINTS (0:24–0:27, purpose: resolve pain #3)
"Same clean setup: phone screen shows a complaint ticket in the RentOk app moving from 'Open' to 'Resolved' with one tap, tenant gets a WhatsApp confirmation. Super: 'Complaint solve, ek tap mein.' VO: 'Complaint aayi, solve hui — sab app ke andar.'"

Shot 7 — END CARD (0:27–0:30, purpose: brand + CTA)
"Vertical clean end card, RentOk logo centred, tagline beneath: 'Save time. Work less. Earn more.' Stat line: '15,000+ properties · ₹100 Cr+ collected · 4.9★ rated.' Large CTA button: 'Get Free Demo.' VO (final line): 'RentOk. Kaam kam, kamai zyada.' Soft uplifting music resolves on the final chord."

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- RentOk logo, wordmark, exact tagline text, and stat figures (15,000+ properties, 100 Cr+ collection, 4.9/675 reviews) — pulled verbatim from rentok.com, must not be paraphrased into different numbers.
- All in-app UI shown (rent reminder screen, KYC verify screen, complaint ticket screen, floor plan) — real screen recordings/brand-accurate mockups of the actual RentOk product, composited in; not to be freely generated, to avoid inventing a fictional UI under a real brand name.
- End-card CTA button and legal footer (if required) — templated motion graphics.
- Any depiction of chat bubbles in the chaos half should use a generic green messaging-app look rather than reproducing Meta's exact WhatsApp trademarked interface; the word "WhatsApp" itself can be used in VO/supers since RentOk's own site names WhatsApp as an integration.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- VO: single Hinglish voice, conversational and slightly weary in the chaos half, warm and relieved in the solution half — not a corporate announcer register.
- Music: chaos half — low, slightly anxious tabla/percussion loop with rising tempo; hard drop to near-silence at the turn (0:16–0:19); solution half — clean, optimistic, simple melodic bed (light guitar/synth), building to a confident final chord under the end card.
- SFX: paper rustle, cash counting, phone notification pings and message pop sounds in chaos half; single soft whoosh on the match-cut; gentle UI tap/chime sounds in solution half.
- Cut rate: fast (roughly 1–2s per shot) in the chaos half to sustain a jump-in feel; noticeably slower, held shots (2.5–3s) in the solution half so the product is legible.
- Captions burned in throughout (muted-autoplay standard for feed placements), matched to VO timing, large and high-contrast.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Risk: reading as generic global SaaS. Mitigation: locked to specific Indian PG textures (steel trunk, tube light, cot register, Hinglish VO, WhatsApp-style chat, INR cash) rather than abstract icon graphics.
- Risk: mocking or humiliating the owner. Mitigation: chaos is shown as relatable overwhelm, not incompetence; the owner is sympathetic throughout, never the joke.
- Risk: over-claiming. Mitigation: every feature and number shown is limited strictly to what rentok.com states (Autopay, WhatsApp reminders, Digital KYC/Tenant Verification, Complaint Management, floor plan, stats) — no invented pricing or capabilities.
- Risk: brand/UI inaccuracy. Mitigation: UI inserts sourced from the real product/screen recordings, not generated from imagination; logo and stats copied verbatim.
- Risk: message dilution from packing too many features in 30s. Mitigation: hard-capped to exactly three problem→feature pairs plus the stat/CTA end card; nothing else added.
- Risk: illegible captions/UI on small vertical phone screens. Mitigation: large supers, safe margins clear of platform UI zones, held solution shots long enough to read.
- Risk: trademark exposure from replicating WhatsApp's exact interface. Mitigation: stylised generic green-chat look, brand name used only in text/VO.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Vertical 9:16: yes, specified throughout.
- ~30 seconds: yes, 7-shot structure totals 30s.
- Single concept, no alternates: yes, one creative idea delivered.
- Only rentok.com used for company/product/brand facts: yes — all features, tagline, and stats are taken from the site snapshot; no other brand source used.
- Chaos made painfully recognisable, then resolved into RentOk as the system: yes, structurally central to the idea.
- Contemporary, credible, Indian, not generic SV SaaS: yes, addressed in Visual System and Failure Prevention.
- No model/provider recommended: none named.

## JUDGE V10
Apply the Stage-1 rubric. Choose exactly one A/B/C winner.

# CONTEST V11 — B04

## PACKAGE A

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
One 9:16 vertical video, ~25 seconds, single continuous "creator" persona, direct-to-camera + handheld product demo, captioned, delivered as one finished cut (no alternate versions).

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
The customer needs performance-style short-form video that reads as organic creator content (UGC) on Instagram Reels/YouTube Shorts, not a studio TVC, for a fictional Indian D2C brand's fragrance-free 2% salicylic-acid serum aimed at oily, blemish-prone adult skin. Success = a viewer believes a real person is genuinely recommending a product she uses, receives exactly one clear, honest reason to buy, and can act on it — while the packaging stays legible and the brand-safety/legal boundary (no invented clinical claims, no guarantees, no medical claims) is respected throughout.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"The one thing I actually needed to hear." A woman with oily, blemish-prone skin talks to camera the way she would to a friend: she's tried actives that either did nothing or irritated her skin, and this serum's single distinguishing virtue — a 2% salicylic-acid formula that's fragrance-free, lightweight and doesn't feel harsh — is the one reason she's stuck with it. No before/after, no percentage-improvement claims, no dermatologist voice-over: just one credible, specific, sensorial reason, demonstrated live.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Hook (who this is for): "if you've got oily, breakout-prone skin" — instant relevance, 0–3s.
2. Proof-by-demonstration: she actually applies it on camera (texture, absorption, no shine left behind) — 3–9s.
1a/anchor. The single purchase reason, stated once, plainly: fragrance-free 2% salicylic acid, formulated for oily/blemish-prone skin, doesn't sting or leave a film — 9–16s.
3. Product identification: clean legible pack shot so the brand/product is recognizable — 16–21s.
4. Call to action: casual, low-pressure sign-off pointing to purchase — 21–25s.
Only one claim is allowed to be the "reason"; everything else is texture/credibility support.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Aspect: 9:16, full-bleed, phone-shot aesthetic (slight handheld micro-movement throughout, no gimbal-smooth moves).
- Setting: home bathroom counter or bedroom vanity with real morning window light — believable Indian urban household, warm neutral tones, slightly cluttered-but-tidy counter (real toothbrush, other bottles) to signal "lived-in," not a set.
- Talent: one Indian woman, late-20s, everyday styling, minimal/no visible makeup, oily-prone but not medically inflamed skin (no depiction of active lesions/medical treatment), natural hair, casual homewear.
- Lighting: soft, direction-consistent daylight (window key + bounce fill), matching Light & Magic's reflection-control logic — the glass/plastic serum bottle should be lit so its highlight sits inside a "family of angles" that reads as clean glass rather than blown-out glare, and kept consistent between the demo shot and the pack-shot insert so the product doesn't visually "jump" between takes.
- Color: neutral warm-naturalistic grade, minimal stylization — deliberately not glossy/commercial-grade, consistent with UGC credibility.
- Framing: mix of tight direct-to-camera (chest-up) and true macro inserts (dropper, fingertip, cheek) — tight framing and a mid-action or close-up opening are used deliberately to hook attention fast without an artificial "ad" cold-open.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
Format: 5 shots, vertical, ~25s total, edited with hard/jump cuts (not dissolves) to preserve amateur-creator rhythm.

GENERATION_PROMPTS

SHOT 1 — HOOK (0:00–0:03, ~3s)
Purpose: stop-scroll relevance hook, opens mid-sentence/mid-action.
Prompt: "Vertical 9:16 handheld phone-style selfie shot, slight natural shake, indoor bathroom with soft warm window daylight. An Indian woman late-20s, minimal makeup, casual home clothing, holds a small serum bottle up near her face, mid-sentence, direct eye contact with lens. Tight chest-up framing. Naturalistic color, no cinematic grade, looks like an authentic phone-shot video, not an advertisement." 
Dialogue (sync): "Okay if you've got oily, breakout-prone skin like me — you need to hear this."

SHOT 2 — DEMO / TEXTURE INSERT (0:03–0:09, ~6s)
Purpose: visual proof, sensorial credibility.
Prompt: "Vertical 9:16 close-up macro insert, handheld, soft daylight from a window camera-left. Same Indian woman's hand unscrews the cap of a small dropper serum bottle, squeezes one drop onto her fingertip, then pats it onto her cheek/jawline. Lightweight clear-gel serum texture visible, absorbing quickly, no greasy sheen left on skin. Natural skin texture retained, no glossy studio finish, authentic amateur framing with minor handheld drift."
Dialogue (sync, continuing over insert): "It's a two-percent salicylic acid serum — but it's fragrance-free, so it doesn't sting or smell strong."

SHOT 3 — TALKING HEAD / THE REASON (0:09–0:16, ~7s)
Purpose: deliver the single clear purchase reason.
Prompt: "Vertical 9:16 handheld selfie-style medium close-up, same woman, same bathroom setting, consistent lighting and wardrobe continuity from Shot 1. She talks directly to camera, casual conversational energy, natural hand gestures, slight camera micro-shake, unpolished creator-video look."
Dialogue (sync): "That's the one thing that actually works for me — it treats the oily, breakout stuff without irritating my skin or leaving any smell behind. That's it, that's the whole reason."

SHOT 4 — PRODUCT INSERT (0:16–0:21, ~5s)
Purpose: clean product identification / pack recognition.
Prompt: "Vertical 9:16 close static-handheld insert shot, product resting upright on a bathroom counter in soft natural window light, label facing camera and fully legible, minimal styling, one hand enters frame to pick it up and turn it slightly toward camera. Lighting consistent with prior shots so bottle highlight and glass reflection match earlier close-up — no harsh glare, no blown highlight on the label. Realistic, non-commercial framing."
Dialogue (sync, optional voice carry-over): "Fragrance-free, made for oily and blemish-prone skin."

SHOT 5 — CTA / SIGN-OFF (0:21–0:25, ~4s)
Purpose: casual, low-pressure call to action.
Prompt: "Vertical 9:16 handheld selfie shot, same setting and wardrobe continuity, woman holds the bottle beside her face, casual smile, direct to camera, natural light, authentic unpolished creator sign-off energy."
Dialogue (sync): "Yeah — I'm keeping this one. Link's below if you want to try it."

SHOT ORDER: 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 5, hard cuts only, total ≈25s.

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- Real/approved brand packaging asset (label artwork, bottle shape, brand name/logo) supplied by the brand and composited/matched rather than freely generated, so label text, brand name and ingredient listing are exact and legally accurate — not left to generative guesswork.
- Burned-in captions (auto-caption style, matching UGC convention) for accessibility and scroll-sound-off viewing, synced to dialogue exactly as written above.
- End-card/CTA text overlay in final ~1s freeze or hold: brand name + generic purchase prompt (e.g., "Shop now" / handle), styled as a simple platform-native sticker, not a broadcast lower-third.
- Any on-screen ingredient callout ("2% Salicylic Acid," "Fragrance-Free") shown as text must mirror only the actual formulation facts already given — no numeric outcome claims added.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- Dialogue: single continuous natural read across all 5 shots, casual conversational pacing, minor imperfections (small pauses, informal phrasing) intentionally kept in.
- Room tone: light natural ambience (bathroom echo/room tone), phone-mic quality — not a clean boom recording — to preserve authenticity.
- SFX: soft cap-unscrew and dropper-squeeze sound in Shot 2, kept subtle and diegetic.
- Music: none, or a very low trending-style bed under Shots 4–5 only, kept under dialogue level — never masking speech (per platform-audio-reliance caution below).
- Edit: hard jump cuts between all shots, no dissolves/wipes, mild handheld continuity break between cuts (expected/typical of UGC, not a flaw), captions burned in.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- No invented statistics, before/after percentages, "clinically proven," "dermatologist recommended," guarantee language, or medical/therapeutic claims anywhere in dialogue or on-screen text — only the stated formulation facts (2% salicylic acid, fragrance-free) and subjective sensory language ("doesn't sting," "doesn't feel greasy").
- Do not depict active medical-grade acne lesions, treatment devices, or implied dermatological procedure — keep skin depiction realistic but non-clinical.
- Keep product label/bottle geometry and highlight position consistent across Shots 2 and 4 so the pack doesn't visually "shift" between inserts (continuity, not just realism).
- Avoid over-polished lighting/grade that reads as a TVC — resist the instinct to smooth skin, add glamour lighting, or use a cinematic LUT; the deliberately "amateur" handheld quality is a credibility feature, not a defect, consistent with testimonial-credibility guidance that overly polished delivery reads as less trustworthy.
- Watch for common generative-hand/product artifacts during the dropper/application close-up (extra fingers, bottle geometry warping) — this insert should get the most QC scrutiny.
- Ensure captions match spoken claim exactly (no caption drift into stronger claims than the audio states).

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- 9:16 vertical: met.
- ~25s total: met (5 shots, 3+6+7+5+4 = 25s).
- One believable Indian woman, direct to camera: met, single continuous talent across all shots.
- Natural product demonstration: met (Shot 2 dropper/application insert).
- One clear purchase reason communicated: met — "fragrance-free 2% salicylic acid, doesn't irritate/no strong smell, for oily blemish-prone skin," stated once as the explicit reason.
- No invented clinical statistics, guarantees, or medical claims: met — only formulation facts and subjective sensory language used; explicitly flagged in Failure Prevention.
- Feels like credible creator content, not a polished TVC: addressed via handheld camera, imperfect audio, jump cuts, minimal grade, casual talent styling.
- Visually controlled and commercially usable: addressed via lighting/highlight continuity on packaging, legible label, consistent wardrobe/setting, brand-safe language.
- No external website used: confirmed, none consulted.
- Fictional Indian D2C brand, fragrance-free 2% salicylic acid serum for oily/blemish-prone adults: reflected in dialogue and product insert.

## PACKAGE B

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
One 9:16 vertical UGC-style performance video, target length ≈25 seconds, single deliverable (no alternates). Fictional D2C skincare brand: "Tvacha Skin," product: "Tvacha Clear Base — 2% Salicylic Acid Serum, Fragrance-Free," for oily, blemish-prone adult skin. One Indian woman, direct-to-camera, home setting.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
The brief asks for content that reads as an organic creator recommendation, not an ad shoot — because on short-form platforms, visible production polish reads as a sales pitch and triggers skepticism, while an unglossy, slightly imperfect, "caught mid-life" presentation reads as a peer sharing something that worked. The single job of this asset is to deliver exactly one believable, non-clinical purchase reason (this serum treats oily/blemish-prone skin without added fragrance/irritation) inside a runtime too short for tangents. Everything else — set, wardrobe, camera handling, edit rhythm — exists to support that credibility and that one reason, not to showcase filmmaking.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"The serum that finally makes sense for my skin." A relatable Indian woman, filming herself at home the way she'd film any get-ready-with-me clip, names the exact frustration of oily/breakout-prone skin, shows the product and how she actually uses it, states one plain reason it earned a place in her routine (fragrance-free, 2% salicylic acid, made for oily/blemish-prone skin — not something that adds irritation), and closes with a low-key, non-hyped call to action. No graphs, no before/after, no dermatologist claim — just one honest reason, demonstrated.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Primary (must land): This serum is formulated specifically for oily, blemish-prone skin and is fragrance-free — so it treats the problem without adding a common irritant.
2. Secondary: It's lightweight/fast-absorbing, not sticky or greasy (sensory reassurance for oily-skin users who fear heavy products).
3. Tertiary: Soft CTA ("linking it below") — no urgency language, no discount claim, no guarantee.
Explicitly excluded: percentage-improvement claims, "clinically proven," "dermatologist recommended," "cures/removes acne," before-after imagery, timeframes for results.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Aspect ratio: 9:16, full-bleed vertical, safe-margin text zone top 12% / bottom 20% for platform UI overlap.
- Look: handheld phone-selfie aesthetic — natural window/room light (soft daylight, slightly warm), no studio lighting rig, no beauty dish, visible minor asymmetries (slight camera shake, natural room clutter softly out of focus).
- Talent: one Indian woman, appears mid-to-late 20s, realistic skin texture retained (visible pores/texture — not airbrushed; skin texture is itself part of the credibility of a skin product), casual homewear (oversized tee or plain kurta), minimal/no makeup, hair loosely tied.
- Setting: home bedroom or bathroom counter, believable Indian middle-class domestic detail (mirror, everyday items), consistent across all shots.
- Product asset: one fixed bottle design — matte off-white or sage bottle, minimal sans-serif label reading "Tvacha Skin — Clear Base, 2% Salicylic Acid, Fragrance-Free," reused identically in every shot (no regeneration drift).
- Color: naturalistic, slightly warm, no heavy grade; avoid glossy commercial color science.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
Format: 6-shot creator-style sequence, cut like a real phone edit (hard cuts, no crossfades/transitions), total ≈25s.
Identity & asset lock: establish one fixed character reference (face, hair, wardrobe, room) and one fixed product-label reference before generating shots; reuse both references in every shot to avoid drift.

Shot list:

1. HOOK (0:00–0:04, ~4s) — Purpose: stop-scroll relatability.
Framing: medium close-up, slight low handheld angle (selfie POV), direct eye contact.
Action/VO: "Okay if you have oily, breakout-prone skin like me, you know—"
Mood: casual, mid-sentence energy, as if caught naturally talking.

2. HOOK CONTINUED (0:04–0:08, ~4s) — Purpose: complete the frustration, build identification.
Framing: same handheld medium close-up, tiny natural handheld drift.
Action/VO: "—finding something that actually works without messing your skin up further is SO hard."
Small natural gesture (hand near face, slight head tilt).

3. PRODUCT REVEAL / DEMO (0:08–0:13, ~5s) — Purpose: show the product, deliver core reason.
Framing: cut to closer insert shot, hands + product in frame, bottle label clearly legible, dispensing one drop onto fingertip.
Action/VO: "I've been using this — it's fragrance-free, 2% salicylic acid, literally made for skin like ours."
Detail: natural hand motion, real twist-cap/dropper action, no CGI-perfect liquid.

4. APPLICATION (0:13–0:18, ~5s) — Purpose: prove real use, sensory reassurance.
Framing: close-up on face/cheek, fingertips patting product in, slight camera handheld sway.
Action/VO: "It's so lightweight, sinks in in seconds — no sticky film, no fragrance stinging."
Skin should look normal/textured, not glossy or retouched.

5. PAYOFF / DIRECT ADDRESS (0:18–0:23, ~5s) — Purpose: restate the one purchase reason with conviction.
Framing: back to medium close-up direct-to-camera, bottle held near jaw/shoulder, casually not "presented."
Action/VO: "This is the first serum that's actually made sense for my skin."
Tone: quietly confident, not performative/salesy.

6. CTA (0:23–0:25, ~2s) — Purpose: soft close.
Framing: same medium close-up, slight smile, small nod.
Action/VO: "Linking it below."
On-screen (deterministic overlay, not generated): brand wordmark + product name small lower-third, appears only in this final 2s.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
Shared identity/reference prompt (use to lock character and product before generating shots 1–6):
"Photoreal Indian woman, mid-to-late 20s, natural unretouched skin with visible texture and pores, minimal makeup, loosely tied dark hair, wearing a plain oversized grey t-shirt, sitting/standing in a softly lit home bedroom with natural warm daylight from a side window, casual domestic background slightly soft-focus. Product: matte sage-green skincare bottle with dropper cap, minimal white sans-serif label reading 'Tvacha Skin — Clear Base, 2% Salicylic Acid, Fragrance-Free'. Maintain identical face, wardrobe, room, and bottle design across all shots."

Shot 1 prompt: "9:16 vertical, handheld phone-selfie framing, medium close-up, slight low angle, direct eye contact with camera, natural soft daylight, [character reference]. She is mid-conversation, casual and unscripted energy, slight natural camera shake, lips move in sync with: 'Okay if you have oily, breakout-prone skin like me, you know—'. No studio lighting, no gloss, realistic skin texture, UGC creator aesthetic, not a polished commercial."

Shot 2 prompt: "Same setup and character as shot 1, continuous handheld medium close-up, natural micro-movement, small head tilt and hand gesture near face, lips sync with: '—finding something that actually works without messing your skin up further is SO hard.' Same lighting, same wardrobe, same room."

Shot 3 prompt: "9:16 vertical, closer insert shot, hands and product bottle in frame, [product reference] label clearly legible and unblurred, natural hand dispensing one drop of clear serum onto fingertip, realistic liquid behavior, natural home lighting matching previous shots, voice-over sync: 'I've been using this — it's fragrance-free, 2% salicylic acid, literally made for skin like ours.' Handheld, slightly imperfect framing, no studio product-shot polish."

Shot 4 prompt: "9:16 vertical, close-up on face and cheek, same woman as previous shots, fingertips patting serum into skin with natural motion, slight handheld sway, natural skin texture visible (not airbrushed), same lighting continuity, lips sync with: 'It's so lightweight, sinks in in seconds — no sticky film, no fragrance stinging.'"

Shot 5 prompt: "9:16 vertical, medium close-up, same woman, same room, direct address to camera, bottle held casually near shoulder/jaw (not presented like an ad), calm confident tone, lips sync with: 'This is the first serum that's actually made sense for my skin.' Natural daylight, handheld, UGC aesthetic maintained."

Shot 6 prompt: "9:16 vertical, same medium close-up framing as shot 5, same woman, small genuine smile and slight nod, lips sync with: 'Linking it below.' Natural light, handheld, casual close, unscripted energy. Reserve lower-third safe area for a static text overlay (added in post, not generated)."

Negative/avoid conditions for all shots: no studio beauty lighting, no airbrushed/plastic skin, no glossy commercial color grade, no extra/misshapen fingers, no logo distortion or shifting label text between shots, no third-person or off-camera actor, no background inconsistency between shots, no on-screen numeric or clinical claim graphics generated into the frame.

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- Final lower-third text card (shot 6 only): brand wordmark "Tvacha Skin" + product name "Clear Base Serum" — added as a static post-production overlay, not generated, using fixed brand font/color.
- Burned-in captions synced to VO across all 6 shots (standard UGC/accessibility convention), plain sans-serif, high-contrast, added in edit — not generated.
- Product label artwork: fixed, pre-approved asset reused via the identity-lock reference described above; never freshly regenerated per shot to prevent label drift/misspelling.
- Any legal/mandatory disclaimer text (e.g., "Results may vary," if brand's legal team requires it) — static text card, post-production, not scripted into dialogue.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- Sync dialogue: her own voice, natural conversational pace, mild authentic Indian-English inflection, no voiceover-artist cadence.
- Room tone kept under dialogue (not fully clean) to preserve "phone-recorded" authenticity.
- Low-level trending-style background music bed (licensed/production-library, not identifiable copyrighted commercial track), volume well under dialogue, fades in from shot 3.
- Edit: hard cuts only between the 6 shots, no dissolves/wipes/lower-third animations except the final static text card — mimics native creator editing rather than agency editing.
- Captions burned in throughout, key phrase ("fragrance-free," "2% salicylic acid," "oily, blemish-prone skin") may be lightly emphasized in caption styling since this is the one purchase reason.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Risk: image looks like a commercial, not UGC → mitigated by handheld framing, natural light only, unretouched skin, hard cuts, no glossy grade (per credibility-through-imperfection principle below).
- Risk: talent reads as scripted/actorly, which undercuts trust → keep line lengths short, conversational, mid-sentence hook start, natural micro-gestures; avoid overly polished delivery or a "studied" sales tone.
- Risk: implied clinical/medical claim creeping in via phrasing (e.g., "clears acne," "dermatologist recommended," "clinically proven") → script locked to sensory/formulation language only ("fragrance-free," "made for oily/blemish-prone skin," "lightweight," "no sticky film"); no percentages beyond the factual 2% ingredient concentration, no before/after, no guarantee language.
- Risk: product label/bottle inconsistency across generated shots → single locked product reference reused verbatim in every shot; label text never regenerated freeform.
- Risk: identity drift (different-looking woman/room across shots) → single locked character+room reference reused across all 6 shots.
- Risk: hand/finger artifacts during product demo close-ups (shots 3–4) → flagged explicitly in negative prompt; these are the highest-scrutiny frames and should be reviewed first in QA.
- Risk: caption/text overlay claims exceeding what's spoken → text overlay limited to brand/product name only, no added claims in graphics.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- 9:16 vertical: yes, specified throughout.
- Performance-oriented UGC: yes, structure and visual system built for feed performance and native creator look.
- Fictional Indian D2C skincare brand: yes — "Tvacha Skin," invented.
- Fragrance-free 2% salicylic acid facial serum for oily/blemish-prone adult skin: yes, product spec used exactly and only as formulation fact.
- One believable Indian woman, direct to camera: yes, single talent, all 6 shots.
- Natural product demonstration: yes, shots 3–4.
- One clear purchase reason: yes — formulated for oily/blemish-prone skin, fragrance-free (no added irritant), stated once clearly and reinforced sensorially.
- Feels like creator content, not TVC: yes — handheld, natural light, hard cuts, unretouched skin, casual wardrobe/setting.
- Visually controlled, commercially usable: yes — locked identity/product references, fixed brand assets, licensed music, clean caption/legal overlay path.
- No invented clinical statistics/guarantees/medical claims: yes — script and negative prompts explicitly exclude these; only the 2% concentration (a factual formulation spec, not a clinical outcome claim) is stated.
- ~25 seconds: yes — shot durations sum to 25s.
- No external website: none referenced or shown.

## PACKAGE C

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
One 9:16 vertical video, ~25 seconds, single continuous "creator" narrative (edited as 5 quick-cut shots in the same location), for use as a paid social / performance ad (Reels/Shorts/Stories-style placement). No external website referenced or required.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
This is a performance-marketing asset that must pass as native creator content inside a feed, not read as a studio TVC, while remaining brand-safe, legally conservative, and edit-controlled enough to be reused as a paid ad. Success = a viewer believes a real person is talking to them, understands in one sentence why this serum suits her specifically, and is moved toward a purchase action — without any invented clinical percentage, before/after claim, guarantee, or medical framing beyond the two factual formulation facts the customer already supplied (2% salicylic acid, fragrance-free).

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"Finally, a serum made for MY skin, not everyone's skin." A relatable Indian woman, filming herself at her own bathroom/vanity counter, interrupts her own thought to explain the one reason she switched: this serum was actually formulated for oily, blemish-prone skin — salicylic acid at 2%, no fragrance to irritate it — so it behaves the way her skin actually needs, not the way a "universal" serum behaves. The single, clear purchase reason is fit-for-skin-type + irritation-avoidance, demonstrated sensorially (fast absorption, no sting, no greasy film, no scent), never claimed clinically.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Hook (0–3s): relatable oily/breakout-prone-skin pain point, mid-action, no logo bumper.
2. Context (3–9s): why past serums failed her (too harsh, too heavy, fragranced).
3. Product + single purchase reason (9–16s): formulated for oily/blemish-prone skin, 2% salicylic acid, fragrance-free — shown while demonstrated on skin.
4. Sensory payoff (16–22s): honest, small, non-clinical reaction (absorbs fast, no sting, no residue).
5. CTA (22–25s): brand name spoken + shown, soft direct ask, static end-card.
This order deliberately places the ask after context is established rather than front-loading it, and pairs the spoken brand name with the on-screen product/logo at the same moment for reinforcement.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Aspect ratio: 9:16, full-bleed, phone-camera field of view (mild wide-angle distortion typical of a front camera).
- Camera: handheld, natural micro-shake, no gimbal-smooth movement, occasional minor reframe as if she adjusted her phone mid-take.
- Lighting: single practical source — window daylight key from one side + ordinary bathroom/vanity practical light as fill; no beauty dish, no rim light, no glamour lighting. Skin must show real texture (light shine on T-zone, a couple of visible closed blemishes) — not airbrushed, no beauty filter.
- Setting: her own home bathroom/bedroom vanity, lived-in, minor real clutter (toothbrush, one other skincare bottle), consistent across all shots.
- Wardrobe/appearance: mid-to-late-20s Indian woman, casual home top, hair tied back or loose, bare face at start, natural unfiltered skin tone appropriate to a general Indian audience (avoid uniform fairness bias).
- Editing texture: jump-cut style typical of UGC (not cross-dissolves), slightly inconsistent framing shot to shot as if self-filmed in one sitting, burned-in bold sans-serif captions synced to speech, one factual on-screen super during the demonstration beat only.
- No studio graphics, no slow-motion glamour inserts, no dramatic score swells.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
1. Lock a single continuous location and consistent character description (reused verbatim across every generation prompt) to preserve identity/continuity.
2. Generate/shoot the 5 shots below in order; treat cuts as intentional jump-cuts, not seamless transitions — this is a UGC authenticity cue, not an error.
3. Record/generate her own scratch dialogue as guide audio; keep natural pacing including one small self-correction or stumble (e.g., "it's- okay let me start over") to avoid an over-rehearsed, ad-like delivery register.
4. Add burned-in captions (karaoke/word-highlight style common to creator content).
5. Composite one factual on-screen text super ("2% Salicylic Acid · Fragrance-Free") only over the demonstration shot — factual, ingredient-level, not a performance claim.
6. Insert a static end-card (brand lockup + product name + soft CTA text) as the final ~1 second — this is a designed, non-generative graphic asset, not part of the live-action generation.
7. Grade lightly to match a phone-camera colour profile (slightly warm, restrained saturation) — avoid a cinematic LUT.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
Reusable character/setting block (paste into every shot prompt for consistency):
"An Indian woman, mid-to-late 20s, natural unfiltered skin with visible T-zone shine and a couple of small closed blemishes, hair tied back, wearing a simple casual home top, filming herself handheld on a phone in her own bathroom/bedroom vanity with morning window light and one warm practical light, realistic amateur phone-camera footage, mild wide-angle lens distortion, natural handheld micro-shake, no beauty filter, no cinematic grade, vertical 9:16 framing."

SHOT 1 — HOOK (0–3s, purpose: stop the scroll with mid-action relatability)
"[character/setting block]. Medium close-up, she is already mid-sentence, holding a small unbranded-label serum bottle up near her face, slightly urgent/excited tone: 'Okay wait, I have to tell you why I switched to this—' Cut begins mid-word, no title card, no logo."

SHOT 2 — CONTEXT (3–9s, purpose: establish the problem before the ask)
"[character/setting block]. She lowers the bottle to the counter, gestures naturally while talking to camera: 'My skin's always been oily, breaks out randomly, and every serum I tried was either too heavy or so fragranced it stung.' Medium shot, casual continuous speech, natural pauses."

SHOT 3 — DEMONSTRATION + PURCHASE REASON (9–16s, purpose: deliver the one clear reason while showing proof)
"[character/setting block]. Close insert on her fingertip catching a drop of clear, lightweight gel-serum from the dropper/pump, then cut to medium shot as she pats it onto both cheeks and forehead. She says: 'This one's literally made for oily, blemish-prone skin — two percent salicylic acid, and it's fragrance-free, so it's not stinging or smelling like a chemistry set.' Natural patting sound, visible quick absorption, no shine or greasy residue left behind."

SHOT 4 — SENSORY PAYOFF (16–22s, purpose: honest, non-clinical reaction)
"[character/setting block]. Medium close-up, she touches her cheek, reacts naturally, half-laughs: 'And it just- sorry, it just sinks in like in ten seconds, no sticky film, layers totally fine under moisturizer.' Slight natural self-correction mid-sentence, unscripted energy."

SHOT 5 — CTA (22–25s, purpose: soft direct ask with brand reinforcement)
"[character/setting block]. She holds the bottle up at label-readable distance to camera, direct eye contact: 'If your skin's oily and breakout-prone like mine, just try [Brand] Clarity Serum — link's right there.' Ends on her holding the bottle steady, cut to static end-card."

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- End-card graphic: brand logo lockup, product name, one-line CTA text, and a small standard skincare disclaimer ("Patch test recommended before first use") — designed as a static asset in post, not generated.
- Burned-in captions: produced from the locked VO script, not generated per-frame.
- On-screen ingredient super ("2% Salicylic Acid · Fragrance-Free"): a designed text overlay, factual and static, composited once over Shot 3.
- Product label/packaging artwork: should be a real designed asset (typography, brand mark) composited onto the bottle prop/insert shots where legibility matters, rather than left to open-ended generation, to guarantee the label is legible and commercially correct.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- Primary audio: her own spoken VO, synced to lips, natural room tone underneath (not studio-clean).
- Optional low-bed background: a single subtle, low-volume trending-style music bed (well under dialogue, ~-18dB), consistent with native creator content conventions — not a cinematic score.
- Minimal SFX only where diegetic: bottle cap/pump click, soft patting sound during application.
- Edit: hard jump-cuts between the 5 shots (not dissolves), captions cut in sync with speech, end-card is the only fully static, non-handheld frame in the piece.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Do not add any statistic, percentage-improvement, dermatologist-endorsement, before/after, or "clears acne" guarantee language anywhere in dialogue or supers — only the two given formulation facts (2% salicylic acid, fragrance-free) and sensory description are permitted.
- Do not let the delivery become too polished/rehearsed-sounding — retain the natural stumble/self-correction; an overly smooth read will register as a TVC, undermining the brief.
- Do not apply beauty filters, skin smoothing, or glamour lighting — this breaks the "credible creator" requirement and can visually contradict an oily/blemish-prone-skin narrative.
- Do not show broken/inflamed skin in extreme close-up (compliance/sensitivity), and do not stage a clinical/white-coat setting.
- Keep brand name/CTA legible and spoken close together (audio+visual pairing) so the ask isn't missed on sound-off autoplay; ensure captions cover 100% of dialogue for sound-off viewing.
- Keep the ask (CTA) after context is established, not front-loaded, to match how this format is meant to land as an "action" objective piece.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- 9:16 vertical: met.
- Performance-oriented, UGC-credible, not TVC-polished: met via handheld camera, jump cuts, natural lighting, unrehearsed delivery.
- One believable Indian woman, direct to camera: met.
- Natural product demonstration: met (Shot 3).
- One clear purchase reason: met — "formulated for oily/blemish-prone skin, 2% salicylic acid, fragrance-free, so it doesn't irritate or feel heavy."
- No invented clinical statistics, guarantees, or medical claims: met — only customer-supplied formulation facts used; all other claims are sensory/experiential.
- ~25 seconds target: met (3+6+7+6+3 = 25s).
- No external website used or referenced: met.

## JUDGE V11
Apply the Stage-1 rubric. Choose exactly one A/B/C winner.
