# CONTEST V12 — B05

Use frozen brief B05 from the top of the final file.

## PACKAGE A

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
A single 16:9 short-form cinematic dialogue scene, approx. 40 seconds, six shots, café interior, two seated actors ("A" = the discoverer, "B" = the concealer). Final output: one graded video sequence with synced dialogue, ambient sound design, no score-driven manipulation, delivered as one continuous edit.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
The customer wants a self-contained confrontation scene that reads as directed cinema, not generic AI-dialogue coverage: restrained performance, no shouting, unbroken spatial logic between the two people, and visible craft in shot choice within a tight six-shot, ~40-second, minimal-camera-movement budget. The success criterion is that a viewer always knows, without thinking about it, who is on which side of the table and who is speaking/reacting — while feeling tension escalate through blocking, object, and silence rather than through cutting rate or camera movement.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
The scene is built around one static geometric fact — a fixed table axis between A (left) and B (right) — and one physical object: a folded letter/document A has found, placed on the table as evidence of concealment. All six shots are variations on that fixed axis (wide, two OTS singles, one insert on the object, one isolated close single, return to the wide) so tension rises purely through what is chosen to be shown, not through how the camera moves. The confrontation ends unresolved — held eye contact over the folded letter — rather than a shouted climax, matching "restrained but tense."

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Geography first: two people, a table, a charged object between them (Shot 1).
2. The accusation is understated and object-anchored, not verbal-exposition-heavy: "You knew." (Shot 2).
3. The hurt is visible in restraint, not volume (Shot 3).
4. A beat of pure physical evidence — the object itself, trembling hand — carries subtext without dialogue (Shot 4).
5. The defense is quiet and defensive, not combative (Shot 5).
6. No resolution — held tension, geography reaffirmed (Shot 6). The viewer is left to judge, which is the emotional payload.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Axis of action: an imaginary line runs along the table between A and B. All six camera setups stay on one side of that line for the entire scene; screen sides are fixed for the whole scene — A is always frame-left with attention/eyeline to frame-right, B is always frame-right with attention/eyeline to frame-left, including in singles where the other person is off-frame.
- Shot sizes vary (wide → OTS medium → insert close → isolated close → wide) to avoid mechanical shot/reverse-shot monotony; only one true insert (the object) breaks the face-pattern and doubles as a breathing beat.
- Camera: locked-off tripod for every setup. No pans, no dollies, no reframing. Motion in the scene comes from actor blocking (hands, posture, a slid document, a flinch) rather than camera movement — this keeps coverage feeling composed rather than "camera-operated."
- Lighting: soft window key from camera-left, low fill, practical café ambience; keeps both faces readable without dramatizing via lighting change (restraint is carried by performance, not chiaroscuro).
- Palette: neutral warm café tones, desaturated slightly toward the confrontation shots (2–5) for psychological compression, returning to neutral in the final wide (6) for closure/distance.
- Aspect ratio: 16:9 throughout, consistent lens height (~eye level, slight downward table tilt in the wide only) across all setups.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
Setup (fixed for entire scene): single café table, two chairs facing each other, camera positioned on one side of the table-axis line only. Three camera positions total, reused across the six shots:
- Position W (wide, centered, slightly camera-left of axis): used for Shots 1 and 6.
- Position OTS-A (behind A's shoulder, framing B): used for Shot 2.
- Position OTS-B (behind B's shoulder, framing A): used for Shot 3.
- Position CU-B (tight single on B, same side of line as OTS-A): used for Shot 5.
- Position Insert (overhead/table-level macro on the letter): used for Shot 4.
Wardrobe/props locked before shooting/generation: A wears muted cool tone, B wears muted warm tone (visually separates them at a glance); the letter/document prop must be visually identical across Shots 1, 2, 4, 6 (same fold, same paper stock, same position on table when not being handled).

Shot list, order, purpose, duration:
1. WIDE TWO-SHOT (establishing) — Purpose: lock geography, establish axis, silent beat. ~6s.
2. MEDIUM OTS on B (over A's shoulder) — Purpose: deliver the accusation, introduce the object into frame. ~7s.
3. MEDIUM OTS on A (over B's shoulder) — Purpose: restrained hurt/response. ~7s.
4. INSERT/CUTAWAY on the letter + B's hand — Purpose: subtext beat, no dialogue, breathing room, protects pacing without adding a face-cut. ~3s.
5. CLOSE SINGLE on B (isolated, same screen side as established) — Purpose: quiet defensive line, most intimate moment. ~6s.
6. WIDE TWO-SHOT (return to Shot 1's exact framing) — Purpose: unresolved closure, reaffirm geography, hold silence. ~9s.
Total: ~38–40s including 1–2s of held silence at head/tail.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
Shot 1 (6s, WIDE, static, 16:9): "Locked-off wide shot of a small café table, eye-level, slight downward angle. A woman/man (A) sits frame-left in a muted cool-toned sweater, hands resting near a coffee cup. Opposite, (B) sits frame-right in a muted warm-toned shirt. Between them, on the table, a plain folded letter. Soft window light from camera-left, shallow depth of field, blurred café patrons in background. Nobody speaks. A watches B settle, quiet ambient café noise, no camera movement."

Shot 2 (7s, MEDIUM OTS over A's shoulder toward B, static, 16:9): "Over-the-shoulder shot from behind A (out-of-focus shoulder frame-left foreground), framing B frame-right, eyeline to frame-left. B glances down as a hand (A's) slides a folded letter into frame across the table. B's expression shifts—controlled, guarded. Quiet café ambience. Dialogue, restrained and quiet: A (off-camera): 'You knew.' B (beat, low voice): '...I didn't think it mattered anymore.' No camera movement."

Shot 3 (7s, MEDIUM OTS over B's shoulder toward A, static, 16:9): "Over-the-shoulder shot from behind B (out-of-focus shoulder frame-right foreground), framing A frame-left, eyeline to frame-right. A's jaw tightens, hands still on the table, breath controlled — restrained hurt, no raised voice. Dialogue: A: 'It mattered to me.' Long pause, exhale. No camera movement, quiet café ambience only."

Shot 4 (3s, INSERT/MACRO, static, 16:9): "Close macro shot of a folded letter resting on a café table, coffee cup steam drifting past, a hand's fingers (B's) hovering near the letter, trembling slightly. No dialogue. Ambient café sound only — distant clink of a spoon, murmurs. No camera movement."

Shot 5 (6s, CLOSE SINGLE on B, static, 16:9): "Tight close-up on B, frame-right positioned, eyeline held toward frame-left (A remains off-frame but B's gaze direction is unchanged from prior shots). Quiet, restrained defensiveness. Dialogue: B: 'What do you want me to say?' Voice low, controlled, no shouting. Subtle facial tension, no camera movement, soft window key light."

Shot 6 (9s, WIDE TWO-SHOT, identical framing to Shot 1, static, 16:9): "Return to the same locked-off wide two-shot as the opening: A frame-left, B frame-right, table and folded letter between them. A reaches out and slowly folds the letter closed, eyes never leaving B. Neither speaks. Hold on both faces in silence for the final seconds before cut to black. Ambient café sound only, no camera movement, no music."

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- Character and prop continuity must be locked via fixed reference images (same actor likeness, wardrobe, letter prop, table/café set) reused across all six shot generations — this is the single highest risk point in generated (non-photographed) production and must be handled deterministically, not re-prompted per shot.
- Dialogue audio: record/generate once per line and treat as a locked deterministic audio asset synced to lip movement in each relevant shot, rather than regenerating audio per shot.
- Color grade: single deterministic LUT applied uniformly across all six shots in post to guarantee visual continuity that generation alone won't reliably produce.
- Subtitle/caption burn-in (if used) must exactly match spoken audio — no divergence between displayed and spoken text.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
Dialogue is sparse and quiet by design; no non-diegetic score. Because so little dialogue is spoken, the remaining soundtrack (café ambience, cup, paper fold, chair creak, breath) becomes the vessel for tension — kept entirely diegetic so the audience isn't consciously aware of being cued, preserving the "restrained, not manipulated" tone. Edit rhythm: hard cuts only, no dissolves; each cut motivated by new information (a look, the object, a line) rather than mechanical alternation. The insert (Shot 4) functions as a reaction/breathing beat that interrupts the face-pattern without breaking spatial logic, and the return to the identical wide framing in Shot 6 provides closure by symmetry.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Enforce one fixed action line for the entire scene; every shot (including singles) preserves each character's established screen side and eyeline direction even when the other is off-frame — this is the single most common way two-hander scenes become spatially confusing.
- Avoid shot/reverse/shot monotony by varying shot scale and inserting one true cutaway (the object) rather than a third face angle.
- Let blocking (a slid letter, a trembling hand, a slow fold) carry the scene's motion instead of camera movement, keeping coverage feeling directed rather than mechanically alternated.
- Guard prop/wardrobe/lighting consistency across all six generations explicitly — inconsistency here is the primary way a generated dialogue scene collapses believability.
- Keep dialogue lines short and vocally restrained in direction notes (quiet, low, controlled) to prevent generative delivery from drifting toward melodrama or raised volume.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Two people opposite each other at a café table: satisfied (fixed table axis, A left / B right).
- One discovered the other concealed something important: satisfied (letter reveal, "You knew" / "I didn't think it mattered anymore").
- Tense but restrained, nobody shouts: satisfied (quiet dialogue, controlled directions throughout).
- Viewer always understands relative position: satisfied via locked action line and fixed screen sides across all six shots.
- Exactly six shots: satisfied (1 wide, 2 OTS singles, 1 insert, 1 close single, 1 return wide).
- Minimal/no elaborate camera movement: satisfied (all setups locked-off/static; motion carried by blocking).
- Cinematic, not mechanical coverage: satisfied via varied shot scale, motivated cutaway, and blocking-driven movement rather than reliance on camera moves or symmetrical shot/reverse-shot.
- ~40 seconds total: satisfied (6+7+7+3+6+9 ≈ 38–40s with held silence).
- 16:9: satisfied, stated per shot.
- No external website used: satisfied — no website consulted.

## PACKAGE B

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
One continuous 16:9 narrative video, ~40 seconds, built from exactly 6 shots, cut together into a single scene: two people at a café table, restrained confrontation after one discovers the other concealed something important. No shouting, no camera acrobatics, full spatial clarity throughout.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
The customer wants a small, controlled dramatic scene that reads as directed cinema, not "coverage." The core creative risk is that a two-person table dialogue, cut conventionally, collapses into mechanical shot/reverse-shot ping-pong that feels like a video call. The brief's real requirements are: (1) unmistakable, stable geography between the two people across all six cuts, (2) tension carried by restraint — stillness, held silences, small physical tells — rather than volume, (3) a shot count and camera economy (six shots, minimal movement) that still reads as authored rather than default. The solution is to treat the six shots as a closed structure — establish, break into the two people, insert the physical proof of the concealment, hold on the accused, and return to the opening master — rather than as an open-ended alternation.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"The table doesn't move, but the ground shifts." The camera is almost entirely locked off; the scene's only real movement is the small object that changes hands/position on the table (the concealed thing, made physical — e.g., a folded receipt, ticket stub, or photograph placed down between them) and the shift in who can hold eye contact. Bookending the scene with the identical wide master at the start and end gives it a closed, authored shape — the audience registers that nothing about the room changed, only what is now known.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Primary read (must land in first 8s): two people, a café table, calm surface, unspoken tension already present.
2. Secondary read (mid-scene): a concrete object surfaces — the "proof" — and the audience understands one person has been caught in a concealment.
3. Tertiary read (closing beat): restraint over resolution — no confession, no apology, no raised voice; the final wide shot leaves the outcome open but the rupture confirmed.
Nothing textual, no captions — all hierarchy carried by staging, object placement and performance.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Aspect ratio: 16:9, locked/static or near-imperceptible micro-push only (no pans, no whip, no handheld shake).
- Axis of action: drawn once, along the table, through the two people's sightline established in Shot 1. Person A (the one who discovered) is fixed frame-left throughout; Person B (the one who concealed) is fixed frame-right throughout. Every subsequent setup stays on the same side of this line so their eyelines always cross correctly and neither ever appears to address an unseen third person.
- Lighting: naturalistic café daylight or warm interior practical light, soft and consistent across all six setups (same time of day, same color temperature) — continuity of light sells continuity of geography.
- Lens/scale discipline: matched focal lengths and framing scale for the two reciprocal singles (Shots 2 and 3) so the cut between them doesn't visually jolt the audience.
- Color: muted, slightly desaturated naturalistic palette; no stylization that would compete with restraint.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
Six shots, static or near-static camera, single afternoon café interior, two actors, one small physical prop (the "proof" object) and two beverage props (cups) for hand-business. Total runtime ≈ 40s.

Shot 1 — WIDE MASTER (establishing) — ~8s
Purpose: Establish geography and the axis of action; both people visible, A frame-left, B frame-right, table between them. A is still, hands around cup; B is mid small-talk gesture, unaware anything has shifted. Locked-off camera, eye-level, slight table-height angle.

Shot 2 — MEDIUM CLOSE-UP, Person A — ~6s
Purpose: A speaks the accusation quietly and flatly, looking frame-right (matches Shot 1 geometry). Minimal movement in frame; tension carried in stillness of the face and voice.

Shot 3 — MEDIUM CLOSE-UP, Person B (reciprocal) — ~6s
Purpose: B's reaction — stirring/hand motion stops mid-gesture, matched shot scale and eyeline frame-left. This is the "caught" beat, played through stillness, not shock.

Shot 4 — INSERT / CUTAWAY on the object — ~4s
Purpose: A places the concealed proof (folded paper/photo) on the table between them; frame holds both hands in shot (A's placing it, B's not yet reaching), reconfirming relative positions without a new master. This is also the buffer shot per continuity practice — it lets the scene breathe before returning to faces.

Shot 5 — WIDER TWO-SHOT / OVER-SHOULDER FAVORING B — ~9s
Purpose: Camera stays inside the same established arc, framed past A's shoulder (soft foreground, frame-left) onto B (frame-right), who delivers the restrained, non-shouted response — quiet, guarded, incomplete ("I was going to—"). A doesn't answer immediately.

Shot 6 — RETURN TO WIDE MASTER (identical setup to Shot 1) — ~7s
Purpose: Close the scene on the same locked-off wide frame; neither moves for a beat, the object remains on the table between them, one of them breaks eye contact or reaches for their cup. No resolution stated — the geometry that opened the scene now holds a different meaning.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
(Each is one executable shot-level prompt; static/locked camera unless noted; 16:9; naturalistic café interior, warm daylight, continuity of wardrobe/props across all six.)

SHOT 1: "Static wide shot, eye-level, inside a quiet daytime café. A woman (A) sits at a small table, frame-left, hands around a coffee cup, still and composed. Opposite her, a man (B) sits frame-right, mid-gesture, relaxed, saying something casual. Table and both figures fully visible, natural window light, muted color palette. No camera movement. Ambient café atmosphere, soft background blur of other patrons out of focus."

SHOT 2: "Static medium close-up on the woman (A), frame-left of center, looking screen-right toward her unseen companion. Her expression is controlled, flat, restrained — she speaks quietly, no raised voice, a slight tightening around the eyes. Warm natural light matching previous shot. No camera movement."

SHOT 3: "Static medium close-up on the man (B), frame-right of center, looking screen-left. His hand, holding a spoon or cup, stops moving mid-gesture as he registers what was just said. Controlled, guarded stillness in his face — no overt shock, just a held breath. Matched lighting and lens scale to prior close-up. No camera movement."

SHOT 4: "Static close insert shot, table-top level, shallow depth of field. A woman's hand (frame-left) places a small folded paper/photograph onto the café table between two coffee cups. A man's hand (frame-right) remains still, not yet reaching. Natural light, soft focus on background figures. No camera movement."

SHOT 5: "Static over-the-shoulder shot from behind the woman's shoulder (soft out-of-focus foreground, frame-left), framing the man (B) in focus, frame-right, across the café table. He speaks haltingly, quietly, restrained — an unfinished, guarded sentence, no shouting. Natural warm light, same café setting. No camera movement, or at most an almost imperceptible slow push-in of a few inches over the duration of the shot."

SHOT 6: "Static wide shot, identical framing and lighting to the opening shot — same café table, woman frame-left, man frame-right, the small paper object visible on the table between them. Neither speaks; one of them (A) looks down or reaches for her cup, breaking eye contact. Held for a beat of stillness before the scene ends. No camera movement."

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- Exact table/prop continuity (cups, the folded paper/photo object, wardrobe) must be identical across all six generations — treat as a fixed asset list supplied to every shot generation, not re-imagined per shot.
- Screen-left/screen-right assignment (A=left, B=right) is a hard, non-negotiable continuity rule applied identically to every shot including the insert.
- Light direction and color temperature must be held constant across all six shots (single locked lighting reference).

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- Dialogue delivered quietly, in a near-monotone, restrained register; no shouting, no overlapping speech. Lines are short, with real silences between them (silence is doing dramatic work).
- Suggested minimal line set: A: "You told me you sold it. Two years ago." → [silence, Shot 3] → A (over Shot 4 insert, V.O. or continued sync): "I found this last month." → B (Shot 5): "I was going to tell you—" → A: "When." → [Shot 6: silence].
- Ambient café bed (soft murmur, cup clinks, no music) under the whole scene; consider a single sparse, low piano or string note entering only under Shot 5–6 to underscore restraint, not to manipulate volume/drama.
- Cuts are hard cuts, no dissolves; the buffer/insert (Shot 4) functions as the breathing space between the two reciprocal close-ups, consistent with using a brief intervening shot to let a beat land before returning to faces.
- Shot 6 uses the exact Shot 1 framing to bookend the scene — an intentional "return to master" structure rather than continuous alternation, which is what keeps six shots from reading as generic coverage.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Do not let any single shot cross to the far side of the table's axis of action — this is the single biggest risk to spatial clarity; if a generated shot yields the wrong side, discard and regenerate from the same locked reference rather than trying to fix it in the edit.
- Keep both reciprocal close-ups (Shots 2 and 3) at matched shot scale and lens feel; a mismatch in framing size between them will read as an error, not a style choice.
- Do not add camera movement beyond the single optional micro-push in Shot 5; any pan, whip, or handheld shake will break the "restrained" tone the brief requires.
- Do not let performers raise volume or gesture broadly — restraint must be visible in stillness and micro-expression, not just implied by the dialogue text.
- Ensure the prop (the concealed-thing object) is visually consistent in Shots 1, 4, 5, and 6 — a changed object undermines the "proof" beat.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Two people, café table, opposite seating: met.
- One discovers the other concealed something important: met (proof object + dialogue).
- Tense, restrained dialogue, nobody shouts: met (staging + performance + audio notes enforce this).
- Viewer always understands spatial relationship: met via locked axis of action, matched eyelines, bookended master shots.
- Exactly six shots: met.
- Minimal/no elaborate camera movement: met (static/locked, one optional micro-push).
- Feels cinematic, not mechanical coverage: addressed via bookend structure, insert shot, and restrained blocking rather than plain alternating shot/reverse-shot.
- ~40 seconds total: met (8+6+6+4+9+7 = 40s).
- 16:9: met.
- No external website used: met — no website material referenced.

## PACKAGE C

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
A single 16:9 short cinematic dialogue scene, approximately 40 seconds, told in exactly six shots, depicting two people at a café table in the moment one confronts the other over a concealed matter. Restrained performance, no camera flourish, unbroken spatial logic throughout.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
The customer wants a scene that reads as authored cinema, not "coverage" — six shots is a tight budget, so every shot must earn its place (establish, escalate, or land the beat) rather than mechanically alternating faces. The two non-negotiable craft requirements are: (1) the viewer must never lose track of who is where relative to whom, and (2) tension must be carried by restraint — stillness, micro-expression, silence, precise blocking — not by movement or volume.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
A locked-off, geometrically disciplined dialogue scene built on one consistent axis of action, bookended by a returning wide shot. The confrontation escalates through two clean reciprocal singles, then resolves into a single wordless insert of the concealed object before returning to the opening wide framing — so the scene has a visual rhyme (out → in → object → out) instead of a flat back-and-forth, giving it a felt shape rather than a mechanical shot/reverse-shot rhythm.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Space and relationship (who they are to each other, that they are opposite one another) — Shot 1.
2. The accusation is made, quietly — Shot 2.
3. The concealer's controlled reaction — Shot 3.
4. The confronter's precise follow-through — Shot 4.
5. Visual proof / the concealed thing itself, silent — Shot 5.
6. Unresolved aftermath, held in the same space we started in — Shot 6.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Characters: ELARA (discoverer, frame-left throughout) and MARCUS (concealer, frame-right throughout) — fixed screen positions, established in Shot 1 and never crossed.
- Wardrobe: muted, contemporary, unbranded — Elara in a soft charcoal knit, Marcus in a plain slate-blue shirt — low-saturation palette so nothing pulls focus from faces/hands.
- Location: small independent café, window-side two-top table, shallow depth of field background with soft bokeh of other patrons/counter activity for life without distraction.
- Light: naturalistic overcast window light, cool-neutral, slight warm practical fill from an interior lamp off-frame; soft contrast, no dramatic chiaroscuro — restraint in lighting matches restraint in performance.
- Lens family: consistent optics across the scene — wide two-shots on a ~35–40mm equivalent, singles and the insert on ~85mm equivalent, shallow stop for singles to isolate faces once the geography is locked in.
- Camera support: locked-off tripod for all six shots. No pans, no dolly, no handheld. Only the bookend wide shots may carry an almost imperceptible breathing drift (sub-pixel, not a move) to avoid a dead, static-photo feel.
- The concealed object: a worn, unmarked folded letter/envelope sitting on the table from the start of Shot 1, positioned consistently (same table position, same orientation) in every shot where it's visible, so it reads as one continuous prop rather than continuity-breaking set dressing. No legible text is ever needed or shown — its meaning is carried by handling and framing, not by readable content.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
Governing rule: establish the action line once, in Shot 1, via the two characters' mutual sightline across the table; every subsequent camera setup stays on the same side of that line (Elara camera-left/looking frame-right, Marcus camera-right/looking frame-left) with no exceptions. Successive same-subject shots differ in angle by a clearly perceptible amount (well over a marginal shift) so no pair reads as a jump cut. The two singles (Shots 3 and 4) are true reciprocal pairs — matching height, matching look-room, mirrored across the line — so the cut between them produces a clean eye-trace from one face to the other. Shot 5 breaks the face/face pattern deliberately with a non-face insert, both to vary the rhythm cinematically and to let the object itself carry a beat of information the dialogue doesn't state aloud. Shots 1 and 6 are the same setup, reused, to bookend the scene and make the six shots feel like a composed structure rather than raw coverage.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
(16:9, locked-off camera unless noted; restrained, near-whisper-level delivery, long pauses, no raised voices)

SHOT 1 — WIDE TWO-SHOT (establishing) — ~8s
"Static wide shot, 16:9, small café interior, soft overcast window light. A two-top table centered in frame, two people seated opposite each other: a woman in a charcoal knit sweater on the left of frame, a man in a slate-blue shirt on the right of frame, both in profile-three-quarter facing each other across the table. A worn, folded envelope sits on the table between them, closer to the woman's side. Shallow bokeh of café activity behind them. Both figures still, tense stillness, coffee cups untouched. Locked-off camera, no movement. Naturalistic ambient light, muted color palette."
Purpose: establish geography, the axis of action, and the object.

SHOT 2 — MEDIUM TWO-SHOT favoring Elara — ~6s
"Static medium two-shot, 16:9, same café table, camera on the same side as Shot 1. Woman (charcoal knit) in the foreground-left, sharp focus, facing frame-right; man (slate-blue shirt) soft-focused over her shoulder on the right, facing frame-left. She speaks quietly, controlled, restrained anger — no raised voice, minimal gesture, her hand resting near the envelope. Natural window light, shallow depth of field, locked-off camera."
Dialogue (restrained, quiet): "You said you didn't know."
Purpose: the accusation, delivered without heat.

SHOT 3 — SINGLE, Marcus (reciprocal) — ~6s
"Static medium close-up, 16:9, man in slate-blue shirt, positioned right of frame, eyes looking frame-left, matching the sightline established in the wide shot. Café bokeh behind him. His expression is tightly controlled — a small tell: jaw tightens, eyes drop briefly to the table then rise again. He does not raise his voice. Shallow depth of field, naturalistic light, locked-off camera, no movement."
Dialogue (low, measured): "It wasn't — it isn't what you think."
Purpose: controlled reaction from the concealer; reciprocal single, mirrors Shot 2's eyeline exactly.

SHOT 4 — SINGLE, Elara (reciprocal) — ~6s
"Static medium close-up, 16:9, woman in charcoal knit, positioned left of frame, eyes looking frame-right, mirroring the reciprocal framing of the previous single. Her hands are still, folded near her cup. Quiet, precise delivery, no shouting, sustained eye contact implied toward off-frame subject. Shallow depth of field, same lighting continuity as prior shots, locked-off camera."
Dialogue (quiet, precise): "Then tell me what it is."
Purpose: the follow-through; completes the reciprocal pair, escalates tension without volume.

SHOT 5 — INSERT, the object — ~5s
"Static extreme close/insert shot, 16:9, tabletop surface, shallow depth of field. A worn, folded envelope sits exactly where it was placed in Shot 1, same orientation, same table position. A hand (woman's, charcoal sleeve visible at frame edge) enters and rests two fingers on the envelope without opening it. No dialogue. Ambient café sound only. Locked-off camera, no movement."
Purpose: wordless proof beat; breaks the face/face rhythm deliberately for cinematic pacing.

SHOT 6 — WIDE TWO-SHOT (return) — ~9s
"Static wide shot, identical framing and camera position to Shot 1, 16:9, same café table, same soft window light. Woman on left, man on right, same postures re-established. The envelope has been moved slightly toward the center of the table by the hand from the previous shot. Neither speaks. He looks down; she watches him, unmoving. Long held silence, ambient café sound only, locked-off camera, no movement, hold to end of scene."
Purpose: bookend return; unresolved aftermath held in the same space as the opening — closes the structure.

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- Fixed character wardrobe and prop (envelope) descriptions to be locked as a style/continuity reference before generation, reused verbatim across all six prompts to maximize cross-shot consistency.
- Envelope kept free of legible text/graphics by design, avoiding generative-text legibility failure and continuity mismatches across shots.
- Screen-left/screen-right assignment (Elara left, Marcus right) fixed as a hard constraint carried into every prompt, never varied.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- Dialogue: near-whisper to quiet-conversational volume throughout; no shouted lines anywhere in the scene.
- Ambient bed: low café room tone (distant chatter, cup clinks, espresso machine) present under all six shots, slightly recessed under dialogue shots, slightly more present in the silent Shot 5 and Shot 6.
- No score until Shot 6, where a single sustained low tone (piano or string, very quiet) enters under the silence and is allowed to hang past picture out.
- Cuts are placed on the ends of held beats/pauses rather than on movement, reinforcing restraint; the cut from Shot 4 to Shot 5 is the one deliberate rhythm-break (face to object) and should feel like a intentional held breath, not a jump.
- Edit pacing: 8s / 6s / 6s / 6s / 5s / 9s ≈ 40s total.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Maintain one continuous action line across all six setups: Elara always frame-left/looking frame-right, Marcus always frame-right/looking frame-left; the camera never crosses to the opposite side, which prevents both characters from appearing to address an unseen third person.
- Shots 3 and 4 are true reciprocal singles (matched height, matched look-room, mirrored across the line) so the eye-trace at the cut lands cleanly on the new face rather than confusing the viewer.
- Successive shots of the same subject are angled distinctly enough (wide vs. medium vs. close, not marginal reframes) to avoid jump-cut ambiguity.
- Shot 5 deliberately breaks the pure shot/reverse-shot pattern with a non-face insert — this is the specific technique for making a small shot count feel authored rather than mechanically covered, and it also gives a safe fallback beat if a performance take doesn't read well on faces.
- Prop (envelope) position is held identical in Shots 1, 5, and 6 to avoid a continuity break the viewer would register as a spatial error.
- No camera movement is used to manufacture tension — tension is carried entirely by blocking, stillness, and restrained delivery, per the brief's explicit ban on shouting and elaborate movement.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Two people, café table, opposite each other: satisfied.
- One has discovered the other concealed something important: satisfied (envelope + dialogue).
- Tense but restrained, nobody shouts: satisfied (all dialogue quiet/measured, explicit direction against volume).
- Viewer always understands relative position: satisfied via strict 180° axis discipline and fixed screen-left/screen-right assignment.
- Exactly six shots: satisfied.
- Minimal/no elaborate camera movement: satisfied (locked-off throughout, no pans/dollies/handheld moves).
- Feels cinematic, not mechanical coverage: satisfied via bookend structure and the non-face insert breaking pure shot/reverse-shot rhythm.
- ~40 seconds target: satisfied (8+6+6+6+5+9 = 40s).
- 16:9: satisfied, stated in every prompt.
- No external website used: satisfied — no website referenced or drawn upon.

## JUDGE V12
Apply the Stage-1 rubric. Choose exactly one A/B/C winner.

## PACKAGE A

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
Vertical Video Ad (9:16), ~30 Seconds.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
Convert the "operational anxiety" of Indian PG/Hostel owners—caused by fragmented tools like WhatsApp, notebooks, and spreadsheets—into a desire for RentOK’s centralized, automated system. The ad must move from "chaos" to "calm," using culturally resonant imagery to avoid a generic SaaS feel.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"The 1990s vs. 2026." 
Contrast the claustrophobic, tactile chaos of traditional "Bahi-Khata" (ledger) management with the airy, effortless efficiency of the RentOK app. The tension is built through rapid, rhythmic cuts and overlapping noise, which then resolves into a singular, satisfying digital experience.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. The Pain: Operational chaos (registers, missed payments, KYC stress).
2. The Cost: "Leakages" and "Jhikjhik" (unnecessary hassle).
3. The Solution: RentOK as the clean, all-in-one system.
4. Key Proof Points: Automated Rent, Digital KYC, Complaint Management.
5. Trust Signal: 15,000+ Property Owners.
6. Call to Action: Get Free Demo.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Phase 1 (Chaos): Warm, slightly saturated tones. Handheld "shaky" camera. Close-ups of cluttered textures (ink-stained registers, piles of Aadhar photocopies, tea stains on desks). Fast-paced editing.
- Phase 2 (Calm): Cool, bright, minimalist tones. Stable, smooth camera movements (slow pans). Clean UI overlays.
- Setting: A typical Indian PG office—functional but cluttered in Phase 1; organized and professional in Phase 2.
- Talent: A middle-aged Indian male owner. Transitions from looking stressed/overwhelmed to relaxed and in control.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
- Style: Live-action blended with high-fidelity App UI overlays.
- Pacing: 0-15s (Fast/Stressful) $\rightarrow$ 15-17s (Hard Cut/Silence) $\rightarrow$ 17-30s (Smooth/Satisfying).
- Audio Strategy: 
    - 0-15s: A "sonic collage" of overlapping phone pings, pages flipping, a tenant's voice complaining in the background, and a ticking clock.
    - 15-17s: Sudden "Whoosh" or "Ding" sound followed by a moment of silence.
    - 17-30s: A contemporary, upbeat, Lo-fi Indian beat (think modern fusion) that feels professional and fresh.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
(For production reference: Visuals should be shot as high-quality live action, not AI-generated, but if using generative b-roll, use these styles:)

- Shot 1-4 (Chaos): "Hyper-realistic close-up, 9:16, an old Indian accounting ledger (Bahi-Khata) with messy handwriting and ink blots, dimly lit office, cinematic textures, shallow depth of field."
- Shot 5 (Chaos): "Over-the-shoulder shot, 9:16, a smartphone screen buzzing with 20+ unread WhatsApp notifications from tenants, cluttered desk with tea glass and scattered papers, natural Indian daylight."
- Shot 6-8 (Calm): "Clean, minimalist interior of a modern Indian office, bright lighting, 9:16, a professional Indian man in a crisp shirt smiling and looking at a smartphone, high-end commercial cinematography, soft bokeh."

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- App UI: Exact screen recordings of RentOK's Rent Collection, KYC, and Complaint modules.
- Text Overlays: Bold, modern sans-serif typography.
- Logo: Official RentOK logo for the end card.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- Shot 1 (0-3s): [Visual: Rapid cuts of messy registers/files] [Audio: Fast page flipping + Phone ringing] [Text: Managing like it's 1990?]
- Shot 2 (3-7s): [Visual: Close-up of red cells in a spreadsheet / "Payment Pending" notes] [Audio: Overlapping WhatsApp notification sounds] [Text: Rent leakages?]
- Shot 3 (7-12s): [Visual: Owner rubbing temples, surrounded by piles of KYC documents] [Audio: Sound of a tenant arguing/complaining in background] [Text: Too much 'Jhikjhik'?]
- Shot 4 (12-15s): [Visual: Extreme close-up of the owner's stressed face $\rightarrow$ Zoom out quickly] [Audio: All sound peaks then cuts to silence] [Text: Stop the chaos.]
- Shot 5 (15-22s): [Visual: Smooth transition to RentOK App. Screen recording showing one-click payment reminders and KYC approval] [Audio: Upbeat, modern Lo-fi beat starts] [Text: RentOK: The Smart System.]
- Shot 6 (22-27s): [Visual: Owner calmly sipping tea, scrolling phone with a smile] [Audio: Music continues] [Text: Automated Rent. Easy KYC. Zero Leakage.]
- Shot 7 (27-30s): [Visual: End card with Logo and "Join 15,000+ owners"] [Audio: Musical resolve] [Text: Get Free Demo $\rightarrow$ RentOK.com]

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Avoid "Silicon Valley" aesthetics: No generic white-background 2D animations or floating 3D icons. Keep it grounded in the reality of an Indian PG office.
- Avoid generic stock footage: Ensure the talent and setting feel authentically Indian (not "global corporate").
- Ensure UI is legible: App screens must be enlarged and centered for 9:16 viewing.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Vertical 9:16? Yes.
- Target 30s? Yes.
- Indian Context? Yes (Bahi-Khata, "Jhikjhik", specific PG pain points).
- RentOK Official Info? Yes (Features: Autopay, KYC, Complaint Mgmt; Social Proof: 15k+ owners).
- No generic SaaS style? Yes (focused on tangible chaos vs. calm).

- Pace: Fast cuts every 3-5 seconds to maintain engagement.
- Lighting: Soft natural window light to emphasize skin texture without masking it.

Shot 1 (0-4s): [Talking Head] Mid-shot of a relatable Indian woman in her late 20s, natural skin, standing in a bright bathroom. She looks at the camera with a knowing smile.

Prompt: "9:16 close-up, Indian woman applying clear skincare serum to her face, gentle patting motion, glowing but natural skin texture, soft daylight, authentic UGC feel."

Shot 4 (18-25s): [Talking Head] Return to mid-shot. She holds the product next to her face, nodding confidently.

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS

    - 0-4s: "Oily skin struggle? 🫠"
    - 10-15s: "2% Salicylic Acid | Fragrance-Free ✨"
    - 20-25s: "Link in Bio to Shop"
- Branding: Fictional logo applied to the bottle in post-production.

- Audio: Natural voiceover (Indian English accent, conversational, warm) + low-volume, upbeat, lo-fi background track.

    - (0-4s): "If you're dealing with constant oil and breakouts, listen up."
    - (4-12s): "I've switched to this 2% Salicylic Acid serum. It's a game-changer for my blemish-prone skin."
    - (12-20s): "The best part? It's completely fragrance-free, so no irritation, just results."
    - (20-25s): "Seriously, your skin will thank you. Grab yours at the link below."

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Avoid "perfect" studio lighting; ensure it looks like a smartphone recording.

    - (Shot 2/3): "I've been using this 2% Salicylic Acid serum. It really helps keep my pores clear and my skin balanced."
    - (Shot 4): "The best part? It's completely fragrance-free, so it doesn't irritate my skin."
    - (Shot 5): "Seriously, if you're blemish-prone, you need to try this. Link in bio!"

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- Audio: Natural voiceover (recorded on a phone mic for authenticity) with a low-volume, upbeat, lo-fi hip-hop background track.
- Edit: Fast cuts, jump cuts between talking head segments to maintain pace. No cinematic transitions.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Ensure the "fragrance-free" claim is highlighted as the primary purchase reason to avoid generic "clear skin" promises.
- Strictly avoid words like "cure," "permanent," "guaranteed," or "doctor-recommended."
- Ensure the talent does not look "over-glammed"; skin must look real to maintain UGC credibility.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- 9:16 aspect ratio? Yes.
- Indian woman? Yes.
- 2% Salicylic Acid serum? Yes.
- Oily/blemish-prone target? Yes.
- No medical claims/stats? Yes.
- ~25 seconds? Yes.
- One clear purchase reason? Yes (Fragrance-free/Non-irritating).
- No external websites? Yes.

## PACKAGE A

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
Cinematic video scene (40 seconds, 16:9).

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
A high-tension, dialogue-driven encounter between two characters at a café. The focus is on psychological pressure and spatial clarity, utilizing a restrained visual style to emphasize the emotional weight of a revealed secret.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"The Heavy Silence." Use a static, formal composition with a muted color palette and strategic over-the-shoulder (OTS) framing to maintain a constant sense of opposition and claustrophobia despite the public setting.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Spatial Relationship (Who is where).
2. Emotional Subtext (Tension/Betrayal).
3. The Evidence (The object of concealment).
4. The Reaction (Restraint vs. Guilt).

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Palette: Steely blues, charcoal, and warm amber accent lighting from café lamps.
- Lighting: High contrast, side-lit faces to create "chiaroscuro" shadows, suggesting duality and secrets.
- Setting: Urban café, rainy window background to isolate the characters from the world.
- Framing: Static shots, shallow depth of field to blur the background.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
- Shot Count: 6.
- Movement: Zero camera movement (static tripod).
- Editing: Slow, rhythmic cuts based on dialogue beats.
- Duration: ~40 seconds total.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
Shot 1: (5s)
Prompt: Cinematic wide shot, 16:9. Two people sitting opposite each other at a small round marble café table. Rain blurring the window in the background. Muted colors, moody lighting. Static camera. Profile view showing both characters.

Shot 2: (8s)
Prompt: Cinematic over-the-shoulder shot from behind Person B, focusing on Person A. Person A has a cold, steady expression, speaking in a low tone. Shallow depth of field. Static camera. 16:9.

Shot 3: (8s)
Prompt: Cinematic over-the-shoulder shot from behind Person A, focusing on Person B. Person B looks unsettled, avoiding eye contact, lips tight. Shallow depth of field. Static camera. 16:9.

Shot 4: (5s)
Prompt: Cinematic extreme close-up, top-down angle. A single weathered envelope and a photograph lying on a marble table between two coffee cups. Static camera. High detail on paper texture. 16:9.

Shot 5: (7s)
Prompt: Cinematic tight close-up on Person A's face. Eyes are piercing and disappointed. Minimal facial movement. Side-lighting creating deep shadows. Static camera. 16:9.

Shot 6: (7s)
Prompt: Cinematic medium wide shot from a side angle. Both characters visible in frame, staring at each other in a heavy, tense silence. The distance between them feels vast. Static camera. 16:9.

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- Aspect Ratio: Fixed 16:9.
- Frame Rate: 24fps for cinematic motion blur.
- Continuity: Constant wardrobe (Person A: Dark blazer; Person B: Grey knit sweater) and constant prop (Marble table, two coffee cups).

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- Dialogue: Low-volume, whispered delivery. Long pauses between lines.
- Sound Design: Ambient café noise (distant clinking of porcelain, muffled rain on glass) lowered in mix to prioritize dialogue.
- Music: A single, low-frequency drone or a minimal cello note holding throughout the scene.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Spatial Clarity: Using OTS shots (2 and 3) ensures the viewer never loses track of the seating arrangement.
- Tension: Prohibit any shouting or sudden movements in the prompt logic to maintain "restrained" atmosphere.
- Cinematic Quality: Explicitly banning camera movement to avoid "mechanical" panning/zooming and focusing on composition.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Two people opposite each other? Yes.
- Concealment theme? Yes.
- Tense but restrained? Yes.
- Spatial clarity maintained? Yes.
- Exactly six shots? Yes.
- No elaborate movement? Yes.
- Cinematic feel? Yes.
- Duration ~40s? Yes (5+8+8+5+7+7 = 40).
- Aspect ratio 16:9? Yes.
- No external website? Yes.

# CONTEST V16 — B01

## PACKAGE A

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
Vertical Video Ad (9:16), ~30 Seconds.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
The goal is to trigger a "this is exactly my life" realization in Indian PG/Hostel owners by visually contrasting the visceral stress of manual management (the "jhikjhik") with the streamlined efficiency of RentOK. The ad must avoid the "corporate tech" aesthetic, instead feeling like a high-quality, modern Indian commercial that understands the ground reality of property management.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"The Great Cleanup." We use a fast-paced, sensory-overload montage of the "Analog Chaos" (registers, buzzing phones, shouting tenants) that abruptly cuts to the "Digital Zen" of the RentOK app. The transition is a psychological relief for the viewer, positioning the app not just as a tool, but as a way to reclaim their time and peace of mind.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. The Pain: Operational chaos = Financial leakage & Stress (The "Jhikjhik").
2. The Pivot: RentOK as the single, clean system of record.
3. The Proof: Automatic rent collection, Digital KYC, and Complaint tracking.
4. The Outcome: Save time, work less, earn more.
5. Call to Action: Get a Free Demo / Download App.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Chaos Phase (0-12s): Warm, slightly saturated, handheld/shaky camera, tight close-ups, rapid cuts. Visual motifs: Coffee-stained registers, overlapping WhatsApp notification bubbles, cluttered desks.
- Clarity Phase (12-30s): Cool, bright, stable/smooth camera movements, wide and clean shots. Visual motifs: Clean white spaces, the RentOK App UI (smoothly animated), a relaxed property owner.
- UI Integration: App screens should not be static overlays but integrated into the scene (tracked onto phone screens) with highlighted "success" states (Green ticks, "Paid" badges).

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
- Setting: A typical Indian PG management office (small room, files, desk) and a clean, modern PG corridor.
- Talent: One primary actor (40-55 year old Indian male, appearing as a seasoned but stressed property owner).
- Editing Style: "Jump-cut" montage for the first half; "Smooth-glide" transitions for the second half.
- Sound Design: A cacophony of WhatsApp "ping" sounds, paper rustling, and distant chatter that suddenly cuts to a clean, rhythmic, contemporary Indian lo-fi beat.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
(Note: Prompts are designed for a high-fidelity video generation pipeline)

Shot 1 (0-3s): 
Prompt: Extreme close-up, vertical 9:16. A weathered, handwritten Indian account register with ink stains and scribbles. A smartphone on top of the book vibrates violently. Cinematic lighting, shallow depth of field, high detail on paper texture.

Shot 2 (3-6s): 
Prompt: Rapid montage, vertical 9:16. Fast cuts of a stressed middle-aged Indian man in a polo shirt rubbing his forehead, surrounded by stacks of folders. Overlaid with floating, semi-transparent WhatsApp chat bubbles in Hindi and English: "Rent pay kar diya?", "Water leakage in room 102", "KYC pending". Handheld shaky cam.

Shot 3 (6-9s): 
Prompt: Medium shot, vertical 9:16. The owner looking overwhelmed, holding a phone in one hand and a pen in another, looking at a messy spreadsheet on a laptop. Lighting is warm and cluttered. Authentic Indian small-office interior.

Shot 4 (9-12s): 
Prompt: Close-up, vertical 9:16. A hand frantically flipping through a notebook page to find a record. The movement is blurred and anxious.

Shot 5 (12-15s): 
Prompt: Hard cut to a clean, minimalist white background. The RentOK app logo zooms in smoothly. Sound of a "ding" or "vacuum clean" effect. Transition to a bright, airy PG office.

Shot 6 (15-20s): 
Prompt: Close-up, vertical 9:16. A smartphone screen showing the RentOK dashboard. A finger taps "Send Bulk Reminders." A satisfying animation of multiple "Payment Received" green checkmarks appearing rapidly on the screen. Smooth camera glide.

Shot 7 (20-25s): 
Prompt: Medium shot, vertical 9:16. The same Indian owner from the first half, now leaning back in a comfortable chair, smiling, looking at his phone. He looks relaxed and in control. Background is a clean, well-lit modern PG corridor.

Shot 8 (25-30s): 
Prompt: Graphic end-card, vertical 9:16. Brand colors (based on RentOK website). Text: "Save time. Work less. Earn more." Center logo: RentOK. Button: "Get Free Demo". High-end motion graphics.

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- RentOK App UI: Must be high-fidelity screen recordings or reconstructed Figma prototypes (cannot be AI-generated to ensure product accuracy).
- Brand Logo: Official RentOK vector logo.
- Text Overlays: "No more jhikjhik," "100% Automatic Collection," "Digital KYC."

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- 0-12s: Soundscape of "Stress." Layered audio: rapid-fire WhatsApp notification sounds, the sound of pages tearing/flipping, a faint humming of a ceiling fan. No music.
- 12s: A sharp "Whoosh" or "Vacuum" sound that sucks out all the noise into total silence for 0.5 seconds.
- 12-30s: A contemporary, upbeat Indian fusion beat (Tablas mixed with a modern synth bassline). Voiceover (VO) in a professional, warm, confident Indian-English accent.
- VO Script: 
(0-12s) [No VO, just chaos sounds]
(12-20s) "Stop the chaos. Stop the leakages. Stop the jhikjhik."
(20-30s) "RentOK. The smart system for Indian PG owners. Save time, work less, and earn more. Get your free demo today."

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Avoid "Corporate Blue" generic SaaS stock footage; ensure the actors and settings look authentically Indian (not "Globalized").
- Ensure the transition at 12s is an absolute contrast; if the second half isn't "clean" enough, the emotional payoff of the product fails.
- UI must be legible on a small mobile screen.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Vertical 9:16? Yes.
- Target ~30s? Yes.
- Indian context (Hostels/PGs)? Yes.
- Chaos $\rightarrow$ System narrative? Yes.
- Used only rentok.com info? Yes (Included "jhikjhik" and "Save time. Work less. Earn more" from site).
- No generic SV SaaS feel? Yes (Handled via sound design and authentic setting).

DELIVERABLE

The ad must create a visceral "recognition of pain" for Indian PG/Hostel owners who are currently trapped in manual operational chaos (WhatsApp threads, physical registers, spreadsheets). It transitions from this high-stress, "leaky" environment to the streamlined, professional efficiency of RentOK. The tone is "Modern Indian Business"—credible and efficient, avoiding generic SaaS tropes in favor of authentic local operational contexts.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"The Great Clear-Out."
We visually and auditorily contrast the "Noise" of manual management (clutter, pings, paper, stress) with the "Silence" and clarity of RentOK. The transition is a physical and mental "reset" for the property owner.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. The Pain: Operational chaos leads to financial leakages and stress (The "1990s" way).
2. The Pivot: RentOK as the definitive "Clean System."

    - Chaos: Overlapping WhatsApp pings, loud paper rustling, background noise of tenants arguing/talking, fast-paced percussive tension music.
    - Order: A singular, satisfying "Ding" (payment received), ambient silence, smooth lo-fi or corporate-modern rhythmic beat.

Since this is a video production, prompts are structured as shot-level descriptions for a production team or high-end generative video tool.

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS

Vertical Video Ad (9:16), 30 seconds.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
Convert Indian PG/Hostel owners by visualizing the mental and operational tax of "analog" management (notebooks, WhatsApp, spreadsheets) and positioning RentOK as the professional upgrade that eliminates financial leakages and administrative stress.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"The Noise vs. The System." 
The first half of the ad is a sensory overload of "operational noise"—overlapping sounds and frantic visuals of fragmented tools—which abruptly cuts to the "silence" and clarity of the RentOK interface.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. The Pain: Fragmented data (Notebooks $\rightarrow$ WhatsApp $\rightarrow$ Excel) = Chaos and Money Loss.
2. The Pivot: There is a better way to manage.
3. The Solution: RentOK—the all-in-one system for Rent, KYC, and Complaints.
4. The Result: Credibility, peace of mind, and zero leakages.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Phase 1 (Chaos): High-energy, fast cuts, handheld camera movement. Warm, slightly cluttered color grade. Close-ups of tactile "chaos" (ink-stained registers, glowing phone screens in the dark).
- Phase 2 (System): Stable, smooth glides/static shots. Clean, bright, airy color grade (whites and RentOK brand colors). High-resolution UI overlays.
- Setting: Authentic Indian urban PG environments (small office desks with calendars, corridors with typical PG signage).

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
- Shot style: Mixed media (Live action + High-fidelity UI motion graphics).
- Pacing: 0-15s (Frantic, 0.5-1s cuts) $\rightarrow$ 15-30s (Calm, 3-5s cuts).
- Talent: Indian male/female (35-50), dressed as a professional property owner.

GENERATION_PROMPTS

Shot 1 (0-3s)
- Purpose: Establish the "Analog Chaos."
- Prompt: Extreme close-up, handheld shaky cam. An old, worn-out Indian accounting register (bahi-khata) with handwritten entries in blue and red ink, smudged pages, coffee stains. Cinematic lighting, shallow depth of field, 8k, hyper-realistic.
- Duration: 3s

Shot 2 (3-7s)
- Purpose: Establish "Digital Chaos."
- Prompt: POV shot of a smartphone screen. Rapid scrolling through a WhatsApp chat list. Dozens of unread messages from tenants: "Rent sent?", "Water problem in Room 202", "KYC documents?". Notifications popping up incessantly. Indian urban background blurred. 8k, realistic screen glow.
- Duration: 4s

Shot 3 (7-11s)
- Purpose: Establish "Administrative Leakage."
- Prompt: Mid-shot of a stressed Indian property owner in a small office. He is staring at a laptop screen showing a complex, messy Excel spreadsheet with highlighted red cells. He is rubbing his temples in frustration. Background has a wall calendar and a stack of folders. Realistic Indian office lighting.
- Duration: 4s

Shot 4 (11-15s)
- Purpose: The breaking point.
- Prompt: Montage of fast cuts: A pen running out of ink, a phone vibrating on a wooden table, a hand flipping through a folder frantically. Ends with a close-up of the owner looking directly into the camera, exhausted.
- Duration: 4s

Shot 5 (15-22s)
- Purpose: The Solution (The Pivot).
- Prompt: Clean, minimalist transition to a bright, white background. A high-resolution smartphone floats in center frame displaying the RentOK App Dashboard. The UI is clean, showing "Rent Collected: 95%", "Pending KYC: 2". Smooth 3D motion, studio lighting, brand colors.
- Duration: 7s

Shot 6 (22-27s)
- Purpose: The Result (Control).
- Prompt: Medium shot of the same owner, now looking relaxed and confident. He is leaning back in his chair, holding the phone, and simply tapping a button. The background is now a clean, well-lit, modern PG lobby. Warm, professional sunlight.
- Duration: 5s

Shot 7 (27-30s)
- Purpose: Branding/CTA.
- Prompt: Motion graphics background in RentOK brand colors. Center text: "RentOK: The Clean System for PG & Hostel Management." Subtext: "Stop the leakages. Start the growth." Official RentOK logo animation.
- Duration: 3s

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- Actual RentOK App UI screenshots/recordings (MUST be used instead of generated UI for credibility).
- RentOK Official Logo.
- Text overlays: "No more Notebooks," "No more WhatsApp Chaos," "One System. Total Control."

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- 0-15s: Sound design is a "cacophony." Overlapping WhatsApp notification pings, the sound of paper ripping/flipping, a ticking clock, ambient city traffic noise. Fast, rhythmic editing.
- 15s: A sudden "Whoosh" or "Vacuum" sound that sucks out all noise into total silence for 0.5 seconds.
- 15-30s: Transition to a modern, upbeat, corporate-lofi track with a steady, confident beat. Clear, professional voiceover (Indian English accent, warm and authoritative).

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Avoid "Stock Photo" look: Ensure the office and PG environments look authentically Indian (e.g., specific types of folders, wall calendars, furniture styles).
- Avoid "Generic SaaS" aesthetic: Do not use floating 3D abstract shapes; keep the focus on the actual utility and the owner's emotion.
- UI Accuracy: Ensure the RentOK app interface is legible and accurate to the website's functionality.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Vertical 9:16? Yes.
- Indian Hostel/PG focus? Yes.
- Pain points (KYC, Rent, Chaos) addressed? Yes.
- No SV SaaS genericism? Yes.
- Duration ~30s? Yes.
- Website used for product context? Yes.

## JUDGE V16
Apply the Stage-1 rubric. Choose exactly one A/B/C winner.

## PACKAGE A

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
9:16 Vertical UGC-style Video (approx. 25 seconds).

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
Create a high-conversion, low-fidelity performance ad that leverages the "trusted peer" aesthetic. The goal is to position the 2% Salicylic Acid serum as a practical, irritant-free solution for oily skin, avoiding "corporate" polish to increase authenticity and trust.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"The Honest Routine": A relatable Indian creator sharing her specific solution for midday oil and congestion, highlighting the fragrance-free aspect as a key differentiator for sensitive, blemish-prone skin.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Hook: The frustration of oily/congested skin.
2. Solution: Introduction of the 2% Salicylic Acid Serum.
3. Primary Purchase Reason: Effective pore-clearing without the irritation of added fragrances.
4. Proof: Visual demonstration of texture and absorption.
5. Action: Clear CTA to purchase.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Talent: Indian woman (25-35), natural skin (minimal makeup), relatable appearance.
- Environment: Well-lit bathroom or bedroom with natural window light.
- Camera: Handheld smartphone aesthetic (slight organic movement).
- Palette: Natural skin tones, white/neutral backgrounds, product packaging colors.
- Text Overlays: Simple, sans-serif "TikTok/Reel" style captions for key points.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
- Framing: Medium close-up (MCU) and Close-up (CU).
- Lighting: Soft, natural daylight.
- Audio: Direct-to-camera sync sound (lavalier or phone mic), minimal ambient background noise.
- Edit: Fast-paced cuts on the beat, organic transitions.

GENERATION_PROMPTS

Shot 1 (0-4s)
- Purpose: Hook.
- Prompt: 9:16 handheld smartphone footage. A relatable Indian woman in her late 20s, looking into the camera in a bright bathroom. She is pointing to her T-zone, looking slightly frustrated but friendly. Natural skin texture, no heavy makeup. High quality, UGC style.
- Dialogue: "If your skin feels like an oil slick by 2 PM, you know the struggle."

Shot 2 (4-9s)
- Purpose: Product Intro.
- Prompt: 9:16 close-up. The woman holds up a minimalist skincare bottle (2% Salicylic Acid Serum). The label is clear and legible. She smiles at the camera. Natural lighting, blurred bathroom background.
- Dialogue: "I've been using this fragrance-free 2% Salicylic Acid serum, and it's a game changer."

Shot 3 (9-18s)
- Purpose: Demo & Purchase Reason.
- Prompt: 9:16 extreme close-up. A few clear drops of serum being applied to a cheek and blended in with fingers. The serum is lightweight and absorbs quickly without leaving a greasy residue. Natural skin pores visible.
- Dialogue: "Because it's fragrance-free, it actually clears my pores without that stinging or irritation I usually get from other actives."

Shot 4 (18-25s)
- Purpose: Result & CTA.
- Prompt: 9:16 medium close-up. The woman looks refreshed, skin looking matte but hydrated. She gives a thumbs up or a confident nod to the camera.
- Dialogue: "Just simple, effective ingredients. Grab yours at the link below."

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- On-screen text overlays:
    - 0-4s: "Oily skin struggles? 😫"
    - 9-18s: "Fragrance-Free = No Irritation ✨"
    - 18-25s: "Shop Now 🛍️"
- Brand Logo: Small, unobtrusive placement in the top corner during Shot 2 and 4.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- Audio: Clear, conversational voiceover.
- Background Music: Low-volume, upbeat, "lo-fi" beat typical of skincare GRWM (Get Ready With Me) videos.
- Pace: Snappy cuts; remove all "ums" and long pauses.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Ensure the "fragrance-free" claim is the central pivot to avoid making generic medical claims about acne cure.
- Avoid studio lighting; if the video looks too "commercial," it will lose UGC credibility.
- Ensure the talent does not use words like "guarantee," "permanent," or "clinical" to remain compliant with the "no invented statistics" constraint.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- 9:16 format? Yes.
- Performance UGC style? Yes.
- Indian woman talent? Yes.
- 2% Salicylic Acid / Fragrance-free? Yes.
- No medical claims/stats? Yes.
- Target ~25 seconds? Yes.
- Direct to camera/demonstration? Yes.

Shot 2 (4-8s): [Visual: Mid-shot. She holds up a minimalist clear bottle of serum. The label is clean and legible: '2% Salicylic Acid Serum - Fragrance Free']. [Audio: "I finally switched to this 2% salicylic acid serum and it's been a game changer."]

# CONTEST V18 — B05

Use frozen brief B05 from the top of the final file.

## PACKAGE A

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
40-second cinematic scene (6 shots, 16:9).

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
A high-tension, low-volume confrontation between two characters at a café. The visual priority is spatial clarity (maintaining the 180-degree rule) and emotional restraint, using static framing to amplify the psychological pressure.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"The Weight of Silence." Instead of using camera movement to create energy, the scene uses stillness and a "ping-pong" editing rhythm to create a claustrophobic feeling of entrapment, despite the open café setting.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Spatial Orientation: Establish the opposite positions immediately.
2. Power Shift: Transition from the "accuser" (Person A) to the "accused" (Person B).
3. The Catalyst: The physical evidence of the concealment.
4. The Result: The emotional impasse.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Color Palette: Muted teals and warm ambers; high contrast but desaturated.
- Lighting: Soft side-lighting from a window, creating partial shadows on faces to suggest duality/secrets.
- Composition: Tight framing with shallow depth of field to isolate the characters from the café background.
- Camera: Static shots. No pans, tilts, or zooms.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
- Shot 1: Establishing Medium Wide (Profile) - 7s
- Shot 2: Medium Close-Up (Person A) - 7s
- Shot 3: Medium Close-Up (Person B) - 7s
- Shot 4: Close-Up (Table/Object) - 7s
- Shot 5: Tight Close-Up (Person A) - 6s
- Shot 6: Medium Wide (Profile) - 6s

GENERATION_PROMPTS
- Shot 1: Cinematic medium wide shot, 16:9. Profile view of a man and a woman sitting opposite each other at a small round marble café table. Soft window light from the side. Muted color grade, shallow depth of field. Static camera.
- Shot 2: Cinematic medium close-up, 16:9. Person A's face. Expression is cold, steady, and expectant. Soft side-lighting, blurred café background. High detail on eyes. Static camera.
- Shot 3: Cinematic medium close-up, 16:9. Person B's face. Expression of suppressed panic, eyes darting slightly, jaw tight. Soft side-lighting, blurred café background. Static camera.
- Shot 4: Cinematic close-up, 16:9. Top-down angle focusing on the marble table surface. A hand slowly slides a folded piece of paper across the table toward the other person. Static camera.
- Shot 5: Cinematic tight close-up, 16:9. Extreme close-up on Person A's eyes. Unblinking, intense, focused. Soft dramatic lighting. Static camera.
- Shot 6: Cinematic medium wide shot, 16:9. Profile view of both characters. They remain frozen in silence, the distance between them feeling vast. Ambient bokeh of a café in the background. Static camera.

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- Aspect Ratio: 16:9 (Hard constraint).
- Shot Count: Exactly 6 (Hard constraint).
- Camera Movement: Zero (Hard constraint).

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- Audio: Ambient café noise (clinking cups, distant chatter) lowered in volume. Dialogue is whispered/low-toned. Heavy emphasis on the sound of the paper sliding across the marble (Shot 4).
- Edit: Hard cuts. No transitions. Rhythmic timing following the production recipe.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Spatial Logic: To ensure the viewer always understands positioning, Shot 1 and Shot 6 serve as "anchor" profiles. Shots 2 and 3 must strictly adhere to the 180-degree axis (Person A always looking right, Person B always looking left).
- Tone Control: Prompts specify "cold," "suppressed," and "frozen" to prevent the AI from generating exaggerated emotional expressions (shouting/crying).

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Two people opposite each other? Yes.
- Concealment theme? Yes.
- Tense/Restrained/No shouting? Yes.
- Spatial clarity? Yes (180-degree rule applied).
- Exactly six shots? Yes.
- No elaborate movement? Yes (Static).
- Cinematic feel? Yes (Lighting/Composition focus).
- Duration ~40s? Yes (7+7+7+7+6+6 = 40).
- Aspect Ratio 16:9? Yes.
- No external website? Yes.

DELIVERABLE

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
A high-tension, low-volume confrontation between two characters at a café. The visual priority is spatial orientation (maintaining the 180-degree rule) and emotional restraint, conveyed through framing and timing rather than camera movement.

"The Quiet Fracture." The scene uses a rigid, static formal structure to mirror the emotional paralysis and restraint of the characters. The tension is built through the contrast between the mundane environment (café) and the gravity of the revelation.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Spatial Relation (Where they are)
2. Emotional Subtext (Tension/Betrayal)
3. Narrative Reveal (The concealed item)
4. Environment (Café atmosphere)

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Aspect Ratio: 16:9
- Lighting: Soft, directional window light creating subtle shadows on faces.
- Palette: Muted tones, desaturated teals and warm woods.

- Composition: Strict adherence to the 180-degree axis.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
- Setting: A quiet corner of a modern café, small square table.
- Characters: Person A (Accuser), Person B (Secret Keeper).
- Pacing: Slow cuts, allowing silence to occupy the space between lines.

GENERATION_PROMPTS

Shot 1: Establishing Wide Shot (6s)
Prompt: Cinematic wide shot, profile view of two people sitting opposite each other at a small square wooden table in a quiet, minimalist urban café. Soft morning window light. 16:9, static camera, shallow depth of field. The atmosphere is heavy and still.

Shot 2: Medium Shot - Person A (8s)
Prompt: Cinematic medium shot of Person A. Steady, piercing gaze, expression of controlled disappointment. Soft side-lighting. Background is blurred café interior. 16:9, static camera. Person A speaks calmly, lips barely moving.

Shot 3: Medium Shot - Person B (8s)
Prompt: Cinematic medium shot of Person B, facing Person A. A momentary freeze, eyes darting slightly before settling on Person A. Expression of guilt and restraint. 16:9, static camera. Soft window light.

Shot 4: Close-up Detail (6s)
Prompt: Cinematic extreme close-up of a hand on the café table. The hand slowly tightens around a white ceramic coffee cup, knuckles whitening. Beside the cup lies a folded piece of paper (the evidence). 16:9, static camera, macro lens, shallow depth of field.

Shot 5: Over-the-Shoulder (OTS) - A to B (6s)
Prompt: Cinematic over-the-shoulder shot from behind Person A's shoulder, looking at Person B. Person B is framed by the shoulder of A, creating a sense of confrontation. Person B opens their mouth to speak but hesitates. 16:9, static camera.

Shot 6: Over-the-Shoulder (OTS) - B to A (6s)
Prompt: Cinematic over-the-shoulder shot from behind Person B's shoulder, looking at Person A. Person A remains motionless, staring with a cold, knowing expression. 16:9, static camera. Fade to black.

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- To prevent spatial confusion: Every shot must maintain the left-to-right relationship (Person A always on the left side of the frame or the foreground shoulder on the left; Person B always on the right or the foreground shoulder on the right).
- To prevent "mechanical" feel: Ensure lighting is cinematic (chiaroscuro effect) rather than flat, and focus on the micro-expressions of the actors.

- Concealment plot? Yes.
- Tense/restrained/no shouting? Yes.

- No elaborate movement? Yes (All static).
- Cinematic feel? Yes (Lighting/Depth of field).
- Duration ~40s? Yes (6+8+8+6+6+6 = 40).
- Aspect ratio 16:9? Yes.
- No external websites? Yes.

- Duration: 6s
- Purpose: Establish spatial relationship and environment.

Shot 2: Over-the-Shoulder (OTS) from Person A to Person B.
- Prompt: Cinematic OTS shot, 16:9. Focus on Person B's face. Person B looks anxious, blinking slowly, eyes darting. Person A's shoulder and back of head are in the foreground, softly blurred. Soft bokeh café background. Static camera. High emotional tension.
- Duration: 7s
- Purpose: Establish the target of the discovery and their vulnerability.

Shot 3: Over-the-Shoulder (OTS) from Person B to Person A.
- Prompt: Cinematic OTS shot, 16:9. Focus on Person A's face. Person A has a cold, steady expression, staring intensely. Person B's shoulder is in the foreground, blurred. Lighting emphasizes the hardness of Person A's gaze. Static camera.
- Duration: 8s
- Purpose: Establish the power dynamic and the delivery of the revelation.

Shot 4: Tight Close-Up (CU) of Person B.
- Prompt: Extreme close-up, 16:9. Person B's face. A slight swallow, a twitch of the lip, eyes filled with trapped panic. Very shallow depth of field. Soft side-lighting. Static camera. 
- Duration: 6s
- Purpose: Internalize the panic of the concealer.

Shot 5: Tight Close-Up (CU) of Person A.
- Prompt: Extreme close-up, 16:9. Person A's face. Deadpan expression, blinking once slowly. The look of profound disappointment. Soft side-lighting. Static camera.
- Duration: 6s
- Purpose: Internalize the resolve of the discoverer.

Shot 6: Medium Profile Shot (Two-Shot).
- Prompt: Cinematic medium shot, 16:9. Side profile of both characters sitting opposite each other. The table is between them, creating a physical barrier. They do not look at each other now; they both look straight ahead. Wide gap of negative space between them. Static camera. Fade to black.
- Duration: 7s
- Purpose: Resolve the scene by visualizing the emotional divide.

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- Dialogue (to be added in post/audio): 
  - Person A: "I found the documents." (Shot 3)
  - Person B: "I can explain." (Shot 4)
  - Person A: "Don't." (Shot 5)

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
- Audio: Ambient café noise (distant clinking of cups, low murmur) reduced to a low hum during close-ups. No music; only the sound of breathing and the spoken dialogue.
- Edit: Hard cuts. No transitions except for the final fade to black.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- To avoid "mechanical coverage," ensure the close-ups (Shots 4 and 5) are tighter than standard coverage, focusing on the eyes to create an intimate, claustrophobic feeling.
- To ensure spatial clarity, the 180-degree axis must be strictly maintained between Shot 1, 2, 3, and 6.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Two people opposite at café: Yes.
- Concealed secret plot: Yes.
- Tense/Restrained/No shouting: Yes.
- Spatial relationship clear: Yes (Wide -> OTS -> Profile).
- Exactly six shots: Yes.
- No elaborate movement: Yes (All static).
- Cinematic feel: Yes (Shallow DOF, specific lighting, tight framing).
- Duration ~40s: Yes (6+7+8+6+6+7 = 40s).
- Aspect Ratio 16:9: Yes.
- No external websites: Yes.

## JUDGE V18
Apply the Stage-1 rubric. Choose exactly one A/B/C winner.
